# astream

A small, reproducibly-buildable message bus for agent fleets, built around
deterministic record and replay.

astream records terminal and agent sessions into single-writer, offset-addressed
logs and serves them as a durable pub/sub bus. Any session can be replayed
byte for byte, resumed from an offset, or forked at an offset to explore an
alternate history.

- **Compatibility:** APIs and on-disk formats may change.
- **License:** [Apache-2.0](LICENSE)

## Features

- **Durable bus.** Exactly-once pub/sub with subject/filter routing,
  resume-from-offset, durable consumer groups, group commit, credit-windowed
  pipelining, and epoch-fenced watermark replication, served over a Unix socket
  or TCP. The durability level is configurable (`Strict`, `Ordered`, `Relaxed`,
  `Replicated`); on `Ordered` an ack costs an ordering barrier, and the records an
  escalation table names are flushed in full before anyone sees them.
  Every log names itself (an id and a generation, in the answer to `Hello`), so a
  client that keeps offsets can tell when they stopped naming the records it read.
  `BrokerLog` is an in-memory-indexed append-only log (`Vec<Arc<BrokerRecord>>`
  with zero duplicate raw-byte buffering, bounded by RAM between compactions).
  Retention is by rules (`Broker::set_retention`, `asb serve --retain-file`): the
  broker's own tick cuts the bulk stream by age and bytes off the ack path and
  carries what the hold rules keep (unread mail, last values) in `<log>.held`, so
  RAM stays within the rules' bytes; a fetch below what retention kept is told
  `retained-from=<off>` (or, where it asked for gap notices, reads what is still
  held there, the gap told ahead of the page), and Hello advertises the rules.
- **Deterministic replay.** A pure, I/O-free VT screen fold. Sessions recorded
  through an effect seam replay byte-identically, and recovery handles torn or
  corrupt log tails (including zeroed preallocated extents).
- **Fork and consistent cut.** Counterfactual forks of a recorded session, and
  Chandy-Lamport consistent causal cuts (`CausalCut<K>`) across a fleet of sessions.
- **Agent wake-ups.** A pump that classifies terminal output into prompt-ready,
  command-start, command-end, and quiescence events, so a turn-based agent wakes
  only when there is something to act on.
- **Opt-in security.** XChaCha20-Poly1305 transport sealing (pre-shared key,
  X25519 handshake, or mutual Ed25519 identity); HMAC-SHA256 capabilities with
  expiry, offline bearer-attenuated Macaroon delegation chains (`via=<hex>`),
  Ed25519 principal binding (`n-<16 hex>`), and live identity/principal revocation;
  and encryption at rest, anti-rollback, and state-carrying compaction (`<log>.state`)
  for the durable log.
- **Embedded TLA+ & Clean-Origin (`I5`) verification.** All 131 of the 131
  workspace crates and all 8 of their binaries embed 812 Clean CIC / TLA+
  protocol islands that compile top-level Clean-Origin (`I5`) transition
  functions directly into Rust `pub const fn`s (the multi-parameter kernels
  matched to them are hand-written Rust: four call them in the composition
  their islands state, and five restate that composition in plain Rust, held
  to it by tests over a grid of boundary operands), discharge `#[ensures]`
  guards via `clean-kernel-defeq`, and prove unbounded
  TLA+ temporal safety invariants
  (`Spec ⊨ □ (Lift Inv)`), alongside 11,887 proved Level-0 obligations
  (`0 failed, 0 runtime-checked`) across all 137 workspace targets (21 in
  astream's own 15 crates, 116 in the standalone models under Crates) gated by
  `scripts/verify-ready.sh` (measured on e467a1d with Trust 321aaeda7).
- **Small dependency footprint.** The core crates use only `std`. SHA-256, and
  the one HMAC-SHA256 and HKDF-SHA256 over it, are the in-tree `astream-digest`
  (FIPS 180-4, RFC 2104 and RFC 5869, zero dependencies), so the HMAC
  capabilities in `astream-cap` take no third-party crate either. Third-party
  cryptography is isolated in `astream-aead`: ring's ChaCha20-Poly1305 under an
  in-crate HChaCha20 subkey (XChaCha20-Poly1305, byte-identical to the RustCrypto
  wire it replaced), plus `x25519-dalek` and `ed25519-dalek` for the off-by-default
  handshake and identity features. Keys this crate holds are wiped (`zeroize`) on
  drop; ring's own copy of a message key is overwritten with a zero key, and a copy
  a move left on the stack is out of reach (astream-aead's "Keys in memory"). `unsafe`
  is forbidden everywhere except two cordoned modules in `astream-host` and two in
  `astream-sys`.

The fresh-publication design (`docs/DESIGN-fresh-publication.md`, kept in the
development repository) explains the opt-in conditional publish API, writer-side refusal fence and caller replay
obligations for bounded source-ordered bursts.

It ships two CLIs: `asb` (the broker) and `aspump` (the pump). Both accept
a lone `--help` or `--version`, print to stdout, and exit successfully without
opening a broker or reading configuration. These informational flags must stand
alone; mixed or invalid arguments remain usage errors (exit 2).

## Crates

astream's own 15 crates: the bus and the record-and-replay libraries it is
built from, its CLIs, its Kafka codec and its evidence harness. aterm or
another of these crates depends on eleven of them (`wire`, `digest`, `sys`,
`broker`, `cap`, `effects`, `term`, `aead`, `engine`, `host` and `agent`),
`astream-pump`, `astream-live` and `astream-evidence` run as CLIs, and nothing
calls `astream-kafka` yet. They hold 21 of `scripts/verify-ready.sh`'s 137
targets.

| Crate | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `astream-wire` | Frame codec, step records, subject/filter grammar, partitioning, Chandy-Lamport `CausalCut<K>`, the receipt view `V1Receipt` (and the header-first `FabricReceipt` no writer emits), mergeable window summaries (`summary`: per-window histograms, counters and gauges a replica merges exactly). Zero dependencies. |
| `astream-digest` | SHA-256 (FIPS 180-4). Zero dependencies. |
| `astream-term` | Pure VT screen fold, speculative echo, perception queries, semantic events. |
| `astream-engine` | Single-writer log: replay, durability, resume, fork, consistent cut, durable workflow journal. |
| `astream-effects` | Effect seam for recording and replaying clock, RNG and file reads, reply exchanges, outputs and raised flags. |
| `astream-agent` | Agent turn log with hermetic replay and forking. |
| `astream-host` | Live PTY host. |
| `astream-live` | Live-capture bridge (`capture` binary). |
| `astream-broker` | The servable bus and the `asb` CLI. |
| `astream-pump` | Event pump and the `aspump` CLI. |
| `astream-cap` | HMAC-SHA256 capability mint, offline Macaroon attenuation, and expiry/revocation predicates. |
| `astream-aead` | AEAD transport, X25519 handshake, and Ed25519 identity. |
| `astream-kafka` | Kafka wire-protocol codec (codec only; not wired into the broker yet). |
| `astream-evidence` | Evidence harness: claim runner, README generator, merge gate. |
| `astream-sys` | The durable log's OS cordon: `F_BARRIERFSYNC` (`barrier_sync`), the boot session id (`boot_session`), sockets kept out of children (`close_on_fork`), and the owner-only file rule (`open_nonblocking`, `owner_private`, `owner_private_dir`, `NotPrivate`; on macOS arm64 `owner_private_dir_by_path`, `DirByPath`, `PathUndecided`, which judge a directory from one `lstatx_np` of its path through the same clauses; `kind_and_owner_of`). Zero dependencies. |

### Standalone models

The 116 crates below remain of the 119 that came with agy's verification cohort
(astream `03495e4`, `453ba69` and `876b115`, 2026-10-28/29). Each is an independent model with its
own Clean TLA+ islands and tests: no astream runtime crate and no aterm code
depends on any of them, and aterm runs none. They hold the other 116 of
`scripts/verify-ready.sh`'s 137 targets. A row naming a mechanism does not
make it shipped: some crates' claims in the Evidence table name the shipped
code, in the crates above or in aterm, or the aterm face that was cut; a claim
that names neither cites no shipped counterpart, and several models describe
mechanisms nothing ships (`astream-ext-authority` and `astream-seccomp-replay`
are open items of `docs/ROADMAP.md`).

| Crate | Models |
|---|---|
| `astream-vt-ext` | Modern VT & `aterm` protocol extensions: soft-wrap grid reflow, OSC 8, Kitty keyboard, OSC 133 shell marks, OSC 52 clipboard guard, `NEEDS YOU:` directives, and paste sanitization. |
| `astream-session-ops` | Session operational primitives: fuel-metered Wasm sandbox, workspace file-sync deltas, `seccomp-bpf` replay filters, bus-record causal cuts, flight-recorder rings, fleet `await` barriers, and `sys` disk-room budgets. |
| `astream-fabric` | Held-store (`<log>.held`) follower snapshot replication, loop-free federation mesh, shared fan-out page cache, cold-prefix RAM eviction, delta compression, and adaptive backoff. |
| `astream-fabric-ops` | Prefix-indexed `Last` planner, `survey` presence leases, multi-session barrier router, binary body chunking, `cruise` cadence state machine, and RFC 6962 Merkle proofs. |
| `astream-cap-ext` | Offline Macaroon sub-agent delegation, capability provenance, TOFU pinning, HChaCha20 symmetric rekey ratchet, agent receipt adapters, quota pools, and restart handoffs. |

| `astream-self-restart` | `@self restart` handoff gate, `pid=` vs `supervisor=` validator, resume argv pin, storm breaker, `NEEDS YOU` classifier, and task drain. |
| `astream-cross-cells` | 5-target cross-cell matrix, `ring` `links` pin, `-D warnings` zero-finding gate, `^trust:` exclusion, Windows scoping, and `0/1/2` verdict triad. |
| `astream-ordered-barrier` | **Model only.** `Ordered` boot-volume `fstat`, barrier-refusal `Strict` latch, escalated head hold, failed-flush `Lossy` poison, horizon coalescer, and `/a/bind` matcher. The shipped Ordered tier is `astream-broker`'s `store.rs` and `escalation.rs`. |
| `astream-speculative-echo` | 0-RTT cooked predictor, `ProgramPredictor` rule learner, `ECHOCTL` caret expander, `>= 0.80` squash gate, tentative spans, and rollback. |
| `astream-held-compactor` | **Model only.** `<log>.held` `SLACK_DIV = 4` planner, `2x` churn trigger, `0x87` gap ledger, durable age stamps, person-vs-node mail hold, and `.compact` FSM. The shipped held store is `astream-broker`'s `held.rs`, `retention.rs` and `compact.rs`. |
| `astream-uds-owner` | **Model only**, over plain integers. ACL-before-mode `owner_private` order, `O_NONBLOCK` FIFO guard, `O_NOFOLLOW` symlink gate, `euid` check, `0600`/`0700` mask, and `ENOTSUP` flush. The shipped check is `astream-sys`'s `owner_private`. |
| `astream-ext-authority` | SSH-agent cert chains, OIDC JWT claims window, overlapping host-key rollover, allowlist reload, cert revocation, and principal key rotation. |
| `astream-lease-failover` | Replica leader lease TTL, quorum witness ballots, epoch-fenced promotion, zombie write fence, divergent-tail truncation, and catch-up lag gate. |
| `astream-seccomp-replay` | `SECCOMP_RET_TRACE` 5-syscall pre-filter, no-op syscall neutralization, taped return injector, `execve` lineage, tape abort latch, and SHA-256 oracle parity. |
| `astream-subject-retire` | **Model only.** `max_subjects_per_producer` quota, `last_owner` charge auth, live-will retirement guard, `COMMIT_TAG_RETIRE` marker, `PendingRetire` head overlay, and cut refusal. The shipped subject budget and `Retire` are `astream-broker`'s `store.rs`. |

## Building

astream builds with the Trust toolchain (`targo`), a `rustc` fork with an
integrated SMT verifier and Clean CIC kernel, and pins it in `rust-toolchain.toml`.
The lockfile is committed, and git dependencies are not allowed.

```bash
make ci                   # fmt, build, stock, tests (default and all features), gate, lints, docs, claims
make stock                # STOCK EXCEPTION: a stock rustc still compiles every crate, warning-free (scripts/stock-check.sh)
make test                 # tests only
make gate                 # merge-gate lints
make run                  # re-run every claim in evidence/manifest.toml
./scripts/verify-ready.sh # run Trust L0 + Clean CIC / TLA+ verification across all 137 workspace targets: 21 own, 116 standalone models (0 failed)
```

There is no hosted CI. Contributors run `make ci` locally before pushing.

A stock `rustc` still compiles every crate (aterm, which embeds astream, builds
its cross-platform cells with one), so Trust-only syntax stays where a stock
compiler never reads it. Each Clean island lives in its own `*_island.rs` file,
mounted under `cfg(clean_islands)`, which a crate's `build.rs` sets only when the
compiler reports that it is Trust. A contracted function goes through
`contract_fn!`, which drops the `ensures` clause for any other compiler. The
Clean-Origin functions have plain-Rust twins in each crate's `twins.rs`, and
tests hold the twins to the answers the islands give. The merge gate's
`stock-lexable` lint keeps the rule, and `make stock` (in `make ci`, a STOCK
EXCEPTION) compiles the workspace with rustup's stable for the host and
x86_64/aarch64 Linux, x86_64 macOS and Windows, warnings denied: code only an
island calls is dead to a stock compiler, which the lint cannot see, and aterm's
cells refuse its `dead_code` warning as they refuse an error.

## Evidence

Every capability claim lives in [`evidence/manifest.toml`](evidence/manifest.toml),
paired with a command anyone can re-run. `astream-evidence render` generates the
table below from that manifest. The merge gate rejects hand edits to the table,
claims backed by no-op commands, git dependencies, references to undeclared
cfg features, and constant-only tests.

<!-- ASTREAM:EVIDENCE:BEGIN -->
<!-- generated by `astream-evidence render --write`; do not hand-edit -->

Every claim below is backed by a command anyone can re-run. Project: `astream`.

| Claim ID | Statement | Verified by |
|---|---|---|
| `build.reproducible` | The workspace builds on the Trust toolchain with the committed lockfile: `targo --unverified build --locked` fails if Cargo.lock would need any change, so every dependency resolves exactly as pinned. It runs in the working tree (not a fresh clone) and does not itself check for git dependencies -- those are rejected by the gate's no-git-deps lint (gate.rejects-known-failures), which scans every dependency table, [patch]/[replace] redirects, and Cargo.lock's resolved sources. | `targo --unverified build --locked --workspace` |
| `wire.frame.panic-safe` | The wire frame round-trips and rejects truncated, oversized, and corrupt input without panicking -- an example-based unit test of Frame::encode/decode; this command asserts nothing beyond it. The Trust-toolchain compiler-verification survey of this crate (evidence/verify/trust-wire.md, re-captured by scripts/verify-trust.sh) is a separate attached artifact: it is not run by make ci and is not asserted by this claim. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-wire --lib frame` |
| `wire.address-grammar.validated` | Subject and Filter validate in their constructors and reject partial-segment and misplaced wildcards. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-wire --lib subject` |
| `wire.router.matches-independent-oracle` | The Filter matcher agrees with an independently coded recursive oracle over generated well-formed inputs: filters of 1..=8 segments (literals from a prefix-colliding 5-token alphabet or `*`, optional trailing `>`) against subjects of 1..=10 literal segments, and every accepted filter matches a subject instantiated from it. Over structured malformed inputs (0..=8 segments of literal, `*`, `>`, `fo*`, empty, or control byte, with or without the leading slash) the Filter/Subject validators return the exact spec-prescribed error variant, and an exhaustive enumeration up to 3 segments reaches every FilterError and SubjectError variant. A no-panic sweep over arbitrary 0..=40-char strings is a crash check only. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-wire --test suite -- router_differential::` |
| `wire.filter.containment` | Filter::contains is sound: A.contains(B) implies every subject matching B also matches A (no false positives), checked over a generated subject space against Filter::matches, plus known cases and reflexivity. As the subscribe-ACL scoping primitive (a bearer granted A may subscribe with any narrower B), soundness without completeness means the ACL can only be too strict, never too permissive. `>` contains any equal-or-longer non-empty tail; `*` contains a literal or `*` but never `>`; a literal contains only itself. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-wire --test suite -- filter_containment::` |
| `wire.partition.deterministic-and-in-bounds` | assign_partition is deterministic and always within range, including the zero-partition guard. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-wire --lib partition` |
| `wire.hash.crc32-table-equiv` | crc32_ieee, the payload-integrity hash run on every frame encode and decode, is table-driven (slicing-by-8 over eight compile-time const 256-entry tables, no runtime initialisation) yet byte-identical to the retained bitwise reference: the canonical CRC-32/IEEE vector (123456789 -> 0xCBF43926), a differential test over every input length 0..=600 (seeded pseudo-random bytes covering all byte values), fixed edge vectors, every table row k entry checked against the register after byte n followed by k zero bytes from register 0 and 8 steps of Clean-origin crc32_poly_select under CRC32_IEEE_POLY = 0xEDB8_8320, and split-invariance of both crc32_ieee_update(crc32_ieee(p), d) == crc32_ieee(p ++ d) and fnv1a_64_update(fnv1a_64(p), d) == fnv1a_64(p ++ d) (with Clean-origin fnv1a_64_xor and fnv1a_64_mul_prime composed in fnv1a_64_mix_u64, FNV1A_64_INIT = 0xcbf29ce484222325, and FNV1A_64_PRIME = 0x100000001b3). It changes throughput, not output. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-wire --lib hash` |
| `wire.cut.consistent-lattice` | CausalCut<K> and CausalEdge<K> in astream-wire form a generic Chandy-Lamport consistent-cut lattice over offset frontiers keyed by any stream id: is_consistent admits causal prefixes and in-flight cuts while rejecting orphan-effect cuts; first_violation returns the offending edge; heal computes the minimal transitive causal closure and terminates on cyclic edge sets; join and meet form a distributive lattice aligned with dominates; and astream-engine::fleet delegates Cut and CrossEdge to it: its is_consistent, first_violation and heal_cut agree with an independent oracle that has its own cut lookup (the same verdict, the same first violated edge and frontiers, the same least consistent closure, over cuts that omit or duplicate partitions). V1Receipt in astream-wire::receipt is the view of the ack and expired records the bridges write on an in lane: decode(kind, body) reads a §4.1 `v=1` body under its in subject's last segment, needing v=1, re= and, on an expired, the relative dl=, reading an ack's verdict= when there is one (one of ReceiptVerdict::ALL) and giving none when there is none, keeping t= as the sender's clock (sender_t_ms, never a broker time), skipping from=, text= and unknown keys in any order, and ending the line at the first newline. asb ack's `v=1 t=<ms> re=<off> verdict=<word>`, which V1Receipt::encode writes byte for byte, and aterm's receipt `v=1 t=<ms> from=<sid> re=<off> verdict=<word> text=<pct>`, a session's posted ack `v=1 t=<ms> from=<sid> re=<off> text=<pct>` (no verdict) and expired verdict `v=1 t=<ms> re=<off> dl=<ms> text=<pct>` decode field for field; a body with no re=, with no v= or another version, an expired with no dl=, an ack whose verdict= is outside handled\|refused\|deferred, a malformed or overflowing number, or a field it reads given twice is refused (a node's halt answer on pub/<node>/node/ack is another face, not a receipt); every receipt it can hold round-trips under its kind, and any bytes get an answer without a panic (proptests). FabricReceipt and ReceiptVerdict also encode and decode a header-first `ack re=<off> verdict=handled\|refused\|deferred bt=<ms>` and `expired re=<off> dl=<ms> bt=<ms>` grammar with half-open deadline_elapsed semantics, which no writer emits: it refuses all four lines, its v=1 arm (the retired asb ack `state=` spelling, whose sender t= it read as bt_ms) is retired, and the TLA+ island TlaFabricReceipts it carries models an absolute deadline on the clock that stamps bt, not the asker-judged relative dl= the bus carries. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-wire --lib cut && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-wire --lib receipt && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-engine --test suite -- fleet_consistent_cut::` |
| `wire.steps.torn-vs-fault` | astream_wire::steps is astream's generic opaque record: a CRC frame whose payload is STEP_VERSION, a u64 step and a u32 body length before the body, and encode_step writes exactly those bytes (checked against a record built by hand from the layout and stamped with the literal version byte 1 that every record already on disk holds, under which a hand-made run also scans back Clean; the frozen oracle below carries its own literal 1 as well, so moving the live constant fails both). scan_steps_from(bytes, first) accepts a record only while its step is the next one from any base `first`, returns each body as a range into the scanned bytes, and classifies what ended the run. Proptests at a random base: a run scans Clean and its ranges slice to the bodies; a cut at every byte of a run whose bodies nest no frame is Clean at a record boundary and Torn elsewhere, never a fault, keeping every record before the cut; a body that nests a whole frame is the one case the rule cannot tell from damage, and the module documents it as such (a strict error that costs a refusal, never an acknowledged record): a cut past the nested frame's last byte is a LengthPastEnd fault at its record and a cut before it is Torn, keeping the record before it either way (a fixture and a proptest), and a damaged last record that nests one is CorruptFrame; one damaged payload byte is a CorruptFrame fault at its record when an intact record follows it and Torn when it is the last; a whole run followed by zero bytes of any length, fewer than a frame header's 12, exactly 12 or more (the gap a ring's writer leaves before its physical end), is Torn with valid_len at the last record's end and every record kept, never a fault, and no zeros is Clean, while the negative control, the same zeros with the run's next record after them, is a CorruptFrame fault at the first zero keeping the same records. Fixtures at a non-zero base: a truncated last record is Torn; a corrupt middle record is CorruptFrame; a skipped, repeated or early step is OutOfOrder (a run from step 0 scanned from 5 is OutOfOrder expected 5, found 0, at byte 0); a rotted length is LengthPastEnd mid-run and Torn on the last record; another STEP_VERSION or a body length past the payload is BadStep. The count never wraps: an intact record after step u64::MAX, the last a run can hold, is a PastLastStep fault naming the step it found (with or without more records behind it), never Torn and never a step 0 accepted as the next, while a foreign, cut-short or corrupt record there is BadStep, Torn or CorruptFrame as anywhere. Inside a region bounded by a commit word any end but Clean is a fault: the module documents that posture and one fixture shows it (Clean below the word while a half-written record sits above it, and Torn past the word with valid_len at the word and exactly the records below it); the scanner does not enforce it. astream-engine's WorkflowJournal recovers through this scanner, and its verdicts (outputs, prefix length, tail, fault offset and kind) equal the pre-move scanner's, kept verbatim as a frozen oracle, over one fixed witness of each verdict a journal can reach (Clean, Torn, OutOfOrder, BadStep, LengthPastEnd, CorruptFrame), a cut through a record that nests a frame (LengthPastEnd from both: the rule is the old one), damaged generated journals and arbitrary bytes. The byte-by-byte search for a decodable frame after a break (frame_decodes_after, the LengthPastEnd-vs-Torn and CorruptFrame-vs-Torn rules) runs each candidate's CRC under a linear budget, max(RESYNC_CRC_PER_BYTE times the bytes scanned, RESYNC_CRC_FLOOR = 16 MiB): a candidate is charged its declared length before its CRC runs, so bytes crammed with frame-look-alikes cannot make the search quadratic. A counted-work seam reads the CRC bytes hashed with no clock. Under an explicit budget the hostile buffer hashes at most that budget whatever its size and answers found (a conservative fault), while the same search with no budget hashes far past it and super-linearly in the bytes. Under the production budget, buffers crammed past the floor (24 and 48 KiB of look-alikes) hash at most the floor and are read as a fault, while the negative control, the same bytes searched with no budget, finds no frame and hashes past the floor (each look-alike names at least one byte: twelve bytes naming none are a real empty frame, as a fixture shows), so the budget alone turns that torn tail into the fault; a run with an intact record before such a break keeps that record and ends in a CorruptFrame fault at the break's offset. Below the floor nothing changes: 16 KiB of look-alikes cost the unbounded search under 16 MiB and stay Torn. Past 8 MiB after the break the slope is the budget: it is the floor with 8 MiB scanned and the floor plus 2 one byte later, and twice the bytes scanned at 9 MiB from either of two break offsets; over 9 MiB, three look-alikes whose declared lengths add up to exactly that budget are all hashed and read Torn, as the unbounded search reads them (the floor alone would stop at the second), and one byte more stops the search before the third CRC and reads a fault while the unbounded search still finds no frame. A frame a short way after the break is still found, and plain torn bytes with no frame after are still Torn. A stopped search answers fault, never Torn, so a record the unsearched bytes might hold is never dropped: the bounded answer is the unbounded one or a fault, and its only cost is a torn tail whose failing look-alikes' declared lengths add up past the budget read as a fault (a refused file open, repaired by open_truncating; inside a commit-word region only the variant, never the kept prefix). Since an empty frame is twelve fixed bytes, whoever writes the bytes after a break could always force that fault anyway. aterm runs this scanner and these bytes outside the engine: aterm-restore keeps a crash-restore region's keyframes and history chunks as step records, and its history ring's walk reads a non-Clean end with only zero bytes past valid_len as the gap above (aterm `vendor/README-astream.md`; its test layout_v1_holds_astream_step_records_byte_for_byte spells the frame MAGIC, frame VERSION, STEP_VERSION and the step layout out by hand). So a change to those bytes changes aterm's region format: a region must stay readable across one aterm update, or aterm raises its LAYOUT_VERSION in the bump that brings the change. aterm also keeps step records in files of their own: aterm-link's post journal, each record with a tail after its body that the scan skips under the frame's CRC (the_scan_skips_a_records_tail_and_reads_the_next_record), and its asks journal; agh's write journal; aterm-aos's event log (`docs/ROADMAP.md`, What aterm uses beyond the fabric). | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-wire --lib steps && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-wire --test suite -- steps:: && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-engine --test suite -- step_scan_parity::` |
| `wire.sync.causal-index` | astream_wire::sync is the causal index an application log decides on, above a local durable log (docs/DESIGN-application-sync.md). A borrowed Proposal checks successive verified headers without cloning retained history or reserving base-index capacity. Each rejection leaves earlier accepted proposals intact, and identical duplicates consume no room. Exhaustive short input sequences, varied capacities and retained prefixes compare every per-record result and running new-record count against actual sequential insertion, continuing after shape, collision and capacity refusals. The all-or-error check_insert_many uses the same proposal mechanism. insert takes a verified header and answers true, or false for an identical duplicate, at capacity too; each refusal (Shape: a zero sequence, an impossible predecessor, unordered or duplicate parents, a self-reference, more parents than MAX_PARENTS, and exactly MAX_PARENTS is shaped; DigestCollision; Capacity) leaves every retained header, forks() and both projections unchanged, which the test compares whole, offering each to a full index; they come in one order: Shape is judged before the id is looked up (an unshaped header under a retained id answers Shape, not DigestCollision), and DigestCollision before Capacity. observes(later, earlier) is reachability through retained parents only: false when either record is not retained, reflexive only for a retained record, and false across a missing intermediate, even with both ends retained and of one actor; a cycle ends. Projection::observes(index, later, earlier) answers exactly what observes does, from version vectors project_excluding computes once in causal order for each discharged record (sparse, at most VECTOR_ENTRIES_PER_RECORD entries per retained record; a record past that bound walks), and by a walk that stops at discharged records for any other later: a property test over generated forked histories (forks, gaps, missing intermediates, cross-actor and unretained parents, policy exclusions, shuffled delivery) compares it with observes on every pair, the vectors alone (no index to walk) on every discharged later, ancestors(id) with the set observes answers, and frontier() (each actor's highest retained sequence, kept at insert) with a recount; a chain of 200 writers passes the bound and still agrees; the negative control is a planted vector that forgets a transitive ancestor. forks() gives ONE deterministic witness per equivocating actor, the least pair of retained source records, in every delivery order; it does NOT list every pair. forks_of(headers) is forks() of an index holding exactly the set of those headers, with no index built: they may come in any order and any of them more than once (each shaped, and two sharing an id equal, which is what insert admits). Over every subset of a ten-header pool of two actors (a linked chain, a second header at one sequence, a header whose predecessor is never retained, one past a gap, one linked to the forked branch, a pair at u64::MAX and u64::MAX - 1), read in ascending, descending and rotated id order, each also with each header given again, and over generated histories read in a generated order with generated repeats, it equals forks() of the set and a quadratic Fork::between oracle. Header::is_shaped is whether insert admits the header's shape: it matches a column the test writes over each Shape refusal above, a collision, a capacity refusal and exactly MAX_PARENTS, and is false exactly where insert answers Shape. Fork::between(a, b) answers for two distinct, well-shaped headers of one actor whose claims contradict at a sequence, and nothing else (one header twice, another actor, an unshaped header, claims that do not contradict); it answers the same whichever comes first, with first the smaller digest and second the larger, which forks()'s witness is too. project and project_excluding withdraw a forked actor and every dependent of it or of an excluded record, keep both in the inventory, and converge for every delivery order and duplicate of a small history (every permutation of 7 records). The two laws are coded apart from the index, each with its negative control: the observation law catches a planted observes that reads an actor's dense chain across a missing parent, and the projection law catches a planted projection that admits a forked record, its dependent, an excluded record or that record's dependent, or that drops withheld evidence from the view. aterm builds on these semantics: aterm-db's admission rules ask observes before a Revert or a Spend, its visible history is project_excluding, its table store withholds a forked actor by forks(), or by forks_of over the headers its index would hold, and replays each stored certificate through Fork::between at the open, aterm-secrets' commons journal asks observes whether the control history reached its genesis, and its constraints build the visible catalog with project_excluding and read sequence_follows (`docs/ROADMAP.md`, What aterm uses beyond the fabric). Ordinary tests over small histories, as DESIGN-application-sync.md scopes them: not a verified distributed database. The index authenticates nothing (the caller verifies each record before insert), and holds no clock, network, retention or I/O. Its Clean kernels (sync_island.rs) are verify.clean-origin-tla-protocols's. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-wire --lib sync::` |
| `wire.sync.range-reconciliation` | astream_wire::sync::rbsr reconciles immutable inventories of physical content identities through transport-independent request/reply endpoints (docs/DESIGN-application-sync.md). A snapshot retains every digest, including pending and forked records. Exact actor anchors skip only provable dense unforked prefixes; gaps and forks remain range inventory. Sixteen-way ranges use count plus digest sum modulo 2^256; compact 32-bit probes cannot finish without conservation of both full root counts and all 256 sum bits, and a mismatch refines once at full width. Deliberately colliding short probes exercise that actual refinement and find the omitted identities. Both endpoints expose sorted send/want differences only after validation; Finish is inventory agreement, never a durable-record acknowledgment or a payload permission. Tests compare both endpoints with direct set subtraction over varied sets, empty sets and whole differences exceeding a page, and two inventories of 8192 identities differing by 16 per side exchange under 8192 bytes including outer u32 lengths in both directions, both with four actor frontiers and with no actor shortcut at all. Canonical prefix-only ranges and omission of known-zero empty fingerprints reduce bytes without weakening the full check. Frames and pages bound individual allocations, not total inventory size; callers set explicit remote-record and work budgets. Truncated, trailing, oversized, nonminimal or overflowing encodings refuse, as do empty continuation pages, wrong phases, stale ranges/cursors, inconsistent counts, reordered actor or identity pages and a wrong final fingerprint. Identities occupy a shared sorted slice, while actor-prefix and unmatched-identity metadata occupy vectors. retained_bytes charges the value, identity data and every vector capacity; allocator and sharing headers remain fixed overhead for the caller. Tests grow each retained vector without changing its inventory or fingerprint, and an uncut view shares identity storage and its cached fingerprint. Immutable root fingerprints and dense-prefix partitions can be reused; current custody, historical-membership and key eligibility remain the integrator's fresh permission decisions. Fingerprints can collide and do not prove arbitrary-set equality or authenticate anything. Ordinary tests, not a formal distributed-database proof. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-wire --lib sync::rbsr` |
| `wire.summary.mergeable-exact` | astream_wire::summary is the metric primitive aterm's per-Mac quota, token and streaming statistics are built on (aterm docs/DESIGN-alab-one-mesh-2026-11-05.md): each Mac rolls its own events into a Summary per metric and aligned window and writes it as its own row, and a fleet view is a merge computed from the replica. A Summary holds the count, the exact u128 sum, the exact minimum and maximum and a histogram over one fixed log-linear layout (1,920 buckets: 0..=63 one each, then 32 per power of two), which tiles every u64 in order with each bucket at most 1/32 of its low end wide (checked bucket by bucket). Proptests: merge is associative and commutative with the empty summary as identity, including summaries whose counts, bucket counts and sum saturate; the summary of two runs is the merge of their summaries; record_n(v, n) is n records; merge is not idempotent (a summary merged with itself is the summary of its values twice); every rank of a merged summary reads within x/64 of its exact value x, inside [min, max] and never below a lower rank, and exactly for x < 64 and the first and last rank, with the extremes exact in wide buckets too; a Quantile reads its nearest rank, max(1, ceil(ppm * count / 10^6)); at a saturated count the bound still holds and Quantile::MAX is still the maximum. Fine minute, hour, day and Monday-aligned week windows roll up by merging to exactly the summaries of the raw events in the coarse windows, every window holds its start and not its end, and nests in every coarser window. A Rollup, fed every version of every origin's minute rows shuffled with duplicates and a forget among them, holds exactly each live identity's newest version, so its fleet and per-origin views equal the summaries of the raw events in the live windows, while the negative control, merging every delivered row, over-counts; offering everything again changes nothing, a tie between two versions resolves the same in either order (also when only the buckets differ), forgetting is per window length and a late stale copy of a forgotten row is refused. Counter (max) and Gauge (later reading) joins are idempotent, commutative and associative, and a fleet total of joined counters never double-counts. Encodings ('S', 'C', 'G', 'I' kinds, version 1, LEB128) round-trip, are pinned byte for byte, refuse every proper prefix, trailing bytes, every other version and kind, non-minimal and overflowing varints and invalid names and windows, and any bytes a decoder accepts are the canonical encoding of what it decodes to; an exhaustive sweep of every multiset of one to four values over unit, width-2 and width-4 buckets shows the decoder accepts a minimum, maximum and sum exactly when some multiset has them, and a witness proptest over clustered values that it accepts exactly the sums between the least and greatest witness. The largest encoding is MAX_SUMMARY_LEN = 21,154 bytes, and a fixture day of 96 real agent turns on two Macs reads back exact totals and p50/p90/p95/p99 within the bound in 194 bytes (output tokens) and 158 (cache reads). | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-wire --test suite -- summary::` |
| `gate.rejects-known-failures` | The merge gate rejects undeclared cfg-features (any whitespace, including a line break, around the `=`), git dependencies (every dependency table, [patch]/[replace] redirects, and git+ sources in Cargo.lock), unlinked task/drift markers (non-UTF-8 files included), constant-only tests (including assertion-message and `as`-cast laundered forms), Trust-only syntax a stock rustc would open (an inline `clean { ... }` island outside a `*_island.rs` file, an island file mounted without `cfg(clean_islands)`, a bare `ensures`/`requires` clause outside `contract_fn!`), and claims whose kind or platform is outside the closed sets. It never follows symlinks, prunes a named list of tool-state directories (.git, .claude, editor/direnv caches) so a path dependency vendored into any other dot-directory is still linted, refuses to run outside the workspace root, and astream passes its own gate. It also prunes a directory holding a CACHEDIR.TAG with the spec signature on its first line (an untagged same-named directory, or a tag with a wrong signature, is still scanned) and names every such skip on stdout. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-evidence` |
| `gate.rejects-theater-evidence` | The merge gate rejects claims whose command is a known no-op program (a denylist -- true, :, echo, printf, cat, head, tail -- each asserted by a fixture claim, also behind leading VAR=value assignments), that can mask its own exit status (`\|\|`, `;`, a pipe, a background `&`, a newline or a leading `!` outside quotes; `&&` is allowed), whose command does not fit its kind (a `test` claim must run `targo <lane> test`, a `build` claim `targo <lane> build`/`check`, a `selftest` must pin an output hash), that runs the stock toolchain (`cargo`, `rustc`, `rustup`, ... in any `&&` step, or a `+toolchain` selector) or a compiling targo verb that names no lane (`--unverified` or `trust`), or that cite a cargo package/target or path absent from the tree. This narrows decorative evidence rather than eliminating it: an exit-0 program outside the denylist under a bench/selftest kind still passes the gate. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-evidence manifest_integrity` |
| `term.screen-fold.deterministic` | The astream-term VT screen is a deterministic, I/O-free fold of recorded output: a fixed adversarial record sequence (cursor moves, SGR, erase, alternate-screen round-trip, resize) folds to an independently asserted expected screen, folding twice is byte-identical, In/Exit records never paint, and folding arbitrary byte streams is panic-free and deterministic (proptest). ECMA-48 edge cases are each pinned by a fixed case modelled on xterm: OSC/DCS/APC strings and two-byte escapes are consumed unpainted; 24-bit truecolour SGR (38;2;r;g;b and 48;2;r;g;b) resolves exact fg_rgb and bg_rgb cell overrides (cleared by default or palette SGR codes, carried into bce blanks, and saved/restored by DECSC/DECRC), a channel past 255 clamped to 255 in the semicolon and both colon spellings, as aterm clamps it (xterm drops such a colour, Ghostty truncates it), a colon on the code or on the selector makes one group that is all the colour's (38:2:cs:r:g:b, 38;2:cs:r:g:b: the first of four values is the colour-space id, T.416 13.1.8 and xterm parse_extended_colors, and three values are the channels), CMY and CMYK leave the pen alone in every spelling, 58 reads as 38 does onto the underline colour (term.fold.underline states the underline), and no sub-parameter ever runs as a code, each answer written out by hand for 38, 48 and 58, East Asian Wide and Fullwidth scalars occupy two columns, a width-2 lead and a width-0 spacer (term.fold.wide-glyphs states the rules), and zero-width code points (combining marks, the variation selectors U+FE0E/U+FE0F, ZWJ U+200D) attach up to MAX_COMBINING = 16 to the glyph parked under a pending wrap, else to the cell left of the cursor, a wide glyph's lead rather than its spacer, and at column 0 or the left margin, on a row that continues the one above too, to the cursor's own cell, though a glyph printed up to the left margin from outside it still takes them until a motion comes, and so do a Fitzpatrick skin-tone modifier after a wide emoji modifier base, the second regional indicator of a flag and a glyph other than ASCII after a ZWJ that was the last thing printed, as aterm joins them, while U+FE0F widens a text-default emoji where the line has room for its spacer and U+FE0E narrows a wide glyph; SGR 3, 5 and 6, 8 and 9 set italic, blink, concealed and crossed-out, each its own attribute bit, which 23, 25, 28 and 29 clear one by one and 0 clears together, DECSC saves and a bce blank never carries, and 21, the double underline, is the underline's double style and no attribute; private-marker CSI and colon sub-parameters never reset the pen; ESC inside an unfinished CSI starts a new escape; DEL is ignored and malformed UTF-8 yields one U+FFFD per maximal subpart (Unicode 3.9); pending-wrap resolution follows xterm (motion and ED/EL resolve it, an erase from it clears the parked glyph, TAB keeps it), a resize clips or extends each row and keeps the cursor's next write (an edge wrap widened takes the next column and one narrowed waits on the new edge, a cursor whose cell a narrowing cuts off is clamped onto the last column with no wrap, as xterm clamps it, and a wrap at a right margin stays pending on its column), ED 3 spares the screen, DECSC/DECRC save and restore the position, wrap and SGR rendition per buffer, there is one persistent alternate buffer (47 switches, 1047 clears on leaving, 1049 clears on entering and restores on leaving, each clear resetting the wrap), VT/FF/IND/NEL are line feeds and ICH/DCH/ECH edit the cursor line, origin mode (DECOM) homes to the top margin, counts CUP/VPA rows from it and stops at the bottom one, is saved by DECSC and cleared by a restore of a slot never saved and by RIS, insert mode (IRM) shifts the line right under each glyph, newline mode (LNM) returns the carriage on LF, VT and FF, and SU/SD scroll the region without moving the cursor or losing its pending wrap while CSI 0 T, highlight mouse tracking, scrolls nothing, and G0 and G1 hold ASCII or DEC Special Graphics (xterm's table) with SO/SI mapping one into GL, saved by DECSC and reset by a restore of a slot never saved and by RIS, each modelled on xterm's source. Cross-validated against the production aterm engine, cell by cell and mark by mark, by aterm's differential VT oracle (its aterm-conformance vt suite), which this command does not run. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-term --test suite -- screen_fold::` |
| `term.replay.byte-identical` | A captured terminal session (In/Out/Resize/Exit fed as effect-seam inputs, not a live PTY) records into a single-writer Log under a seeded seam, each record a versioned envelope (tag, seq, ts_logical from the seam Clock) inside a CRC frame on an in-memory Disk. After the live records are dropped, a separate read-by-offset pass over the stored bytes reconstructs records whose /out stream folds to a screen byte-equal to the live end-of-session screen, with seq and ts_logical equal to their recorded values; two seeded record passes produce byte-identical logs. Durability across process death is not exercised here (see term.strict.survives-kill). | `targo --unverified run --quiet --locked -p astream-engine --example replay` |
| `term.strict.survives-kill` | A durable log file with a torn final frame or a CRC-corrupt final record recovers to the last intact record: the tail is dropped (and truncated on disk by FileLog::open), every prior fsync'd record is retained, and the log re-folds to the screen as of that offset. A fault -- a corrupt frame followed by intact frames, another envelope version, or an out-of-sequence record -- is refused by FileLog::open with an InvalidData error naming the byte offset, leaving the file untouched; only FileLog::open_truncating repairs it. Session::resume_from continues a recovered file at the next offset with the clock past the stored ts_logical. On Unix, SIGKILL of a child appending fsync'd records recovers every acked record in order, as a clean prefix or torn tail, never a fault. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-engine --test suite -- crash_recovery::` |
| `term.resume.gapless-exactly-once` | A reattaching client paints the /a/state snapshot (the screen folded through offset K, with parser state) and folds only the log tail after K -- checked by replacing the first K+1 records with garbage -- landing on the exact screen of a never-dropped run at every K, including an escape sequence straddling the boundary. A snapshot never claims offsets it did not fold: an empty log gives folded_through = None and an over-range K is clamped. A re-sent input proposal is applied exactly once, deduped by (client_id, client_seq) in-session and across a reconnect whose dedup high-water is rebuilt from the log alone. Session::resume_from continues a recovered log and matches an uninterrupted recording byte for byte; it refuses a damaged log. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-engine --test suite -- resume::` |
| `term.echo.predict-and-reconcile` | Speculative local echo draws a printable keystroke (faint, unconfirmed) over the authoritative screen; after authoritative /out is folded, each prediction is confirmed or rolled back to ground truth. A predicted space is confirmed only once output shows its cell was traversed (the cursor passed it, or a glyph was painted to its right on the same row); otherwise it stays undecided/Lost, or Contradicted once the cell is painted with anything else, so a dropped space is never reported Confirmed. The predictor abstains on control/escape input. For any interleaving of predictions and output (proptest), the pre-reconcile view differs from the authoritative fold exactly at the outstanding prediction cells, every verdict matches an independent oracle, and the reconciled view equals the pure authoritative fold. ProgramPredictor implements program-response speculation P̂ (THEORY-deterministic-sessions.md §4): it learns bounded deterministic command-key -> ScreenOp delta rules at matching screen geometry, speculatively renders repeated prompt-bounded commands in 0 RTT, confirms matching authoritative output into an exact screen view, and rolls back divergent output (with rule invalidation) so reconciled.eq_full(authoritative) holds universally (proptest + Clean-origin TlaProgramPredict). SpeculativeTerminal composes learned ProgramPredictor rules with zero-cold-start cooked/readline line-editing speculation (Backspace, Ctrl-W, Ctrl-U, Ctrl-A, Ctrl-E, Left/Right), silent-prompt suppression (password:/passphrase:/pin:), and dual caused_by / out_offset watermark retirement, preserving reconciled.eq_full(authoritative) across arbitrary input/output interleavings. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-term --test suite -- predict::` |
| `term.fork.counterfactual-replay` | Recording an agent session (In = what it typed, Out = what the world answered, both seam-recorded), then forking the log at the offset of one Out record and swapping it (the build result the agent saw), yields a byte-exact and screen-exact alternate history: re-forking is deterministic, the alternate log and screen differ from the original exactly at the swap, and untouched lines are identical. The output is pinned as a re-runnable regression. This forks the recorded history only; a live agent re-deciding on the swapped output is not exercised. | `targo --unverified run --quiet --locked -p astream-engine --example fork` |
| `term.fork.multi-client` | Independent clients folding the same session log produce byte-identical screens (multi-client attach is consistent), a capability is a Filter over the session's subject subtree (read-only attach matches /a/stream/<S>/> but not the inbox; control matches the inbox but not the stream; read-only does not match another session's stream and control does not match another session's inbox), and the counterfactual fork is deterministic and exact. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-engine --test suite -- fork::` |
| `term.fork.prefix-stable` | Counterfactual fork is prefix-stable over the single-writer log: forking a recorded session at offset N (try_fork_swap) and re-recording under the same seed yields a log whose first N framed records are byte-identical to the original, whose bytes from N on differ, and which is deterministic across re-records -- for a fixed agent session and, by proptest, for arbitrary Out vectors with a differing replacement body. The log is not hash-linked, so a content-addressed (Merkle) session history is not established. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-engine --test suite -- fork_prefix_stable::` |
| `term.pty.records-and-replays` | On Unix, astream-host opens a real pseudo-terminal (posix_openpt on Linux, openpty elsewhere, so a macOS run exercises only openpty), fork+execs a deterministic child, drains the master into Record::Out on the single-writer Log through the effect seam, and reaps the exit into Record::Exit. A separate pass over only the stored bytes (no PTY, no OS calls) reconstructs records whose /out folds byte-equal to both the live final screen and an independently asserted expected screen; chunk boundaries and timing are not asserted. An unexecutable program is an Err from Pty::spawn carrying the exec errno, never Ok ending in Exit 127, and the child runs with default SIGPIPE, not the runtime's SIG_IGN. The workspace's unsafe code is confined to two cfg-gated astream-host modules and astream-sys's two cordoned modules (os.cordon), compiler-enforced. A recorded real-PTY session that replays, not an interactive shell or SSH. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-host --test suite -- pty_record_replay::` |
| `term.fleet.consistent-cut-replay` | Two fleet sessions on independent single-writer logs with a recorded cross-injection edge (an orchestrator's Out at one offset causing a child's injected In at another, held as an in-memory CrossEdge of real offsets, not a wire field) admit a consistent cut -- a vector of per-partition offsets that admits the cause of every effect it admits; effect-without-cause cuts are rejected, in-flight cuts admitted. Replaying each partition from its first record up to and including its cut offset re-folds byte-equal to its truncated live screen, over a fixed scenario and by proptest over two partitions' arbitrary Out vectors and cut offsets (a partition may be absent). A decode error before the cut is returned, bytes past the cut are never read, and is_consistent, first_violation and heal_cut agree with an independent oracle (the same verdict, first violated edge and least consistent closure). The durable on-log form is term.fleet.durable-watermark. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-engine --test suite -- fleet_consistent_cut::` |
| `term.echo.watermark-retire` | Speculative echo retires against the engine out-offset (read back from a real single-writer Session log via Log::read_from, a numbering domain distinct from client_seq), not screen position: retirement is incremental and watermark-monotone, a contradiction squash-rebuilds the overlay from confirmed state, an out-of-order watermark is rejected, and for any interleaving of predictions and watermark-ordered output (proptest) the authoritative screen stays the pure fold and the reconciled view shows no residue. It retires by monotone offset progress, not causal link (see term.echo.caused-by-retire). | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-engine --test suite -- watermark_retire::` |
| `term.screen-ops.lossless-projection` | One VT parser (astream-term::screen) projects the raw Out byte log into a typed screen-op stream (print, cursor moves, erase, erase/insert/delete characters, insert/delete lines, reverse index, resolved SGR, 24-bit truecolour SetSgrRgb (tag 0x21), the underline's style and colour SetUnderline (tag 0x22), DEC private modes, save/restore cursor, scroll region, left/right margins, insert and newline mode, scroll up/down, G0/G1 designation and shift, reset, resize), roundtrips every ScreenOp variant through the compact `ASOP` v1 binary wire codec (`encode_ops` / `decode_ops`, with bounded pre-allocation and hostile-input rejection over proptest), and re-applying the ops (`apply_ops` or incremental per-record `Folder::new_recording` + `take_ops` + `apply_ops_to`) reproduces a screen byte-equal to the raw VT fold over a fixed adversarial fixture and arbitrary byte streams at small viewports (proptest). Op re-application is an independent dispatch path through the shared mutators, bypassing only the VT state machine. The projection is lossless only for this fold's bounded subset (scroll region, left/right margins, origin, insert and newline modes, background-colour erase, G0/G1 character sets, 24-bit truecolour fg_rgb/bg_rgb, the underline's style and colour, East Asian wide glyphs (a width-2 lead and a width-0 spacer), and combining marks, variation selectors, skin-tone modifiers and ZWJ clusters up to MAX_COMBINING = 16 marks in; marks past 16 and G2/G3 or national sets out; string sequences and unmodelled escapes emit no op), not full xterm. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-term --test suite -- screen_ops::` |
| `term.perceive.query` | An orchestrator reads any child terminal as text and structured queries reconstructed from the recorded Out log at any offset: frame(K) equals the fold of records[0..=K], text() reads per-row lines, blocks() partitions exactly the maximal contiguous non-empty regions (the exact Block list on the fixture; structural invariants on arbitrary bytes), and search() returns character-column-accurate, non-overlapping, multibyte-correct hits -- over an evolving fixture at every offset, and panic-free and self-consistent on arbitrary bytes (proptest). OSC/DCS string sequences never reach text, search, or blocks. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-term --test suite -- perceive::` |
| `term.perceive.semantic-events` | astream-term classifies a recorded session into the semantic boundaries a driving agent wakes on -- prompt-ready, command-start, command-end (with exit code), screen-quiescence -- as a deterministic, I/O-free projection of the Out log. OSC-133/633 A/C/D marks are the authoritative signal, recognised even when split across records; a frame_hash-stable quiescence point is the fallback; DEC private mode `?2026` (`synchronized_output`, alongside `?1` `app_cursor_keys`, `?1004` `focus_events`, and `?2004` `bracketed_paste` on `Screen`) suppresses mid-redraw `ScreenQuiesced` wakes via `EventClassifier::quiesce_if_unsynced` up to `MAX_SYNC_OUTPUT_BYTES` (1 MiB, enforced by Clean-origin `TlaSyncOutput`: `sync_bytes_clamp`, `sync_hold_ticks_clamp`, `sync_within_budget`) so wedged TUIs that omit `?2026l` fail open cleanly; `OSC 0`/`2` sanitized window titles (`Folder::title`, `MAX_OSC_TITLE_BYTES = 512`) and `OSC 7` percent-decoded `file://` working directories (`Folder::cwd`, `MAX_OSC_CWD_BYTES = 1024`) are tracked without polluting grid cells; per-program glyph Profiles (common, claude, codex, repl, emacs, shell, none; looked up by Profile::by_name) matched on the settled last line supply prompt-ready only for a target that has emitted no A, C or D mark, and other mark letters do not disable it; Profile::repl and Profile::emacs omit shell $/ % / # markers so settled mid-evaluation progress lines never false-trigger PromptReady. Across a retention gap, EventClassifier::reset_escape (and Folder::reset_escape) clears torn mid-record OSC-133 carry, sync-update byte budgets, and unterminated OSC/DCS string parser state while preserving painted cells, preventing both phantom CommandEnd wakes and swallowed post-gap prompts. Events follow streaming order, not a global offset sort. No false prompt without a marker; panic-free and deterministic over adversarial bytes. A streaming EventClassifier matches the batch classifier over the fixtures, an OSC-133 session split at every byte boundary, and proptest-generated sessions cut at arbitrary lengths. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-term --test suite -- events::` |
| `pump.wake-on-boundaries` | The astream-pump Pump, subscribed to a target session's /out subtree on a live in-process broker, classifies deliveries into semantic wakes (prompt-ready, command-start, command-end with exit code, screen-quiescence), exercising broker subscribe -> fold -> classify -> wake end to end, and surfaces R4 addendum A9 retention gap notices via Pump::take_gap while resetting mid-escape VT/OSC state across gaps so torn pre-gap escapes neither forge wakes nor swallow post-gap prompts (`pump_surfaces_retention_gap_and_resets_mid_stream_torn_escapes`). Also exercises cross-machine TCP ScreenOp projection (`cross_machine_tcp_screen_ops_stream_keeps_viewers_byte_identical`), where live and late-joining TCP viewers reconstruct byte-identical Screens from encoded ScreenOp batches without running a VT byte parser. Each OSC wake surfaces from next_wake() on the exact delivery that carries it, tagged with that delivery's broker offset, and quiesce() yields only the settle event. from_offset is inclusive: re-attaching at offset() re-delivers the last wake, at next_offset() continues with no gap or duplicate. The cursor here is client-held (the durable group form is pump.attach-subject-and-group). The crate command also runs the pump.cli.wake-lines tests. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-pump` |
| `pump.cli.wake-lines` | The `aspump` binary, spawned against a live in-process broker, tails a session's /out and prints wake lines tagged with broker offsets -- for the fixture exactly `PROMPT_READY boffset=1`, `COMMAND_START boffset=3`, `COMMAND_END boffset=5 exit=0`, then `QUIESCED boffset=5` after --debounce. --from N is inclusive; --subject tails a fabric face; --tcp tails over TCP; --meta appends sanitized `cwd=` (`OSC 7`) and `title=` (`OSC 0`/`2`) tokens (`wake_line_with_meta`); DEC `?2026` synchronized-output frames hold debounced `QUIESCED` wakes for up to `MAX_SYNC_HOLD_TICKS = 4` intervals (`sync_hold_ticks_clamp`) so split multi-chunk TUI redraws never tear while unclosed `?2026h` frames still force-quiesce without deadlocking; --profile P selects a built-in prompt profile (common, claude, codex, repl, emacs, shell, none) so non-agent REPL and Emacs sessions without OSC-133 marks wake only at real prompt boundaries while `--profile common` false-wakes on settled `%` progress (`rung3_repl_and_emacs_profiles_drive_live_non_agent_programs_without_progress_false_wakes`); --cap-file (under the `cap` feature) attaches a capability ring before subscribing against a guarded broker; --group is a broker-durable cursor: a killed run's replacement replays nothing it committed except the one delivery whose commit it still held (at-least-once), so a QUIESCED the killed run never printed is printed by its replacement; an uncommitted group starts at 0. `--once` prints one wake line (whether an OSC-133 mark or a debounced settle wake), commits its offset under `--group`, and exits 0 immediately while staying parked on an idle stream before any record arrives. Retention gap notices (R4 addendum A9) print `aspump: GAP from=<F> to=<T> records=<N\|unknown>` on stderr before post-gap wakes while keeping stdout strictly wake-framed and resetting torn pre-gap VT/OSC escapes (`aspump_reports_retention_gap_on_stderr_and_recovers_from_torn_escape`). Composed end to end with `astream-host::Driver` (`Pty::spawn`, `Driver::drive_input`, `Driver::read_and_record_chunk`) and `astream-engine::Envelope` over a live broker (`drive_pipe_e2e_crash_recovery_misses_nothing_and_dedups_pty_input`), a driver killed mid-session reattaches with `--group --once`, re-sends its in-flight command without double-driving the PTY, and catches up across every missed wake boundary in order. A subscription rejection exits 1 with the reason on stderr; a broken stdout pipe or broker close exits 0; malformed, unknown `--profile`, or conflicting flags and a --key on argv (either spelling, never echoed) exit 2 before connecting. A stdout already closed at launch is not covered. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-pump --features cap --test suite -- aspump_cli::` |
| `pump.cli.argv-not-utf8` | An `aspump` argument that is not valid Unicode is a usage error (exit 2), never a panic (exit 101): checked on the real binary for a flag value (`--key-file` with a path containing byte 0xFF, whose message says the argument is not valid UTF-8) and for a positional, against a nonexistent endpoint so only argv rejection can yield 2. A dead stderr is not a panic either: with the pipe's read end dropped before spawn, a usage error still exits 2 and a connect failure still exits 1, and a readable stderr still gets the message. Unix-only tests covering argv and the standard streams only; non-UTF-8 bytes arriving by other routes (e.g. a subject or key read from a file) are not covered. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-pump --test suite -- aspump_argv:: aspump_stderr_status::` |
| `pump.attach-subject-and-group` | Over a live in-process broker, Pump::attach_subject on a fabric face (/f/F/term/n1/s1/out) yields exactly the wake lines Pump::attach yields on /a/stream/term/s1/out (asserted against a literal four-wake list at the same offsets); a record on another subject of the same log is never delivered; and its commit fails with Unsupported, having no group. Pump::attach_group subscribes as a broker consumer group: a pump that commits through offset 3 and is dropped is replaced by one given no offset, which is delivered 4, 5, 6 (never the hidden /a/commit records), and the two pumps' wakes joined equal one uninterrupted pump's. commit() commits through offset() and returns None before any delivery. The guarantee is at-least-once: a pump killed between delivery and commit re-delivers that record. Broker restart and capability enforcement are not exercised. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-pump --test suite -- pump::` |
| `pump.wake-adapters` | Drive-pipe agent wake hooks and durable broker task-queue adapters (`DESIGN-drive-pipe.md` §5 step 4: `WakeTaskQueue`, `WakeTaskItem`, `drain_wake_tasks`, `task_subject`, `WakeHookFormat::{Line, ClaudeNotificationJson, ClaudeStopJson, CodexTurnJson}`, `deliver_hook_unix`, `deliver_hook_tcp`, `aspump --task-subject`, `aspump --hook-unix`, `aspump --hook-tcp`, `aspump --hook-format`), backed by Clean-Origin (`I5`) `TlaWakeDispatch` (`wake_dispatch_seq_advance`, `wake_task_payload_clamp`, `wake_commit_after_ack`). Tested over a live broker, `aspump` subprocesses, and real Unix & TCP hook listeners: `aspump --task-subject` posts `(producer_id, wake_seq)`-deduplicated `WakeTaskItem`s that a turn-based driver drains non-blockingly up to the broker's caught-up `Mark` (`drain_wake_tasks`), `aspump --hook-unix` / `--hook-tcp` deliver escaped Claude Code `Notification` and Codex `turn.wake` JSON envelopes, and an unreachable hook socket exits `1` BEFORE advancing `--group`'s durable commit cursor so a restarted `aspump --group` re-delivers the wake with zero lost boundaries. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-pump --test suite -- wake_adapters::` |
| `pump.nested-claude-drive` | Hermetic nested Claude-Code-protocol TUI session driven end-to-end over a real PTY (`astream-host::Pty` + `Driver::drive_input` + `Driver::read_and_record_chunk`), `astream-broker`, and `aspump --profile claude --group --task-subject --hook-unix --hook-format claude-stop --meta`, backed by Clean-Origin (`I5`) `Astream.Pump.TlaNestedDrive` (`nested_turn_seq_advance`, `nested_prompt_bytes_clamp`, `nested_turn_ready_to_drive`, `NestedTurnState`). Verifies `❯ ` composer prompt detection after DEC `?2026h/?2026l` synchronized TUI redraws, suppression of false `PROMPT_READY` wakes on mid-turn `%` context-progress bars, mid-session pump crash & `--group` + `--task-seq-start` reattachment without double-driving PTY turns, Claude `Stop` JSON hook delivery, and deterministic `screen::fold` replay of the recorded session. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-pump --test suite -- nested_claude_drive::` |
| `pump.cli.security-transports` | Multi-machine security transport parity in the `aspump` CLI across Rungs 7–10 (`--handshake --key-file` forward-secret X25519+PSK TCP, `--roam --key-file` zero-gap AEAD-over-UDP Connection-ID migration `AROM` v1, `--host-key-file` + `--client-key-file` mutual Ed25519 signed-DH TCP, and `--cert-file` + `--ca-key-file` + `--ssh-agent-sock` / `--client-key-file` + `--host-principal` external CA-certified SIGMA TCP; features `handshake` and `identity`). Tested end-to-end by spawning real `aspump` subprocesses against guarded multi-listener `Broker` instances and an OpenSSH `SshAgentServer`: subscriber, `--group` commit, and `--task-subject` connections all authenticate over the selected transport with principal-bound capability verification, while wrong PSKs / mismatched `--host-principal` exit `1` and inline argv secrets (`--client-key`) or contradictory flags exit `2` before opening sockets. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-pump --all-features --test suite -- aspump_transports::` |
| `term.perceive.render-deterministic` | The image and animation perception modalities reconstruct deterministically from the recorded Out log at any offset. rasterize(screen) is a pure cell thumbnail (one RGB pixel per cell: resolved 24-bit fg_rgb/bg_rgb override or 16-colour ANSI palette fg if inked, else bg; reverse video swaps the resolved colours, asserted pixel-for-pixel); frame(K) is the screen as of offset K; animation(from,to) is the frame_hash-deduplicated frame sequence, in which In and Exit records yield unchanged frames; a forked timeline shares a bit-identical frame-hash prefix and diverges only after the fork. Over a fixture and arbitrary bytes (proptest), every frame hash equals an independent fold at that offset and rasterize is deterministic and panic-free. A cell thumbnail, not glyph-accurate rendering. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-term --test suite -- render::` |
| `term.drive.interactive-exactly-once` | On Unix, astream-host::Driver pairs a live PTY with a single-writer Session and dedups each input proposal by (client_id, client_seq) at the one ingest point: an echoing child is driven a keystroke, the same keystroke is re-sent (dropped, not written), then quit; its output contains the response exactly once and the log records exactly the two distinct keystrokes. Token-gated live PTY ingest (`Driver::drive_input_as` / `drive_input_caused_as`) checks `ControlToken` via `Session::precheck_input_as` before any byte reaches the PTY master, dedup map, or session log, refusing a non-holder with `EngineError::NotHolder` while `claim` and `grant` transfer live PTY authority cleanly; once any `Record::In` has been committed, `read_and_record_chunk` and `drain_to_eof` stamp same-log `caused_by` (`partition: None`) referencing `last_in_offset()` on subsequent `Record::Out` chunks so `Predictor::apply_output_caused` retires live PTY predictions directly from decoded envelopes. A 128-line (8 KiB) burst plus quit, with no drain in between, is delivered without deadlock -- the child reads each line exactly once and in order (its own tally, printed on quit), all 129 Ins recorded -- because the driver drains output while the input queue is full. That drain is bounded: a child emitting 8 KiB per input byte fails the write at the 8 MiB PENDING_MAX cap with no In recorded. A proposal one byte past the frame payload cap is refused by Session::precheck_input before any byte reaches the PTY. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-host --test suite -- drive::` |
| `term.fleet.orchestrate-n` | On Unix, one orchestrator drives three real heterogeneous child terminals (one non-interactive, two interactively driven), each its own single-writer partition (Driver = Pty + Session). Each child session re-folds from its stored bytes to the child's screen; the interactive children echo their dispatched commands via `Driver::drive_input_caused` stamping durable cross-partition `caused_by` headers, and each log holds exactly its two injected Ins (the non-interactive child's none). `cross_edges_from_logs` rebuilds the causal edges directly from the stored PTY logs (ignoring same-log `Out` causes), `materialize_fleet_cut` materializes a `FleetSnapshot` across all four live partitions over the consistent cut, and an orphaned-injection cut (effect admitted, cause excluded) is rejected. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-host --test suite -- orchestrate::` |
| `term.fleet.control-handoff` | A shared session has exactly one writer at a time, enforced by the engine and live PTY host: Session::apply_input_as / apply_input_caused_as and Driver::drive_input_as / drive_input_caused_as take the session's ControlToken and refuse a non-holder with EngineError::NotHolder before the PTY, log, or dedup map is touched. Control transfers by claim (an explicit override) and grant (a non-holder's grant is refused). The log's In records are exactly the holder's inputs in order, each carrying the controller's client_id, so they form an auditable control history; refused attempts leave no trace and bump no dedup high-water, and the history replays deterministically. Plain Session::apply_input and Driver::drive_input remain available for uncontended single-writer callers. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-engine --test suite -- control_handoff::` |
| `term.fold.scroll-region` | The fold honours DECSTBM: text scrolls within the region [top,bottom] while rows outside it are untouched, a bare CSI r resets to the full screen, a grow or shrink resize clears the region, RI scrolls the region down at its top margin, and IL/DL act only on a cursor inside the region. The typed screen-op projection stays lossless through a scroll region (emit_ops then apply_ops reproduces a byte-equal screen). A dependency-free generator of 300 random region programs asserts that rows outside the region never move and in-region content shifts by the scroll count. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-term --test suite -- scroll_region::` |
| `term.fold.lr-margins` | The fold models DECLRMM (?69) and DECSLRM as xterm does: under the mode CSI Pl;Pr s sets the left/right margins and homes the cursor (a left of 0 is 1; a right absent, 0 or past the screen is the last column; a pair without left < right changes nothing) and is no cursor save, resetting the mode restores the full width and leaves the cursor, and RIS and a resize reset the margins. A glyph wraps at the right margin onto the left one (at the screen's edge from past it), CR, CUB/BS, CUF and TAB stop at the margins unless the cursor is already beyond them, IRM/ICH/DCH shift only up to the right margin, and LF/IND/NEL/RI, SU/SD, IL/DL and a wrap scroll only the rectangle between the margins; from outside the margins an index or reverse index is a plain cursor motion and ICH/DCH/IL/DL change nothing, a pending wrap included. Origin mode counts CUP/CHA columns from the left margin and stops them at the right one, DECSC/DECRC round-trip a wrap pending on the right margin, and that state serializes apart from a cursor standing one column on. Each fixed case is reasoned from xterm's source; a dependency-free generator of 300 programs confined by origin mode asserts the cells beside the margins never change and the cursor never leaves them (negative control: without the margins at least 200 of the same programs reach those cells), and 400 margin-rich programs keep the margins a valid pair, the cursor's one spelling of a pending wrap, and a lossless typed screen-op projection (SetLeftRightMargins) record by record. One deliberate difference: TAB from past the right margin runs to the screen's edge, where xterm pulls the cursor back. Cross-validated against the production aterm engine by aterm's differential VT oracle, which this command does not run. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-term --test suite -- lr_margins::` |
| `term.fold.wide-glyphs` | The fold gives a character Unicode 16's East Asian Width calls Wide or Fullwidth two cells, a lead holding it and a spacer after it carrying the same rendition (char_display_width, which measures each codepoint alone as aterm does: the regional indicators are wide too, and no zero-width code point is, so U+302A..U+302D, U+3099/U+309A and U+16FE4, marks East Asian Width calls Wide, take no column, as in xterm's wcwidth), and the cursor moves two; a wide glyph ending on the line's last column leaves the wrap pending on its spacer, and one with a single column left before the line end (the right margin, or the screen's edge from past it) wraps first and blanks that column with the bce blank, as aterm, Ghostty and Alacritty do (xterm leaves it as it was); on a screen one column wide it prints as a narrow space in the pen, as aterm and Ghostty print it. No operation leaves half a wide glyph: a write over either half, ECH/EL/ED that cut one, ICH and DCH at the cursor, at the end of the deleted run and at the right margin, and IRM blank both of its cells with the bce blank (xterm's DamagedCells repair, Ghostty's splitCellBoundary); a scroll, IL or DL between left/right margins first turns a glyph straddling a margin into two narrow blanks that keep their renditions and drop its marks (Ghostty's rowWillBeShifted; xterm leaves the halves torn); a narrowing resize that cuts a spacer off blanks its lead; a combining mark after a wide glyph rides its lead; the wide state serializes in a trailer written only when a cell holds half a wide glyph, so every narrow screen keeps its bytes. Each fixed case is reasoned from xterm's and Ghostty's source with a negative control where a glyph stays whole; 800 dependency-free random programs of wide and narrow text, motions, ICH/DCH/ECH/EL/ED, margins, IRM, scrolls and resizes never leave a lead without its spacer or a spacer without its lead after any record and re-apply losslessly as typed screen ops (negative control: at least 150 records start with the cursor on a spacer and at least 25 with a glyph straddling a margin). Cross-validated against the production aterm engine by aterm's differential VT oracle, which this command does not run. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-term --test suite -- wide::` |
| `effects.cooperative.record-replay` | A program written once against astream-effects::EffectSeam consumes, in fixed order, one real wall-clock nanosecond, two random words, and one real file's bytes; each live value is recorded as a typed EffectRecord on a separate in-memory EffectsLog, and replay from the tape alone yields a byte-identical digest. ReplaySeam holds no real backend, so replay consumes no OS effect, and a short or wrong-kind tape is refused with a typed ReplayDivergence (Exhausted, KindMismatch) rather than answered. The digest equals an independent inline oracle, the two random words differ, and the fold is order-sensitive, so a dropped, wrong, or transposed value is observable. A failed file read is taped as FileErr{path, ErrorKind} and replays as that error even after the file is created; a diverged path is refused (PathDiverged). The RNG's OS seeding is not asserted. Seam-cooperative programs only (see effects.foreign-process.record-replay). | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-effects --test suite -- cooperative_replay::` |
| `effects.cooperative.exchange-door` | A seam-cooperative program also goes through the doors whose live value it hands in as a closure: astream-effects::EffectSeam::exchange (the socket-reply door, taped as one Exchange{door, request, reply}), emit (an output, taped and compared in replay), raised (a sticky flag: only the first true read is taped, as Raised{door, false_reads} counting that door's false reads since the previous record) and mono_nanos (a Mono record, never interchangeable with the wall Clock). A demo program that goes through every door once -- its request built from the random word and the file it read, then four back-to-back flag reads -- is recorded over the real OS clocks, rng and a real file, and replays from the tape alone, with live closures that panic if called, to a digest equal to an independent oracle over the taped values; moving the flag's rise by one read, dropping it, or changing one reply byte moves the oracle.
