A publish refused before it is written (over the frame cap) owes nothing and leaves the client in step; a write that fails owes nothing and latches the client (BrokenPipe, never AcksOwed); a read that fails part-way through the owed acks (the stream closes after the first of two) latches the client and zeroes the count, so a loop that reads acks while owed_acks is above zero ends on the failure instead of spinning on BrokenPipe, and a blocking publish whose own ack read fails owes nothing; recv_publish_ack with nothing owed is InvalidInput and reads nothing. client::drain asks its committer before it takes: against a real broker, a drain whose committer owes an ack is refused with AcksOwed and takes nothing, and once the ack is read the same drain on the same group subscription returns the same two records (offsets 0 and 1), where a drain that took first would have lost them and committed past them on the retry. Nothing reads owed acks for the caller: each ack's answer (offset, dedup flag, refusal) goes to the caller that sent the publish. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --lib client::owed_acks:: && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --features cap --lib client::owed_acks::` |
| `broker.client-nonblocking-publisher` | A publisher can keep its commits in its publish pipeline and read its acks without waiting for them. Client::send_commit writes a group commit without reading its ack and owes that ack as a send_publish does (the broker answers a commit through the same in-order ack stream, a PublishAck carrying the commit record's offset); Client::commit is now the guard, send_commit and recv_publish_ack. Client::try_recv_publish_ack returns the next owed ack if the broker has already answered it and None if not yet, paying an owed ack exactly when it returns one or the owed write's refusal, and is InvalidInput, reading nothing, when nothing is owed. It is frame-atomic: the client keeps what a try read in a receive buffer, so a frame that only partly arrived is finished by the next read, a try or any blocking reply read, and none of its bytes is dropped. Against a scripted stream that gives every byte of three owed replies (an ack, a publish's refusal, a pipelined commit's ack) in its own read with a WouldBlock read before each, the tries answer exactly one None per byte of each frame and then that frame's answer, in order, and a blocking commit afterwards reads its own reply; for every split of the first ack a blocking recv_publish_ack finishes the frame a try began; a read bringing a frame and a half answers the frame and keeps the half; a stream that closes part-way through a frame a try began is UnexpectedEof and latches the client with nothing owed. The negative control is a try built on proto::read_frame (a field only these unit tests set): its read_exact drops the header byte a WouldBlock read cut off, every byte of the three owed replies is eaten as 'not yet', and its first answer is the reply queued after them, taken with no error for the first publish's ack. What does not wait is the read: the pipelined writes stay blocking writes, which the broker back-pressures once about 1,024 of a connection's acks are unresolved (PIPELINE_DEPTH), a bound a caller owing fewer than Client::MAX_PIPELINE_WINDOW never meets. A client from connect (an AnyClient, through the duplicate of its socket the Closer holds, now shared so no second descriptor is opened) or from a typed constructor (Client::connect, connect_tcp, connect_tcp_timeout, and with their features connect_tcp_sealed, connect_tcp_handshake and connect_tcp_identity) sets its socket non-blocking for the try and back to blocking after it, and counts only WouldBlock as not yet: no read timeout can fire on that socket, so a TimedOut is the connection's own ETIMEDOUT, and against a scripted stream the try returns it and latches the client with nothing owed. A client made from_stream reads as its stream reads, a WouldBlock or TimedOut read being None (against the same script, None and then the ack). Against a real broker, each case below runs on every transport connect dials -- the Unix socket aterm's bus is served on, plaintext TCP on loopback, (aead) the sealed wire, whose record layer keeps a record that partly arrived, and (handshake) the same wire behind the forward-secret handshake -- each connection an AnyClient from connect: a publish, a send_commit and a publish written with no read are owed three acks, so a head query is refused AcksOwed{3} with nothing sent, the acks come back in send order at offsets 0, 1 and 2, the head counts three records, and the group resumes past the commit's upto (its first delivery is offset 2); with the owed-ack guard off, a head query sent behind a send_commit reads the commit's PublishAck as its reply, fails InvalidData and latches the client. With the writer held after committing a publish's batch and before answering it (Broker::pause_next_acks), on those four transports and on a client from each typed constructor (the hang detector shutting down a duplicate of the client's own socket), a try returns None and a send_commit returns, each under a one-minute hang detector that would shut the connection down, the client stays in step (a head query is refused AcksOwed{1}), once the writer goes on the blocking reads wait for both acks (offsets 0 and 1) and the commit moved its group, and a burst of 64 publishes, a commit and a publish is then read by trying alone, in send order (offsets 3 to 68), the connection in step after it. The negative control of the mode switch leaves the socket non-blocking after the try, on the same transports and constructors: the blocking read that must wait for the held ack answers WouldBlock at once and latches the client, which also shows the mode the try set reached the socket the stream reads. Over a Unix socket pair whose far end wrote two acks and shut down, the tries switch the socket's mode, answer both acks, then report the close as UnexpectedEof. Exercised on the host that runs it (macOS in this repository); the mode switch through a duplicated socket on Windows is not exercised. Not built: an ack reader on another thread, and a broker that refuses everything after a refused batch (DESIGN-aterm-fabric.md section 14, open question 7). | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --lib client::ack_split:: && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --features identity --lib client::ack_split::` |
| `broker.subject-budget-read` | A producer can read the distinct-subject budget it is refused by. Request::Budget{producer_id} (Client::subject_budget) appends nothing and answers Budget{subjects, reserved, max}: the last-value index entries charged to that producer, the subjects its registered wills reserve, and MAX_SUBJECTS_PER_PRODUCER. Over a real broker: a producer holding 4096 subjects reads 4096 of 4096 (remaining 0) before its 4097th is refused and after an identical request lands as an update of a subject another producer introduced, which spent nothing, while the introducing producer's count rises from 1 to 2. On a guarded broker the answer goes only to a ring that could publish as that producer: a grant bound to it reads its own count (1 after one publish) and an unbound grant reads anyone's, while a grant bound to another principal, a read-only grant and an unattached connection are refused ERR_UNAUTHORIZED, a whole reply after which the connection is answered as before. Against a scripted peer playing a broker that predates the verb (its ERR_REFUSED malformed request), the client answers None and stays in step. The request and reply round-trip through the codec with distinct field values, under tag numbers the protocol budget pins. The count is per log: a sharded broker's shards are not summed, and nothing here gives budget back. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --test suite -- last_value:: && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --features cap --test suite -- cap_keyring::a_producers_subject_budget && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --lib proto` |
| `broker.subject-retire` | A producer can retire an exact subject it is charged for from the last-value index and reclaim its distinct-subject slot, both unconditioned (Client::retire_subject) and conditioned on the subject's latest offset and log identity (Request::Retire{producer_id, subject, offset, log}, Client::retire, announced in NonceExt tag 0x03 as REQUEST_RETIRE and read by Client::supports_retire). Only an exact Subject is accepted (a wildcard filter is refused ERR_BAD_SUBJECT); a retire on a replica is ERR_REFUSED, and on an uncarried retention-cut log (retain_before) is ERR_RECORD_REFUSED (is_retire_cut), whereas state-carrying compaction (retain_before_carrying_state) carries last_owner (carried_charges) in <log>.state so retirement and max_subjects_per_producer accounting remain exact across compaction and reopen. On an un-cut or carried-compacted leader log, conditional Retire refuses a mismatched log identity, an absent subject at offset >= head, or a present subject at offset > cur_off with ERR_RETIRE_REFUSED (is_retire_log); when offset == cur_off it requires the charged producer (an updater that did not introduce the Last entry is refused is_retire_charged) and no live or durable will that can still append targeting the subject (refused is_retire_will), then appends a marked /a/retire record (COMMIT_TAG_RETIRE = 2, RETIRE_PRODUCER_SEQ = u64::MAX, rejected by older record and replicate decoders), credits the producer's subject budget, and records owned retirement history so Last and moot retries gate retirement on the visible head passing the retire offset; when subject is already absent and offset < head, or superseded at cur_off > offset, conditional Retire answers PublishAck{offset, duplicate: true} (moot) without appending a record or retiring the superseding value. The previous writer's unmarked /a/retire header (commit_tag = 0, producer_seq = 0, no group commit) remains a reserved retirement command in both live replication and replay; ordinary publishes refuse /a/retire, and hidden replication is refused on a nonempty owned log. Other unmarked /a/retire records stay ordinary visible legacy data. Pre-reservation data or data admitted by the intervening build with the identical seq-zero header cannot be distinguished from that old command; preserving the old writer's acknowledged retirement semantics is the explicit compatibility policy. Retirement survives reopen and state-carrying compaction. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --features "cap retention" --test suite -- cap_hardening::client_retire_subject_enforces_cap_and_reclaims_last_over_wire cap_hardening::client_retire_narrowed_rules cap_hardening::client_conditional_retire_verb_cas_moot_supersede_and_nonce_ext && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --lib -- store::tests::staged_retire_uses_the_preceding_subject_charge_and_refuses_invalid_targets store::tests::marked_vs_unmarked_retire_record_and_pending_retire_visible_head_gating && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --features cap --lib -- broker::tests::a_marked_retire_needs_replication_authority_but_advances_no_group` |
| `broker.protocol-mismatch-named` | Two ends of different astream protocols are told so, on both sides. A request whose version byte is not the broker's PROTO_VERSION -- one older and one newer, as the first frame and pipelined ahead of another, over Unix and TCP, and on a guarded broker before any attach -- is answered with exactly one Error of code ERR_PROTOCOL (9) in the refusal layout every version keeps, stamped with the PEER's version byte and carrying the broker's version in the byte after the message, and then the broker closes; a frame in the broker's own protocol that does not decode is still the in-protocol ERR_REFUSED and the connection stays usable. The client reads a reply stamped with another version (all a broker that predates the refusal sends) or that refusal as a ProtocolMismatch{broker, client} inside an io::Error of kind Unsupported (protocol_mismatch reads it back; it is not a BrokerRefusal), on a request and on a subscription, and is out of step after it, sending nothing more; an ordinary refusal in its own protocol leaves it in step. The frozen layout is pinned byte by byte and read back from the refused peer's side. This build holds only its own protocol's codec, so no peer of another protocol is run: the other end is a raw frame or a scripted stream. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --features cap --test suite -- protocol_mismatch:: && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --lib proto` |
| `broker.request-keepalive` | A request/reply connection that keeps alive is one the broker can tell is dead. From its first Keepalive (answered with a Mark, appending nothing) a TCP connection is held to the broker's keepalive deadline (KEEPALIVE_DEADLINE, 60 s by default; Broker::set_keepalive_deadline), and a connection silent past it is ended as a closed one is: through a TCP relay frozen without a FIN, the connection that kept alive is ended and its will fires -- the first will on the log and the only one -- while a TCP connection that never kept alive and a Unix-socket connection that did, both idle since before it went quiet, are not ended and still answer. Client::keepalive_if_idle sends only on a connection that has sent nothing for the interval (against a scripted stream); against a broker that predates the verb (its ERR_REFUSED malformed request, scripted) keepalive is false, the client stays in step, and nothing more is sent. On a guarded broker a keepalive before any attach is ERR_UNAUTHORIZED, a whole reply, and the same connection keeps alive once a grant is attached. The test deadline is 200 ms and no latency is measured. Not asserted: that a connection which keeps talking is never cut (each read re-arms the deadline), and a real packet loss rather than the frozen relay. A client that never keeps alive is noticed only at the next broker restart. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --features cap --test suite -- keepalive::` |
| `broker.barrier-count-by-last` | An acknowledged broadcast is a count over the log, not a broker primitive: the barrier's name is the offset B in its PublishAck, a member's answer is an ordinary record on /f/F/pub/<node>/<sid>/ack/<B>, and the issuer counts one Last query over /f/F/pub/*/*/ack/<B>. With five members, one pre-publishing an ack below B and four answering (one `refused`), the rows at offsets >= B are exactly the four answerers, and counting without the offset test gives a different set; a re-sent ack dedups to its original offset; a query before any answer returns an empty page at once and the connection stays usable. A pattern, not a primitive: an absent member may be busy, crashed or nonexistent, presence rows can be stale, the broker is unguarded, and Client::last drops the resume cursor, so a fleet past the Last bounds must page with Client::last_page (not exercised). | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --test suite -- barrier::` |
| `broker.proto.tag-budget` | A codec-level budget: this unit test pins nineteen request tags (0x01-0x13: Publish, Subscribe, ForkSubscribe, Commit, ProcessAndProduce, SubscribeGroup, Attach, Replicate, Last, Fetch, Will, Hello, Keepalive, IdleMarks, Budget, Retire, GapNotices, BrokerTimes, Stats) and eight response tags (0x81-0x88: PublishAck, Delivery, Error, Mark, Nonce, Budget, Gap, Stats) to their variants, each round-tripping through encode and decode, and sweeps every byte 0..=255 against a zero-filled body to assert the decodable set is exactly those twenty-seven. It pins PROTO_VERSION 2 and BREC_VERSION 2, and asserts that every tag under the wrong version byte decodes to nothing and that a Mark cut short before `resume` is malformed, not an empty resume. It proves codec rejection only: no broker is started and no Error frame asserted, so it says what the wire admits, not what a broker answers. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --lib proto` |
| `aead.seal-open.authenticated` | astream-aead seals messages under a 32-byte pre-shared key with XChaCha20-Poly1305 (ring's ChaCha20-Poly1305 under an in-crate HChaCha20 subkey, draft-irtf-cfrg-xchacha-03 section 2.3) and random 192-bit nonces. Seal/open round-trips; two seals of one plaintext differ; any flipped byte, a wrong key or AAD, or truncation fails to open, and a failed open leaves no plaintext in the buffer (ring zeroes the span it decrypted; nonce and tag stay as they were); the draft-irtf-cfrg-xchacha A.3.1 vector pins ciphertext and tag byte-for-byte, and its section 2.2.1 vector pins HChaCha20, every bit of whose key and input reaches the subkey. The SealedStream record layer, over loopback sockets, binds each record's AAD to both hellos, direction and sequence, so cross-connection, reflected, reordered, replayed or dropped records and a wrong key are refused, poisoning the stream, and zeroizes both the 32-byte session key and the plaintext receive buffer on drop. A frame longer than a record splits into bounded records and reassembles byte-identically (a 16 MiB one crosses a real sealed socket in broker.sealed-tcp-roundtrip); out-of-range length prefixes are rejected before any body is read; a timed-out read resumes at the record boundary; try_clone shares both sequence counters. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-aead` |
| `aead.wire-identical-to-rustcrypto` | The ring-backed XChaCha20-Poly1305 is the wire RustCrypto's chacha20poly1305 0.10.1 sealed before it (astream 7066fab), held to answers recorded from that build by a scratch crate outside the workspace (crates/astream-aead/tests/fixtures): 49 seal_with_nonce outputs over four keys and four nonces, AAD lengths 0 to 300 and plaintext lengths 0, 1, 15, 16, 17, 63, 64, 65, 255, 256, 4096, 4097 and 16389 come back byte for byte and open, and a flipped bit in either half of the nonce, the tag, the ciphertext's ends, the AAD or the key fails every one; 17 HChaCha20 subkeys (RustCrypto chacha20 0.9.1's hchacha over the same keys and nonces, and the draft's section 2.2.1) match; a recorded PSK SealedStream session opens in both directions and refuses a flipped tag bit, a changed hello and the wrong role. A log sealed at rest by the recorded build (record payloads under their log index, the anti-rollback watermark and the compacted state) reads back as that build read it, and the wrong key is refused with its files untouched. When the switch landed, a wrong HChaCha20 constant, rotation or round count and a wrong ChaCha20 nonce layout were each made and reverted, and each failed these tests; this command does not re-run those mutants. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-aead --lib hchacha && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-aead --test recorded && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --features anti-rollback,retention --test suite -- at_rest_fixture::` |
| `broker.sealed-tcp-roundtrip` | Broker::serve_tcp_sealed and Client::connect_tcp_sealed run the same exactly-once Frame protocol inside the XChaCha20-Poly1305 SealedStream under a pre-shared key. Over real sockets: sealed publish -> subscribe is ordered exactly-once, resume is gapless, and re-sends dedup; one connection that publishes then subscribes receives both acks and deliveries through its cloned handles; a record at the store's payload cap round-trips byte-identically (one byte more is refused); a wrong-key client fails at connect, and a keyless peer sending a forged confirm gets only the broker's hello and confirm before the connection closes. A silent peer is dropped at the pre-authentication deadline (asserted between 4 s and 12 s), and with 64 peers held in handshake the next connection is dropped at once. Off by default (the `aead` feature); AAD binding is aead.seal-open.authenticated. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --features aead --test suite -- sealed_transport::` |
| `broker.any-connection-closer` | `connect(&Transport, endpoint, timeout) -> (AnyClient, Closer)` is one transport-erased connection over Unix, plain TCP, the sealed and the handshake wires; a wire the build lacks is refused as Unsupported naming the feature (asserted in a default build, not by this command). Over real sockets: publish/fetch round-trips with dedup on all four transports; a close through the Closer (a duplicate of the socket under any record layer) ends a reader thread's pending Subscription::recv promptly with Ok(None), and a second close is a no-op; Closer read/write timeouts turn a silent or non-reading peer into a timeout error; a connect timeout bounds the sealed and handshake handshakes; connect_tcp_timeout tries each resolved address; a zero timeout is InvalidInput; Debug of a keyed transport redacts the key. Name resolution and Unix connects are not bounded, and a timed-out request/reply leaves the connection out of step, so it must be dropped. That the recv was already parked in its read when the close landed, and that a plain TCP connect to a silent peer is bounded, are observed only on Linux: broker.any-connection-closer.linux. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --features handshake --test suite -- any_connection::` |
| `broker.any-connection-closer.linux` | On Linux, the two halves of broker.any-connection-closer that no other host observes. A connect timeout bounds a plain TCP connect to a peer that never answers: with a listener's accept queue filled by raw connects, Linux drops further SYNs (no RST, under the default net.ipv4.tcp_abort_on_overflow = 0), and connect(&Transport::Tcp, ..) fails as TimedOut no sooner than the bound and within a fixed slack of it. And before each Closer close, on all four transports, the kernel reports the reader thread asleep (its /proc task state), so the close unparks a blocked read rather than landing before the read began. Linux-gated: astream's only gate is `make ci` on macOS, which reports this claim skipped, never verified, and nothing runs it on Linux. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --features handshake --test suite -- any_connection::a_connect_timeout_bounds_a_connect_the_peer_never_answers any_connection::the_closer_unparks_a_blocked_recv` (re-run on linux only; skipped elsewhere) |
| `aead.handshake.forward-secret-key-agreement` | The handshake derives a fresh per-session key from an ephemeral X25519 exchange authenticated by the pre-shared key (NNpsk: key = HKDF-SHA256(salt=PSK, ikm=X25519(ephemerals), info=label \|\| client_pub \|\| server_pub)), discards the ephemeral secrets, and zeroizes its PRK, while the HMAC under it zeroizes the key blocks and pads it derives from the PSK and the PRK, so a later PSK compromise cannot decrypt past traffic. HMAC-SHA256 and HKDF-SHA256 are astream-digest's, through its wiping door (pinned there to RFC 4231 and RFC 5869 A.1-A.3: digest.hmac-one-construction); the session key recorded over the sha2 crate it replaced is still derived here, which pins the info layout (label, then the two ephemerals in role order) only this crate builds, and another PSK is another key; only raw X25519 is delegated (x25519-dalek). The transcript is role-ordered so a reflected message derives a different key, and a low-order DH point is rejected. A matching PSK agrees and talks over a real socket pair; a mismatched PSK cannot establish a working session. Off by default (the `handshake` feature). | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-aead --features handshake` |
| `broker.handshake-tcp-roundtrip` | Broker::serve_tcp_handshake and Client::connect_tcp_handshake run the PSK-authenticated ephemeral X25519 key agreement before the same exactly-once Frame protocol, each connection deriving a fresh session key for the SealedStream. Over real sockets: publish -> subscribe is ordered exactly-once, resume is gapless, and re-sends dedup, each over a distinct session; a wrong-PSK peer is refused while a correctly keyed client is unharmed; a plaintext peer is refused (bad handshake magic). The handshake runs off the acceptor thread under a per-connection deadline, so a normal client handshakes and publishes while a silent peer is parked. Off by default (the `handshake` feature). | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --features handshake --test suite -- handshake_transport::` |
| `aead.roam.zero-gap-migration` | Zero-gap AEAD-over-UDP connection-ID migration (`AROM` v1: `RoamListener`, `RoamStream`, `RoamHandle`, `RoamCloser`, `client_roam_connect`), backed by Clean-Origin (`I5`) `TlaRoamMigration` (`roam_amp_budget_clamp`, `roam_epoch_advance`, `roam_amp_allows_send`, `roam_contig_offset_matches`). Combines stateless HMAC-SHA256 `PKT_RETRY` source-address cookies (`< 0.35x`, zero pre-auth server state), forward-secret ephemeral X25519 + PSK session derivation, XChaCha20-Poly1305 packet sealing with a 64-packet sliding anti-replay window, RFC 9000 §8.2 `PATH_CHALLENGE` / `PATH_RESPONSE` return-routability + 3x anti-amplification budget on unvalidated candidate paths, and ordered chunk retransmission (`AROM` v1 is astream's lightweight UDP wire, not wire-interoperable IETF RFC 9000 / TLS 1.3 QUIC). Tested over real UDP sockets across multiple live path migrations, blackhole recovery, wrong-PSK rejection, and spoofed-CID / replayed-packet silent drops. Off by default (the `handshake` feature). | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-aead --features handshake -- roam::` |
| `broker.roam-udp-migration` | Broker::serve_udp_roaming, Client::connect_udp_roaming, RoamHandle::migrate / migrate_socket, and `asb serve --listen roam:<host:port> --key-file ...` run the exact Frame protocol over migrating UDP Connection-ID sessions (`AROM` v1). Tested over real UDP sockets and `asb serve` subprocesses: blackholing and migrating both publisher and subscriber across multiple ephemeral UDP sockets preserves open `Subscription`s with zero gap and zero duplication, resolves in-flight pipelined publish acks in order without reconnecting or re-attaching capabilities, suppresses false Last Will firing during migration while firing the registered Last Will on genuine session close, and silently drops off-path spoofed-CID floods without disrupting live traffic. Off by default (the `handshake` feature). | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --features handshake,cap --test suite -- roaming_transport::` |
| `aead.identity.mutual-signed-dh` | Authentication by long-term Ed25519 identity keys with no shared secret, via a mutual signed Diffie-Hellman handshake: the client pins the broker's host key and the broker allow-lists client identities. Identity keys only sign the ephemeral transcript and the session key comes from the ephemeral X25519 DH alone, so forward secrecy survives an identity-key compromise, and IdentityKeypair scrubs its 32-byte seed and SigningKey on drop. Each side signs its view of both ephemeral keys under a role-tagged context (defeating ephemeral substitution and reflection), checked with verify_strict before any trust decision. Over a real socket pair, mutual auth succeeds and the server learns the client id; a wrong pinned host key, a client not on the allow-list, and tampered or role-swapped signatures are refused. Only raw Ed25519 is delegated (ed25519-dalek). Off by default. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-aead --features identity` |
| `broker.identity-tcp-roundtrip` | Broker::serve_tcp_identity presents a host identity and allow-lists client identities; Client::connect_tcp_identity pins the host key and presents its own. After the mutual signed-DH handshake the same exactly-once Frame protocol runs, forward-secret per connection. Over real sockets: publish -> subscribe is ordered exactly-once with gapless resume and dedup; a client pinning the wrong host key, a client not on the allow-list, and a plaintext peer are refused. The handshake runs off the acceptor thread under a per-connection deadline. Off by default (the `identity` feature). | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --features identity --test suite -- identity_transport::` |
| `aead.cert.external-authority-and-ssh-agent` | Rung-9 external identity authority (`CertificateAuthority`, `IdentityCertificate`, `CertRole::{Host, Client}`), multi-root CA trust store and monotonic KRL revocation (`CertTrustStore`, `SharedCertTrust`), zero-downtime overlapping host-key rollover (`HostCertRotator`), out-of-process OpenSSH `SSH_AUTH_SOCK` Ed25519 signing (`SshAgentSigner`, `IdentitySigner`, `SshAgentServer`, `ssh_agent_list_over`, `ssh_agent_sign_over`), and mutual certificate-authenticated SIGMA (`client_cert_handshake`, `server_cert_handshake`), backed by Clean-Origin (`I5`) `TlaCertAuthority` (`cert_validity_clamp`, `cert_epoch_advance`, `cert_horizon_reached`, `cert_wire_version_valid`). Ephemeral X25519 alone keys `SealedStream` (forward-secret under CA, host, and client key compromise), while M3/M4 bind role-tagged ephemeral transcripts to SHA-256 certificate digests. Tested over real TCP and Unix `ssh-agent` sockets: multi-algorithm agent filtering, strict Ed25519 signature verification on agent replies, overlapping host-key rotation, multi-root CA rollover, KRL epoch rollback refusal, and refusal of forged, expired, revoked, wrong-role, and wrong-principal certificates. Off by default (the `identity` feature). | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-aead --features identity -- cert::` |
| `broker.certified-tcp-roundtrip` | Broker::serve_tcp_certified, Client::connect_tcp_certified / connect_tcp_certified_verified, FollowerTransport::Certified, and `asb serve --listen certified:<host:port> --host-cert-file ... --ca-key-file ... (--host-key-file ... \| --ssh-agent-sock ...)` bind broker, client, and Replicated follower links to external CA-signed `ACRT` certificates and `SSH_AUTH_SOCK` agent custody. On guarded brokers, capabilities naming `p=<principal>` must match the authenticated client certificate principal (closing cross-worker capability impersonation across `h-*`, `a-*`, `n-*`, `s-*`), and live `Broker::revoke_cert_serial` or `serial:<u64>` revocation-file entries terminate open connections/subscriptions and suppress pending last wills. Tested end-to-end over real TCP sockets, Unix `ssh-agent` sockets, Replicated quorum followers, and `asb serve` subprocesses. Off by default (the `identity` feature). | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --features identity,cap --test suite -- certified_transport::` |
| `broker.log-encrypted-at-rest` | Broker::open_encrypted / Broker::open_encrypted_bound / Broker::open_guarded_encrypted* / OpenOptions::at_rest / ShardedBroker::open_opts / `asb serve --at-rest-key-file [--anti-rollback]` seal each record's payload and every sidecar (`<log>.state`, `<log>.held`, `<log>.hw`) on disk with XChaCha20-Poly1305 (astream-aead), using a fresh random nonce per frame and v2 `<log>.seal` (`ASLB`, 256-bit CSPRNG `seal_id` written via 0600 tmp+fsync+rename) domain-separated log-binding AAD (`ASTREAM-ATREST-V2\0` \|\| `seal_id` \|\| domain_tag \|\| le64(index/seq), governed by Clean-origin `TlaAtRestLogBinding` / `at_rest_version_advance`). Tested: bodies and subjects are absent from the raw file; reopening with the right key recovers everything in order with dedup intact; a wrong key is refused as a corrupt tail, never silently truncated; cross-log record splicing and cross-log `<log>.state`, `<log>.held`, and `<log>.hw` sidecar swaps between two logs sharing the same 32-byte key are caught by the AEAD tag; guarded + encrypted brokers (`open_guarded_encrypted*`, `asb serve --secret-file ... --at-rest-key-file ... --anti-rollback`) enforce capability proofs and seal records on disk simultaneously; `open_encrypted` reads committed legacy v1 unbound fixtures (before `<log>.seal`) while keeping v1 AAD so mixed-AAD compaction/appends never corrupt existing logs, whereas `open_encrypted_bound` / `open_guarded_encrypted_bound` strictly require `<log>.seal` on non-empty logs. Repair is keyed: BrokerLog::open_encrypted_repair cuts a sealed log at its first damage while keyless `asb repair` refuses a sealed log untouched. Off by default (the `at-rest` feature). Sealing hides content, not structure: record count, sizes and timing stay visible, and tail-truncation detection requires `anti-rollback` (broker.log-anti-rollback). | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --all-features --test suite -- at_rest::` |
| `broker.log-anti-rollback` | Broker::open_encrypted_verified keeps an authenticated monotonic watermark sidecar (`<log>.hw`, sealed under the at-rest key with a domain-separated AAD, holding the head offset and a per-log identity taken from the first record's tag), rewritten via tmp+rename after every durable commit and fail-closed (a failed watermark write poisons the log until reopen), so no batch is acked without its head covered; the first commit binds the watermark to the log's identity before its first record lands, so a crash there leaves a log a verified open accepts; on open, a head below the watermark is refused as a rollback. Tested over real files: a truncated log, a same-key watermark from another log, a deleted watermark on a non-empty log, and a tampered watermark are each refused; a normal reopen recovers and advances it. The watermark is Strict-only: a verified open at any other durability tier is refused InvalidInput before the file is created, because a weaker tier acks heads a power loss can take back below a watermark that persisted. Off by default (the `anti-rollback` feature). It defeats a key-less attacker only: a key holder can forge a watermark, and restoring an older snapshot of this log's own (log, .hw) pair would need a hardware monotonic counter, out of scope. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --features anti-rollback --test suite -- anti_rollback:: && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --features anti-rollback,retention --lib -- watermark pre_bind && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --features anti-rollback,retention --test suite -- store_regressions::a_failed_first_watermark_write_leaves_a_log_verified_open_accepts store_regressions::a_failed_watermark_write_neither_duplicates_nor_acks_the_retry` |
| `broker.log-retention` | Broker::retain_before / BrokerLog::retain_before drops records below a floor offset while keeping surviving offsets absolute: after retain_before(3) on a 5-record log the two survivors keep offsets 3 and 4, the head is unchanged, a floor at or below the base drops nothing, and a reopen recovers the base and offsets. On an encrypted log kept records recover under their original offset AADs, and retention re-identifies the anti-rollback watermark and binds the base into it, so a tampered `<log>.base` sidecar is refused. The compaction is crash-safe and streamed: the kept suffix is copied in bounded chunks (no read holds more than a chunk and one frame) to `<log>.kept` with the log's permissions, an intent `<log>.compact` naming the old and new base and the log's old and new first frames is made durable before the rename, and an open after a crash following any durable step (the copy, the staged state, the intent, the rename, the state's install, the base, the re-identified watermark, the intent's removal) finishes the compaction or discards it by the log's first frame -- losing no acked record, reading none twice, answering the same `Last`, re-identifying an anti-rollback watermark beside the log whenever the open holds the key, verified or not (as a keyed compaction does), and leaving none of its files -- while an intent that names neither frame refuses the open with nothing touched; an in-process failure after the rename poisons the log until that reopen. The copy, the intent and the staged state are created with the log's permissions, as is the carried state; a log created where a compacting one was deleted opens again. A broker runs the copy, and its flush, with no lock held: a publish acks while it copies, the finish copies what was acked meanwhile, and the finish's flush under the locks covers only that; compactions on one broker run one at a time. The copy reads the live log by position through the log's own descriptor, shared (`compact::KeptCopy`), never through a second open of its path: on Windows that open succeeds and the open's whole-file lock (`LockFileEx` through the log's descriptor) refuses every read through it, this process's own second handle included (aterm F24's review); its reads move no cursor an append depends on, since every append is positional (`broker.positional-append`). NC: with the open log's path made write-only, the Unix stand-in for that lock, a copy read through a second open is refused and the cut fails (`compact::tests::the_copy_reads_through_the_logs_own_descriptor`); no Windows run is part of this claim's evidence. A floor that would drop a producer binding or a still-pending will is refused by plain `retain_before`, while `retain_before_carrying_state` persists pruned bindings, pending wills, consumer-group watermarks, and the latest `Last` row of quiet subjects in an authenticated `<log>.state` sidecar and restores them across restart. At the end of a log only an all-zero tail, or a frame the file ends inside of, is truncated as torn on open; a complete-sized frame that is not a record is refused with the file untouched even when zeros follow its first bytes -- a record whose suffix was zeroed, or an empty frame, which is what a torn header decodes as -- because trailing zeros do not prove the write was interrupted. On by default (the `retention` feature; a build with `--no-default-features` has none of it). Dedup keys of pruned records are dropped, so exactly-once holds only within the retention window. A crash is injected after each durable step both as the process stopping (what the kernel holds survives) and as a power loss (only flushed content survives, and of the directory changes since its last flush only a prefix: a model kept in-process over the real files, not a device simulation); a power loss inside a step, a file flushed only in part, and metadata reaching the media out of order are not simulated. When these calls run is the caller's; the broker's own retention, by rules and on a tick, is broker.retention-rules. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --features retention,anti-rollback --test suite -- retention:: --skip replicated_retention:: && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --features retention,anti-rollback --lib -- retention && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --features retention,anti-rollback --test suite -- store_regressions::fetch_and_last_value_read_the_right_records_after_retention store_regressions::retention_keeps_the_log_file_permissions && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --features retention,at-rest --lib store::tests:: && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --features retention,anti-rollback --lib -- compact:: broker::tests::a_publish_acks_while_a_compaction_copies broker::tests::compactions_on_one_broker_run_one_at_a_time` |
| `broker.retention-rules` | A broker retains its log by RULES (REQUIREMENTS R4; Broker::set_retention, `asb serve --retain-file`): each a filter, a class, a max_age and a max_bytes, plus a default rule for every subject no rule names, checked when made (at most one prefix rule and one default, a bounded count) and read from a rules file a line each. The PREFIX rule alone sets the cut, the log's new base: the oldest record of its stream it still wants by its age and bytes, that record itself, at no batch boundary; a HOLD rule never moves it. Below the cut, what a hold rule still wants (unread mail until every reader group named by its template has read past it or its age is up, a last-value subject's latest record) is carried at its absolute offset in a held store `<log>.held`, appended between compactions and rewritten once it churns past twice what it carries, sealed under the log's at-rest key when it has one; each hold class keeps its own floor and, past its own bytes or age, gives up its oldest; a class past its bytes gives up at least a quarter of them (retention::SLACK_DIV), so a log at its bounds is copied within four times the bytes it takes (NC: a class cut back to exactly its bytes is copied whole for every batch, about twenty times). R4's acceptance on a real log: the stream retained at 100 records of 250 published beside a presence record at offset 3, the oldest reads 151, a fetch from 5 is refused with that floor (NC: one that clamps), and Last of the presence subject answers its record at 3, across a restart. While one unread mail waits a week on a hand clock, a batch and a tick an hour, the retained and held bytes stay within the sum of the rules' bytes and the mail reads at its own offset until its 7 days are up (NC: a cut that is the minimum over every rule's floor keeps every record since the mail, past the bound); a hold class past its bytes gives up its oldest, logs one line naming the subject and the count, and a read below its floor is refused with it (NC: a drop that raises no floor); an age rule cuts by the hand clock; a mail is released once its reader reads it (NC: a cut that carries nothing loses it). A restart reads the held store, its floors and its rules back; a crash after any durable step of a tick's compaction, the held store's own first, as a process stop and as a power loss, plaintext and sealed under anti-rollback, reopens whole (NC: the frames of a compaction that never landed applied at the open); a torn held tail is cut and damage refuses the open naming the file; a rules change keeps each hold class whole from where it was, so a held mail still reads (NC: every class reset to the base); every record's age survives restarts, the tick's stamps durable in the held store: an age rule cuts by when a record was first seen across two restarts (NC: an open that restamps), a crash that loses stamped records never ages the records that take their offsets (NC: stamps not cut to the recovered head, or cut in memory only), and a rewrite keeps them (NC: a snapshot without them); a cut a caller asks for between ticks (retain_before_carrying_state) plans on the last tick's clock, whose stamp a cut to the head leaves, so the mail it carries is not aged at once (NC: a prune that drops every stamp at or below the base: the carried mail stamped at 0 and given up by the next tick); a log without rules keeps no held store, and one created where another was carries none of its. The broker's RETENTION TICK, a thread started by the first rules or cap set since its open (a reopened log answers reads by the rules its held store kept, and compacts by none, nor advertises any, until they are set again), takes the cut off the ack path: a batch that takes some class past the room the last planning left it, counted by each record's own class, asks it for a tick (NC: a writer that never asks leaves the oldest at 0; one woken by the log's bytes asks while a quiet class sits at its bound; one whose wake a tick that changes nothing leaves stale), rules set while a tick or a caller's compaction runs wait for it (NC: a set under the log lock alone loses a carried mail across a restart, and holds from the base before the cut), it runs every RETENTION_INTERVAL for the age rules, a tick due while a retention call compacts is skipped and counted (BrokerStats::retention_skips; NC: one that waits), a publish acks while a tick's compaction copies (NC: the batch lock held across the copy), the caps (set_max_retained_records and _bytes) run through it, a subscriber from 0 is delivered the held records and then the retained ones in offset order (NC: a tail that clamps to the base), and it starts only on a broker something retains and is joined before the writer at shutdown. Rules set while the writer is in a batch's write window, where a broker's open puts the wills it fires and where `asb serve --retain-file` and aterm set them with no flush first, wait for that batch (BrokerStats::retention_waits) instead of being refused (NC: a set without the writer's batch lock is refused `a batch is being written outside the log lock`). END TO END ON THE BROKER aterm RUNS (guarded, Ordered at a 100 ms horizon and Relaxed, with aterm's escalation table and its four rules at scaled bytes, every cut the tick's on a hand clock; the table and horizon read from the fixture tests/fixtures/aterm-broker.escalate and the rules from tests/fixtures/aterm-broker.retain, a rules file parse_rules reads, which aterm-link's astream_fixture_tests holds equal to its durability::escalation, ORDERED_HORIZON and broker_rules::default_rules, so these are the rules aterm runs and a change to either side fails aterm-link until the other moves with it at aterm's next pin of astream): a person's unread mail is held for its week whoever reads, while a node's goes with the cut once its inbox group commits past it, and a fetch of that read mail answers `retained-from=` (live NC: a reader template naming the person lane's node releases the person's mail before day 6); a halt and presence rows outlive day 7 and a restart in Last (NC: a compaction that carries no Last row); a binding, a pending will and a person's inbox commit cut out of the file are restored by the reopen (NC: an open that restores no carried group commit re-delivers the read mail); retire_subject and the conditional retire of a closed session's `ev` face succeed after the tick's cut and give the subject budget back, and a plain retain_before on a log its rules retain carries the state too (live NC: an uncarried retain_before on an unretained broker gives is_retire_cut); while an escalated batch is held for its full flush the tick never cuts above the visible head (NC: the tick's ceiling taken from the log's head). A RULE MAY FORGET (Rule::forgetting, Rule::forgets, `forget` in a rules file at any place in its line; off by default; refused with `last` by Retention::new, for the default rule as for a filter's, and by the rules file naming its line, a subject's Last row being what such a rule keeps; Hello's and the held store's encoding carry it as bit 1 of a rule's keep byte, round-tripping, and refuse any other bit, so an older decoder refuses rules that forget rather than read them as keeping): once retention has given up every record of a subject it governs, each tick (one with nothing to cut included) has the writer append a marked `/a/retire` of it under the producer it is charged to, so it leaves Last and that producer's distinct-subject count live, at a reopen (the R frame reading the rules back as forgetting) and after a later cut past the retire, as a retire does, and the tick counts it (RetentionTick::forgotten, at most retention::FORGET_MAX a tick, a full one asking for another); not while one of its records is still held, a will that can still append targets it, its newest record lies at or above the base, it is hidden or under `/a/`, on a replica or after an uncarried cut (held::tests: a read lane forgotten at the first tick, a held one once it is read, a will-targeted one and one rewritten above the cut kept, a presence row kept, the budget 6, 5 then 4; NC: the same log under a rule that does not forget forgets nothing). On aterm's mesh bus rules (tests/fixtures/aterm-mesh-bus.retain, a rules file parse_rules reads: aterm's broker rules with one more before the mail rule, holding alab's mail for the person carry's group `/f/alab/cur/n-carry/node/carry` as well as the addressed inbox, and forgetting; aterm-link holds it equal to the rules its mesh bus log runs at aterm's next pin of astream), Hello advertising that rule alone as forgetting: a person's post is held below the cut until the carry has read it too, then goes with its lane (live NC: the same post under aterm's broker rules, and on fleet f1, whose mail no carry reads, goes at the first tick); an alab mail lane the week cut gave up whole leaves Last and the carry's budget, live and after a restart, the tick reporting forgotten=1 and the next 0, while the lane the carry's will targets, an F-fleet lane, a halt and a presence row stay (live NC: aterm's broker rules forget none, both lanes and both charges staying across the restart); and a lane with a record still held stays in Last, charged, until that record is read and goes, then is forgotten, across a restart (live NC: its sibling, read by both readers, forgotten at the tick that keeps it). On the wire: a Fetch below the oldest offset of its filter (the offset at and above which it is complete, the greatest of its subjects' floors) answers ERR_RECORD_REFUSED `retained-from=<off>` (BrokerRefusal::retained_from, permanent), its words `records of <filter> below offset <off> are not all kept`, instead of a page that silently starts later (NC: a Fetch that clamps; the old words), while a fetch of a held subject whose records were carried reads on; a Fetch on a connection that asked for gap notices (BrokerLog::fetch_told, Client::fetch_told) reads from its `from` the records retention still holds below that oldest, in offset order, the gap notice for the range and its count ahead of the page, refused only where its filter holds none there: n2's unread mail below n1's read one through `/f/F/in/>`, and mail held below the base through `/f/F/>` (NC: a fetch_told that refuses below the oldest; a broker that writes the notice after the page, one that tells no fetch, and one that tells every fetch, which a connection that did not ask then reads); a told read whose page holds only records at or above the oldest is still refused, and one whose bounded scan runs out below it is answered as holding and reads on to the held mail (NC: either branch of that decision inverted); Client::fetch keeps each of an asking connection's notices apart, with its filter, for Client::take_gaps (NC: one that folds them into one range), and a notice anywhere but ahead of a fetch's page is out of step (NC: a client that takes one anywhere); Hello's request set announces told fetches as bit 2 (REQUEST_TOLD_FETCH, Client::supports_told_fetch; NC: a Hello without it); and a told fetch against a broker that refuses the ask as an unknown request reads as a plain one (NC: one that propagates the refusal); a Fetch's Mark carries that oldest as an additive trailer after `durable`, so a head query (max 0, never refused) is the Head-style query (Client::oldest, seen_oldest), and an older client reads the same Mark; Hello's extension block advertises the rules the broker retains by, the default one included, as tag 0x05, and none from a reopened log whose held store kept rules until they are set again (Client::retention_rules; NC: a Hello that leaves them out, and one that advertises the rules read back). A TAIL IS TOLD WHAT IT LOST (R4 addendum A9): the log-wide read floor never moves a Subscribe or SubscribeGroup cursor, and a tail below its filter's oldest, where it starts or when a compaction lands while it reads, is sent a gap notice (Response::Gap, tag 0x87: from its cursor to that oldest, and how many of the filter's records were given up there and not counted before, where the held store's in-memory ledger names them all) before any later record: a group whose two oldest unread mails its class's bytes gave up is told 0..2, counting 2, before its first held mail (NC: a tail told nothing, and one that writes the notice after the chunk), a tail a cut passes while it reads is told before its next record (NC: one that looks only when it starts), and a parked tail before the records it wakes to (NC: a park that drops the notice its wake read), a later give-up inside a range already told is told again with its own count and never a record twice (NC: no count of what was given up since its last look), a count is unknown, never a guess, for the prefix rule's range, after a restart, and after a compaction no rules planned, which also raises every hold class's oldest to its cut and writes that as the held store's spill floor before its copy, so a reopen still tells it, and a crash before the cut landed cuts that frame away (NC: a count that skips its checks; a planning that ledgers nothing; a cut that leaves the ledger whole, or the floors where they were; one that raises the spill in memory only, by retain_before and by the caps), the ledger is bounded as the floors are (NC: one that never drops); a give-up the ledger can no longer count, inside a range a counted notice told, is told by the range again (NC: a range told again only when it grows; a ledger that takes a planning's give-ups as if it were whole below them), and a look that tells nothing leaves the range told where it was, so a later give-up it cannot count inside the range the floors' spill widened is still told (NC: a look that moves it); the notice is negotiated as idle marks are (Request::GapNotices, tag 0x11, answered with a Mark; Hello's request set bit 1, NonceExt::supports_gap_notices): a client that did not ask is never sent one (NC: a broker that tells every tail), and Client::subscribe against a broker that refuses the verb as an unknown request still tails (NC: a client that propagates the refusal); Subscription::recv_event returns Event::Gap, recv and take skip it, and take_gap hands back what they passed; a sharded fan-in forwards each shard's notice in its order (ShardedSubscription::recv_event's ShardedEvent::Gap, and take_gaps, shard by shard; NC: forwarders that read deliveries alone), and `asb sub`, `asb drain` and `asb fetch` write `asb: <verb>: GAP from=<F> to=<T> records=<N\|unknown>` on stderr, their stdout framing unchanged, `asb fetch` reading the held mail below its filter's oldest (NC: a sub that reads records alone, a drain that never takes the notice, a fetch that does not ask). THE PLANNING IS EXPORTED FOR A SIMULATION: in a test or debug build (`cfg(any(test, debug_assertions))`, never a cargo feature) retention::plan, its View and Plan, the held record, oldest, stamp, prune_stamps, clamp_stamps, carried_since, counted_from and the Ledger are pub, so a simulation of the broker plans every cut as its tick does (aterm's deterministic fabric simulation): planned by the export on a log's own records and a held store applied as the broker's applies a plan, each tick of a fixture under aterm's four rules (tests/fixtures/aterm-broker.retain) at small bytes, which cuts, carries, releases read mail and gives records up by age and by bytes, takes the plan's cut, carries and releases as many records, loses the same records of the same subjects, and leaves every filter's oldest and every fetch where the plan says (NC: the plan at a clock before the age rules bite is not what the later tick does). The `retention` feature is on by default; a broker given neither rules nor caps never compacts by itself. Not built: the held store is not replicated (a follower holds no `<log>.held`), and rules are set per broker, not fleet-wide. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --features anti-rollback --lib -- retention::tests:: held::tests:: broker::retention_tick_tests:: proto::tests::a_marks_oldest_trailer_is_additive_both_ways proto::tests::the_nonce_extension_block proto::tests::a_gap_notice_round_trips_and_a_short_one_is_malformed broker::tests::the_rules_are_set_past_the_wills_an_open_fires broker::tests::retention_after_a_writer_panic_fails_and_never_hangs && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --bin asb -- a_retain_file_reads_as_its_rules && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --test suite -- subscription_liveness::gap_notices_are_negotiated_and_an_older_broker_still_tails asb_regressions::sub_and_drain_say_on_stderr_what_retention_gave_up asb_regressions::fetch_reads_what_retention_still_holds_below_its_oldest_and_says_what_it_gave_up unlocked_write::set_retention_waits_for_a_batch_being_written retention_planner:: && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --features cap --test suite -- aterm_retention:: && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --test sharded -- a_shards_gap_notice_reaches_the_fan_in_before_its_records` |
| `broker.residency-tier` | A broker's retained history need not sit decoded in RAM: `OpenOptions::resident_budget` (`with_resident_budget`), `BrokerLog::set_resident_budget` / `residency` / `cold_reads`, `Broker::set_resident_budget` / `residency` / `cold_reads`, `ShardedBroker::open_opts` / `set_resident_budget` / `residency`, and `asb serve --resident-budget BYTES` bound the hot decoded window at the head of the log to `BYTES` of `resident_cost` (always keeping the newest record resident; `None` / `usize::MAX` keeps every retained record resident, the default). `BrokerLog::open_opts` applies the budget as its streaming scan folds each record (`tier::Opening`, `broker.streaming-open`): a record outside the tail window the budget keeps is summarized into the pages and dropped as it is read, and only that window's records are kept decoded and given `Arc<BrokerRecord>` handles, stopped-node failover (`failover::inspect_node_log`, `promote_replica_log`, `repair_follower_for_leader`) opens candidate and follower logs with `resident_budget = 0`, and `Tier` reclaims excess `VecDeque` ring capacity (`hot_shrink_target`) when `set_budget`, oversized pushes, `drain_front`, or `truncate` shrink the hot window below a quarter of its buffer. Every retained record keeps a 13-byte column entry, in pages of 256 (`tier::Page`, `tier::Columns`: its on-disk frame offset and broker time (`bt=`) from its page's, its subject's index among its page's interned ids, `plaintext_frame_bytes` and hidden flag; `broker.paged-tier`), read as a `tier::Summary`; older records evicted from the hot window are read back on demand via positional `pread` / `seek_read` (`BrokerLog::record_arc`) through the same CRC frame decode, index-bound at-rest open, and `BrokerRecord` parse as recovery, returning byte-identical records across single appends, group-committed batches, reopen, `at-rest` encryption, and retention cuts that shift kept frame offsets (`tier_tests`). Summary-driven retention planning (`retention::plan_over` over the tier, `retention::Planned` and `retention::plan_with` over a view of summaries) plans cuts and floors over summaries alone and calls `load(i)` only for cold records carried below the cut by a hold rule, failing closed (`cut == base`) if a carried record cannot be read back (NC: `load(i)` returning `None` refuses the cut; `plan_with_reads_only_the_cold_records_a_cut_carries_and_refuses_an_unreadable_one`), compaction prunes dropped `(producer_id, producer_seq) -> Offset` deduplication entries in RAM (`dedup_retains_from_cut`, `broker.dedup-index`) without reading or decoding dropped cold prefix frames (`cold_reads() == 0` across steady-state prefix cuts, even across damaged cold prefix payloads below the cut), and multi-tick `retain_by_rules` on a zero-budget log matches an unbounded log across `Prefix`, `Keep::Last`, and group-gated `Keep::All` rules and reopen. Summary filtering keeps `BrokerLog::stats`, wire `Stats` (`face_stats`), `any_deliverable`, selective `plan_tail_wakes`, `Fetch`, and multi-chunk `Subscribe` (`read_tail_chunk`) from reading non-matching or hidden cold frames (`cold_reads() == 0` for `Stats` and equals only the matching cold records for `Fetch` and `Subscribe`; NC: broad `/f/tier/>` reads cold frames), while `ForkSubscribe` still loads a non-matching cold `fork_at` to deliver its counterfactual replacement. If a matching cold frame on disk is damaged after open, `Fetch` stops short before the damaged offset without skipping it, and `Subscribe` and `ForkSubscribe` deliver intact earlier records and terminate with an explicit `ERR_REFUSED` naming the unreadable offset without emitting a caught-up mark, truncating a fork snapshot, or spinning on `BrokerHandle::shutdown`. `asb serve --resident-budget 0` serves cold history byte-for-byte to `asb sub` and `asb fetch` over a Unix socket and rejects malformed or misplaced `--resident-budget` flags with exit 2 before reading secret files. Each retained record's page entry and dedup key remain in memory (O(retained records): 17.29 B a record for one producer of consecutive seqs, `broker.bench.index-bytes-ceiling`) until retention compacts them. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --all-features --lib -- tier:: tier_tests:: retention::tests::plan_with failover::tests::stopped_failover_opens_logs_with_zero_resident_budget_and_repairs_tail && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --no-default-features --lib -- tier:: tier_tests:: && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --test suite -- cli_roundtrip::asb_serve_resident_budget_serves_cold_history_over_socket cli_roundtrip::asb_rejects_bad_flags_with_exit_2` |
| `broker.dedup-index` | Exactly-once ingest is answered by an exact index (`dedup::DedupIndex`) rather than a `HashMap<(u64, u64), Offset>` entry per retained record wherever a producer's keys run. A producer none of whose keys neighbour (one seq apart) keeps each one LOOSE, one `(producer_id, producer_seq) -> offset` hash entry as the map had, so a producer that publishes a key or two (`asb pub` with no `--id`) costs what the map cost. Once two of its keys neighbour, its keys are PACKED: sorted by seq in chunks of at most 1024 keys (`CHUNK`), each as maximal runs of consecutive seqs (16 B a run) and one offset per key, a `u32` from the chunk's base (`u64`s once a chunk's offsets span more than `u32::MAX`), so a producer publishing consecutive seqs costs 4 B a record and a sparse key beside its runs (an ack's `ACK_SEQ_BASE \| input`, published under the same id) 20 B. An insert inside a producer's seq order moves at most a chunk's offsets and runs (a full chunk splits in halves), and a retention cut drops a prefix of each chunk whose offsets rise in seq order and filters any other through at most a chunk's keys of scratch. It answers every question the map answered: a retained key's original offset (a re-send acked `deduped` at it, nothing appended), its absence once a retention cut dropped its record (the same `dedup_retains_from_cut` predicate), the key count (`dedup_len`, wire `Stats`), and an insert of a key already held replacing its offset, as the map's insert did (a replica whose retention kept a record its leader pruned and re-took). dedup::tests runs 400 seeded streams of consecutive, gapped, scattered, near-`u64::MAX` and publishing-and-acking producers with duplicate keys, replaced keys, head jumps past `u32::MAX`, retention cuts and opens that rebuild the index in offset order, at the shipped chunk size and at 2, 3 and 8, comparing it with the map after every step (each held key, its two neighbours, the count, the sorted entries, and a rebuilt index holding the same keys); NC: an index whose lookup forgets the last key of each run fails every seed. Counted by the test build: a producer that publishes and acks under one id moves at most 2·CHUNK entries a key, live and rebuilt in offset order; 50,000 one-key producers hold no more than the map; a cut through a producer with an ack key mid-log and a re-sent old seq builds at most 16·CHUNK B of scratch. store's model test compares the index's entries with its model after every flush, crash and reopen. Measured by `examples/broker_index_bench` (a counting global allocator around a reopen at a residency budget of 0, 128-byte bodies): 4.09 B of the 28.09 B a retained record holds for one consecutive producer, where the map's entry was 52.1 B of 118.83; 36.12 B a record for a processor that publishes and acks under one id, whose 1,006,000-record reopen took 471 and 507 ms against 38.3 and 38.9 s under D10c's first per-producer index; 147.00 B a record for one-record producers, against 267.06 under that index; a re-sent consecutive key answered in 27.6 to 32.0 ns, where the map took 138.1 to 152.9. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --lib -- dedup:: store::model_tests:: store::tests::publish_dedup_and_recover store::tests::r7_dedup_window_survives_restart_and_5000_newer_sequences store::tests::stage_replica_extends_dedups_or_refuses_by_offset tier_tests::compaction_prunes_dropped_dedup_keys` |
| `broker.paged-tier` | Every retained record costs the residency tier 13 bytes, in pages of 256 (`tier::Page`, `tier::Columns`, DESIGN-aterm-fabric §14 open question 8, part c): a page holds its first record's frame start, the broker time of its first record that has one and the `u32` interned ids of the subjects its records name (each id counted per record and freed when its last record goes), and four column vectors, indexed by a record's place as the slot vector was, hold each record's frame start and broker time as `u32`s from its page's, its plaintext-frame bytes in 30 bits beside a has-time and a hidden flag, and its subject as a `u8` index into its page's ids. A value no column holds is kept whole by the record's offset; const assertions hold the frame cap inside the 30 bits and a page's 255 contiguous frames inside a column's range. No offset is stored: record `i` is `base + i`, and a cold read is one frame, from its page's frame start and its column. Summaries (`tier::Summary`, `tier::Summaries`), filtered reads, the selective wake planning, `Stats` and the retention planning (`retention::plan_over` over `retention::Records::entries`, which the tier implements) read the pages without decoding cold frames, a walk over many records page by page (`tier::Walk`: a page found once for its records, its columns read as slices, a run of one subject named once), and `BrokerLog::stats` reads no record for its pending wills, which the log's latest-will map holds (`broker.latest-will-map`; the pages' `Tier::naming`, which passes whole a page whose ids do not name a subject, is the test build's read-back it is held to); a filtered read (`Fetch`'s `fetch_from`, a filtered tail's `read_tail_chunk`) reads its range through `Tier::passing` (aterm F11c), which asks its filter of a subject a page names at most once a page, and not again in a later page while its 256 verdict slots (`id % 256`) keep the subject's verdict, passes whole a page it reads at least as many records of as the page names subjects and wants none of, reads any other page record by record (a record's two column entries and its subject's id among the page's and, if it is not hidden, two bits), and meets each record that is unhidden and wanted, or whose summary cannot be read, as `summary` an index answers; a cut drops whole pages and their entries from the front of the columns and moves each page's first frame start. The slot vector the pages replaced (24 bytes a record, `tier_reference.rs`) stays for tests: in a test build every tier keeps one beside its pages, fed the same pushes, builds, cuts, truncations and budgets, and checks the record each push wrote and, after each build, cut and truncation, every record, the page walks, `naming` and the filtered reads' passes against the answers an index, so every broker unit test compares the two. tier_paged_tests runs 54 seeded streams over every page size from 1 to 256 records (frame starts and broker times outside their page's column range, cuts whose byte shift saturates, rebuilds and repagings) and after every step compares each record's summary, frame start, broker time and residency, the walks, the filtered reads' passes (wanting each subject alone, all but one, all and none), the counts, the subjects named and, with `retention`, the retention plan (`Tier::check_plans`) with the slot vector's; tier_tests runs 27 seeded streams against a real log, sealed at rest and not, at five residency budgets (0, 512 B, 4 KiB, 64 KiB and unbounded), on small and production pages (single publishes with re-sent keys, group-committed batches, hidden commit records, reopens whose broker times equal the live ones, retention cuts), after every step every retained record reading back equal to what was published, the pages answering as the slot vector and, with `retention`, planning as it under rules of their own, and every fourth step every `Fetch` page (one record a page over 7 records a scan, and up to 1,000 over 300) and filtered tail step (9 and 1,000 records a step, with and without a fork's `also_at`) equal to a read of one summary an index; and a log of 1,200 records of 16 to 32 KiB, sealed and not, reopened at budgets of 0 and 16 MiB, reads every record back equal to what was published, each one outside the window from the file once. NC: a page based on its second record and pages indexed one record late are caught by both checks, on a tier and on a real log, a frame start one byte off by the read-back, and a walk that keeps a subject's name across a page boundary at every page size; a pass that passes whole the first page it wants a record of (`PageMutant::DropWanted`) is caught at every page size by the slot vector, on a tier and on a real log (a stream of its own at each size), and by the reads of one summary an index on a real log. A test or debug build counts what a tier ran (`Broker::tier_tally`, read as a `TierTally`, a type every build compiles: pushes, lookups by index, walks and their rows, the pages a filtered read passed whole and the times it asked its filter of a subject, wide values; tier::tally_tests); aterm's attach bench reads it around each restart (aterm D10f). An attach at about 0.5M and 1.0M retained records pushes 24 to 77 records, and its refill's scan passes the 0.5M to 1.0M records behind the sessions' watermarks: through D10f's `Tier::scan` as 503,000 to 1,001,498 rows of 75 to 271 walks a restart, beside 32 to 172 lookups by index (aterm fabric entry -7010, astream `f623fd3`), where the per-record reads `Tier::scan` replaced looked each of them up by index (502,981 to 1,001,597 lookups, no walk: entry -7001, astream `77bddda`, kept as `park/d10f-tally-7001`). Measured by `examples/broker_index_bench` (aterm D10e, A B B A against the slot vector): 17.29 B a retained record at both 503,000 and 1,006,000 records of one consecutive producer (13.20 of the tier, 4.09 of `broker.dedup-index`), where the slot vector held 28.09; under a 16 MiB budget the open log holds 36,888,375 B at 1,006,000 records, where it held 47,750,003; a cold read's p50 at budget 0 542 ns in both arms' four runs at loads of 10.2 to 10.4 on 18 cores (708 and 709 ns against 708 and 625 at loads of 51 to 63); `BrokerLog::stats` at 1,006,000 records 41 and 42 ns, where the slot vector, reading every summary for the wills, took 676 and 659 µs, while a walk of every summary costs about 0.4 ns a record more than the slot vector's (`stats` over the page walk, two sets: 0.95 to 1.29 ms against 0.67 to 0.98). A filtered scan of all 1,006,000 records that matches none (`broker_index_bench`'s `scan=fetch`, aterm D10f, one S P W W P S set at loads of 7): 1.68 to 1.77 ns a record through `Tier::scan`, where the pages' per-record lookups took 5.11 to 5.68 and the slot vector 5.14 to 5.23; at 503,000 records (aterm F11c, one A B B A set at 1-minute loads of 9.1 to 13.8 on 18 cores), 0.013 to 0.014 ns a record through `Tier::passing`, which passes every page whole by its one subject id and reads no record (a page's cost spread over its 256 records, not one record's), where `Tier::scan` took 1.76 to 2.33, and a refill's `Fetch`es of `FETCH_SCAN_MAX` records each (`scan=refill`, eight a refill) 0.88 to 0.96 µs each at the p50 and at most 1.08, where they took 113.8 to 122.3 µs at the p50 and at most 293. aterm's attach bench process holds 43.0 MiB at about 1,020,000 retained records under its 16 MiB budget, where the slot vector's held 54.3 to 54.7 (aterm D10e). | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --all-features --lib -- tier:: tier_tests:: retention::tests:: && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --no-default-features --lib -- tier:: tier_tests::` |
| `broker.streaming-open` | A log's open reads its file through a bounded window and folds each record as it is decoded, so an open holds about the store it leaves, not the file and every record decoded (`open_scan.rs`, `OpenFold`, DESIGN-aterm-fabric §14 open question 8, aterm F16): the scan reads 4 MiB at a time (`open_scan::WINDOW`; more only for a frame longer than that, at most a header and `MAX_PAYLOAD_LEN`; never past the file's length), judges each frame as the whole-buffer scan judged it, by its header and the payload it announces, and hands each accepted record to the fold, which pushes it into the paged tier's columns and the dedup and every other index (`tier::Opening`, `IndexFold`) and keeps of the tail window the residency budget keeps, the window `Tier::from_parts` decides from the tail, where each record starts and what it charges: every record pushed stays decoded only while the budget holds them all, the first to leave the window lets them go, each record after it is let go once folded, and the window's records are read again from the file once the scan has decided it (`open_scan::decode_run`, each refused unless it is the record at the offset and of the charge the scan decided), so a log the budget holds whole is decoded once, and in a larger one no record decoded after the first left the window outlives its fold. The tier's pages and columns are allocated once, as `Tier::from_parts` allocated them at the count recovered, so they do not pass through every smaller size an allocator may keep once freed (`tier::Expect`): at once for the count a clean close recorded in the identity sidecar (no more than the file's length over a frame header; a refold at the count recovery kept), and after any other stop, whose sidecar names only what an open, a lossy record or a cut saw, once the columns hold `tier::SAMPLE` (1,024) records, for the count the file's length predicts at the bytes per record passed or the sidecar's count if more (an eighth over of what the sidecar's count does not vouch for, at least twice what was passed, at most what the bytes left hold in frames of a header each); `open_scan_tests` reopens a log killed after the run that created it, after one that reopened it and appended as many again, and after a clean close, and no open moves its columns off a block past `SAMPLE` (the reservation at the sidecar's count alone, put back by hand, moved them off every power of two from 4 to 16,384). The records of a unit that has not ended wait, up to 4 MiB of them, for the record that ends it, since recovery cuts only an unended last unit; an open whose recovery cuts below what it already folded folds the log again up to the cut. A frame that stops the scan (torn, damaged, unreadable, newer) is judged again on every byte to the end of the file, and recovery (`recover::classify`) reads those bytes, held once: the window's own buffer is handed on as them (`open_scan::tests`); the identity rules read the first frame, the frame the last open's newest record had and the newest kept by position, compaction recovery and the at-rest binding the first frame; `check_open` streams the same scan and keeps nothing. The whole-buffer open is kept in test builds as the reference (`ReadOpen::whole`, `scan_from`) and `open_scan_tests` holds the two to each other: the scan alone over 600 randomly damaged sealed and plaintext logs through windows of 1 byte to 4 MiB (its verdict, prefix, seal and floor marks, and every record at its bytes), and the open over logs the real writer wrote (re-sent keys, batches, group commits, wills, bindings, retirements, retention cuts that carry state or refuse, clean and unclean stops), sealed, plaintext and with the anti-rollback watermark verified, at budgets of 0, 512 B, 16 MiB and unbounded, each reopened after a random tear (cut short at its end, zeros appended, a torn header, garbage, a flipped byte, a lost identity sidecar) as two copies through a random window and pending bound: the refusal or the explicit repair, the records read back and the cold reads they cost, every index, the tier's pages and window, the retention plan, the identity settled and the bytes left on disk agree, and the run asserts it met cuts, refusals, repairs, refolds, and windows both held decoded and read again. NC: a scan that drops the record straddling its window's end, and a tier that keeps its window from the head (read again from the head), are each caught. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --all-features --lib -- store::open_scan_tests:: store::open_scan::tests:: tier::tests::an_open_predicts && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --lib -- store::open_scan_tests:: store::open_scan::tests:: tier::tests::an_open_predicts && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --no-default-features --lib -- store::open_scan_tests:: store::open_scan::tests:: tier::tests::an_open_predicts` |
| `broker.latest-will-map` | `BrokerLog::stats`, which `Broker::stats` and `Broker::health` run under the log lock (aterm's broker takes its health every 30 s), and the log's will queries (`pending_wills`, retention's blocker and the wills a carrying cut keeps) read no record: each producer's latest will the log holds, carried in `<log>.state` or in a retained `/a/will` record, is kept in memory (`durable_latest_wills`), seeded from the state and folded from each record by the open and by every append, and cut with the log (`cut_latest_wills`: an entry below a cut gives way to what the cut carried for its producer, or goes), so it holds what reading every retained will back held, live as after a reopen (aterm F19). The read-back stays in test builds (`latest_wills_read_back`, over `Tier::naming`) and will_map_tests holds the map to it after every step of 16 random logs written through the real writer (wills registered, replaced, fired, deduped and fenced under their own key, a few aimed at a hidden subject; publishes, batches, retirements, plain and carrying cuts, clean and unclean reopens), sealed and plain, at residency budgets of 0 and 16 MiB: the same wills at the same offsets, `stats`' pending count equal to the read-back's, and each reopen's map equal to the live log's (its settled flags where an open derives them from retained records); the run asserts it met reopens, cuts that dropped wills besides each seed's last, and plain cuts that left a producer an older will an earlier cut carried. NC: a cut that leaves the map as it was is caught on every seed. At a budget of 0, with 40 wills cold, `stats` and the will queries count no cold read where the read-back counts 40; through a broker, `Broker::stats` and `Broker::health` count none with six wills cold. Measured by `examples/broker_stats_bench` (one A B B A set at 1,006,000 records, A astream main with the bench): `stats` at budgets of 0 and 16 MiB took 0.63 to 1.04 µs with 32 fired wills retained and 20.1 to 33.5 µs with 1,000, where reading them back took 21.6 to 25.0 µs and 588.7 to 744.5 µs, 30 to 32 and 933 to 1,000 cold reads a call. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --all-features --lib -- store::will_map_tests:: tier_tests::stats_and_health_read_no_retained_will_back_at_a_budget_of_zero && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --lib -- store::will_map_tests:: tier_tests::stats_and_health_read_no_retained_will_back_at_a_budget_of_zero && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --no-default-features --lib -- store::will_map_tests:: tier_tests::stats_and_health_read_no_retained_will_back_at_a_budget_of_zero` |
