| `broker.positional-append` | Every append to a log, un-batched or a batch's written with the log's lock released, is written by position at the end the log tracks, its durable length (`storage::write_all_at`: `pwrite` on Unix, `seek_write` on Windows, each looping on a short write), never at the file cursor, which a cold read (`BrokerLog::record_arc`) and the open's scan move on Windows, where `seek_read` leaves it at the end of what it read (aterm F24). `append_tests` hands one log between a reader thread that reads every retained record back at a residency budget of 0, cold but the newest, and a writer that makes one un-batched append and one batch of 1 to 4 records a round, 24 rounds, after every step: each append, a batch's locked begin, its unlocked write and its locked end. On a real file and on the simulated device, whose reads move a cursor as Windows' do, every record reads back equal at every pass, the file is exactly the concatenation of its records' frames and its length the log's, and a reopen recovers every record. NC: the append put back at the file cursor (`BrokerLog::cursor_appends`, test builds only) fails the simulated device on every OS, with un-batched appends and with batches alone, and the test holds that it fails the real file on Windows alone, passing on Unix, where `pread` leaves the cursor; no Windows run is part of this claim's evidence. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --lib -- append_tests:: && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --all-features --lib -- append_tests:: && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --no-default-features --lib -- append_tests::` |
| `broker.bench.index-bytes-ceiling` | What a retained record costs an open log's memory, whatever its residency budget: `examples/broker_index_bench` writes a Relaxed log of 503,000 and of 1,006,000 records (one producer, consecutive seqs, one subject, 128-byte bodies, aterm's attach-bench filler), reopens each at a residency budget of 0 and counts with a counting global allocator the heap the open log holds; broker_index_bytes_per_record, the larger of the two per retained record, stays at or below 20. It reads 17.29 at both counts (the tier, 13.20 B a record: its columns' 13 and its pages' 0.20, and the dedup index's 4.09 B, its offsets and its chunks' headers, `broker.paged-tier`, `broker.dedup-index`), where the 24-byte slot vector before the pages read 28.09 (D10c) and the per-record summary and hash entry before that 118.83 (D10b, DESIGN-aterm-fabric §14 open question 8). The count is the allocator's, so no load moves it; the bench's publish, re-send and open times are reported, not gated, and its other shapes (`INDEX_SHAPE=acks`, `producers`) print no metric. | `INDEX_SHAPE=filler INDEX_RECORDS=503000,1006000 targo --unverified run --quiet --release --locked -p astream-broker --example broker_index_bench` |
| `broker.bench.open-peak-ceiling` | What a log's open holds at once, whatever the log's size: `examples/broker_index_bench` writes a Relaxed log of 503,000 and of 1,006,000 records (aterm's attach-bench filler), reopens each at a residency budget of 0 as an embedder does (`BrokerLog::open_opts` with the budget in its `OpenOptions`) and counts with a counting global allocator the most heap live at once during the open, less what was live before it; broker_open_peak_bytes_per_record, the larger of the two per retained record, stays at or below 40. The streaming open (`broker.streaming-open`) holds the store it leaves (17.29 B a record, `broker.bench.index-bytes-ceiling`) and its bounded window and pending unit beside it: 21.46 B a record at 1,006,000 (21,592,586 B), where the whole-buffer open before it read the file into one buffer and decoded every record before the tier kept its window, 495.42 (498,390,554 B); under a 16 MiB budget 43,731,740 B against 498,390,127 (aterm F16, one A B B A set against astream main `edd653b`). The count is the allocator's, so no load moves it; the open's time is reported, not gated. | `INDEX_SHAPE=filler INDEX_RECORDS=503000,1006000 targo --unverified run --quiet --release --locked -p astream-broker --example broker_index_bench` |
| `kafka.handshake-and-metadata` | astream-kafka encodes and decodes Kafka's classical and KIP-482 flexible/compact request/response framing (unsigned LEB128 varints, `COMPACT_STRING`, `COMPACT_NULLABLE_STRING`, `COMPACT_BYTES`, `COMPACT_ARRAY`, `COMPACT_RECORDS`, and `TAG_BUFFER`; Clean-origin `TlaCompactFraming`: `kafka_compact_len_plus_one`, `kafka_compact_array_cap_clamp`), Castagnoli CRC-32C (RFC 3720 / iSCSI polynomial 0x82F6_3B78, pinned to 123456789 -> 0xE306_9283), pure-Rust RecordBatch v2 across all five spec-defined Kafka codecs (`magic=2`: uncompressed `0`, RFC 1952 Gzip / RFC 1951 DEFLATE `1` with IEEE CRC-32 + ISIZE trailers, Snappy raw/Xerial framing `2`, LZ4 Framing v1.6.1 `3` with XXHash32 checksums, and RFC 8878 Zstandard `4` with 1-stream & 4-stream canonical Huffman, `Treeless` repeat Huffman, `Predefined`/`RLE`/`FSE_Compressed`/`Repeat` ANS sequences, skippable frames, and 64-bit `xxhash64` content checksums, plus zigzag varint records with headers, keys, values, timestamps, idempotent producer `(producer_id, base_sequence)` deduplication, and CRC-32C verification from attributes..end), and all 12 core Kafka client/group wire APIs across classical and flexible versions (`ApiVersions` v0..=v3, `Metadata` v0..=v9, `Produce` v3..=v9, `Fetch` v4..=v12, `ListOffsets` v1..=v6, `FindCoordinator` v0..=v3, `JoinGroup` v0..=v6, `SyncGroup` v0..=v4, `Heartbeat` v0..=v4, `LeaveGroup` v0..=v4, `OffsetCommit` v2..=v8, `OffsetFetch` v1..=v6) across multi-partition `TopicMeta` topologies, serving live TCP connections over both `KafkaStore` and `DurableKafkaStore` (`--features durable`, backed by multi-shard `astream_broker::ShardedBroker` with 1-to-1 partition-to-shard mapping and auto-compacted last-value group/offset snapshots). Tested non-circularly and over real loopback TCP sockets: multi-partition classical and KIP-482 flexible Produce/Fetch/ListOffsets with Gzip (external zlib dynamic-Huffman BTYPE=2 and concatenated multi-member streams), Snappy, LZ4, and Zstandard (external `zstd` v1.5.7 1-stream, 4-stream, and >128 KiB `Treeless` multi-block fixtures plus concatenated + skippable frames) isolate partition offsets and deduplicate idempotent retries, two-consumer `JoinGroup`/`SyncGroup`/`Heartbeat`/`OffsetCommit`/`OffsetFetch`/`LeaveGroup` rebalances fence stale generations with `ILLEGAL_GENERATION` (Clean-origin `TlaConsumerGroup`: `kafka_generation_advance`, `kafka_partition_clamp`), and `DurableKafkaStore` recovers multi-partition batches, committed group offsets, and producer dedup state across full broker and server restarts. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-kafka --all-features` |
| `broker.log-identity` | Every log names itself: BrokerLog::identity() is a LogIdentity {id, generation}, settled on every open and recorded in a `<log>.id` sidecar (a CRC frame, written tmp+fsync+rename) before the open returns; an open that cannot read or record it fails, naming it. Over real files: a reopen, growth, and a repair with nothing to cut keep it; a log deleted and recreated, its predecessor's sidecar left at the name, gets a new id; an open_repair that cuts records keeps the id and advances the generation, durably, while the refused plain open before it rewrites nothing; an older copy restored over the log, and a different log copied under its name, advance the generation; a copy that ends inside a frame below the durable floor (taken while the log was written), restored alone, opens cut to its last whole record as a shorter log, its generation moved with no lineage, never refused; a log with records and no sidecar derives its id from its bytes (the same id again for the same bytes, another once it grows), and an unreadable sidecar is replaced by a fresh id, not the one those bytes derive; retention (`retention` feature) keeps the identity across retain_before and the reopen after it, and a sidecar-less log whose every record was pruned is minted a fresh id rather than derived from its base and head alone. On the wire the answer to Hello carries it (Client::log_identity, hello_identity and seen_log_identity, which a guarded attach_all fills from its own Hello under the `cap` feature; Broker::log_identity) as an additive trailer: without one the frame is byte-identical to the older Nonce, with one it starts with those bytes, and a body cut inside it is malformed; Replicate carries the leader's the same way. A replica adopts its leader's identity once the leader's records reach its newest one: a real leader and follower over TCP agree after one replicated publish, durably across the follower's reopen; a replica holding three records keeps its own identity through the leader's echoes of the first two and takes the leader's at the third, while one that holds a different record than the leader's at offset 1 keeps its own through an echo of offset 0, the refused offset 1 and an echo of its newest record; a Replicate with no identity (an older leader) leaves the follower its own; a broker that owns its log keeps its own; a replica whose log is found rewritten (an older copy restored under it) takes a fresh id at generation 0, never its leader's id at the next generation, until the next replicated record hands the leader's back. Beside the identity the Hello answer carries an extension block (the served tier, the lineage; Client::served_durability, log_lineage): after a Relaxed broker stopped uncleanly and came back in another boot a client reads the moved generation and resumes where it continues. The sidecar is FORMAT 2 (state, served tier, boot, volume, durable floor, lineage) and FORMAT 1 still reads; the unclean-stop rules are broker.unclean-generation. LogIdentity and Lineage are astream-wire's vocabulary (astream_wire::logid, which the broker re-exports), so a reader that keeps offsets outside the log parses and compares identities without the broker: LogIdentity::parse takes exactly `<32 lowercase hex>.<decimal u64>`, a zero-padded generation included at any length whose value fits (`<id>.01` is `<id>.1`), and Display writes the canonical form back; LogIdentity::continues_from holds for exactly the pairs other than the same identity that continues answers Some for under a lineage holding every step, over every pair on two ids and the generations 0..=8 and at u64::MAX, where a version without the id rule and one admitting the same generation each disagree (negative controls); the same identity continues everywhere, u64::MAX included. No older astream binary is run, and a rollback that keeps the newest record the last open or clean close saw is not detected. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --features "cap retention" --test suite -- log_identity:: && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --lib -- logid proto::tests::identity_trailers proto::tests::the_nonce_extension_block && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-wire --lib logid` |
| `broker.log-newer-format-refused` | A log holding a record in a NEWER record format (its version byte above BREC_VERSION, inside a CRC-valid frame) is refused by name and never treated as corruption: BrokerLog::open and open_repair both return ErrorKind::Unsupported naming the offset, without the `asb repair` hint, and leave the file byte-identical, for such a record mid-log and as the last frame. Real corruption keeps its path: an older record format this build no longer reads, an offset gap followed by records, a bad CRC on the last frame, and a frame whose length runs past the end with a newer-format record intact behind it are each InvalidData (refused, the file untouched), and open_repair truncates each to the intact prefix. Out of scope: the frame header's own version byte, which its CRC does not cover, so a newer one cannot be told from damage; and no log written by a real newer build is opened. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --lib -- store::tests::a_newer_format store::tests::an_older_format` |
| `broker.log-publish-checked` | BrokerLog::publish_checked, the append aterm's Strict journals make (aterm-db's local and network_state, aterm-journal): on the Relaxed and the Strict tier, a retained producer key with a different subject or body answers Conflict carrying the original record and leaves the head where it was, across a reopen; identical bytes dedup to the original offset; a distinct local key keeps the other distributed branch. The guarantee covers the retained dedup window, and the window's edge is the negative control: once retain_before has dropped the record, the same key with different bytes appends, while the key of a record still retained still conflicts. aterm never reaches the conflict, its keys being always head+1. Lifetime idempotency is the caller's, in its own history (docs/DESIGN-application-sync.md). | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --lib checked_ingest` |
| `broker.batch-seals` | Every record the broker log writes carries a batch MARK after its last field: the last record of a batch a 5-byte SEAL (0xB5, the batch's record count as a u32 LE; the batch is records seq-count+1..=seq), every other record of the batch the one CONTINUES byte 0xB4, optionally followed by the 9-byte monotone broker-time trailer (0xB6, bt_ms as u64 LE; REQUIREMENTS R3); a record with nothing after its last field was written by a build that predates the marks. Records stay BREC v2, and RECORD_MARGIN is 56 so a sealed record carrying bt= at the cap still fits the frame inside the at-rest seal. The parser takes nothing, the continues byte, or exactly one well-formed seal (each with or without the 0xB6 bt= trailer) after the last field and refuses anything else, and an older build reads marked logs unchanged: committed fixtures (tests/fixtures) show a v2 unmarked log and FORMAT 1 sidecar written by astream 9f95697 open on this build with every record and the identity kept, and a marked log written by the first marking build (24 records through one pipelined connection, so some batches hold several) decodes frame by frame through a frozen copy of the v2 parser as it stood at 2888062 to the same records, its seals tiling the log and every other record marked as continuing. The same release's records appended above a durable floor this build recorded (a revert to a build before log identity, which never rewrites the sidecar, then the upgrade again; with and without a clean close) are all kept, nothing moved, and that release's 5-record log restored alone over a sidecar whose floor is 3 is read whole: an unmarked record is a unit of its own, never a torn batch. The classifier is unit-tested on hand-built logs above a durable floor: a hole in the newest batch is cut at the batch's start, removing written bytes; the same hole with a later sealed batch intact behind it, or below the floor, refuses; an intact unmarked record behind a hole refuses as the rules before the floor do; after an unclean Relaxed stop the longest intact prefix is kept; a file ending inside a marked batch is cut whole, moving nothing in the boot that wrote it; a write that never finished is cut moving nothing while a whole-sized damaged frame, or a whole last record under a rotted length field, moves the generation; a record frame planted inside a body (offset 0, or one far past the batch) is ignored, so the torn batch carrying it is cut, not refused; a copy ending inside a frame below the floor is cut as a shorter log while zeros or a rotted length there refuse; a CRC-valid frame that is not the next record refuses whatever the floor. With the floor unknown (no sidecar, FORMAT 1) the rules that predate it apply verbatim. A frame planted at exactly a place a record of the torn batch could hold still refuses the torn write it sits in. On a base-0 log no retention has touched, stats().retained_bytes is the file's length: at the open of either fixture and of a fresh log, after an append and at a reopen (retained_bytes_is_the_files_length_on_a_log_with_no_retention). aterm-journal builds on it: its Checkpoint hashes that physical prefix up to this number, and its live check refuses a journal whose file length differs from it (aterm's strict_checkpoint_keeps_acknowledged_prefix_and_archives_torn_suffix, live_truncation_withholds_reads_and_new_acknowledgements). | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --lib -- brecord:: recover:: && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --test suite -- brec_fixtures::` |
| `broker.unclean-generation` | A weak-tier power loss is announced, not hidden: a log whose last run served Relaxed (or Ordered, whose rule broker.ordered-tier tests) and is still recorded Open in its FORMAT 2 sidecar under another boot (kern.bootsessionuuid via astream-sys), an unnamed boot or off the boot volume, a sidecar marked Lossy, or an embedder's Prepared::Unclean moves the generation once at the next open, with lineage (generation, common) where the log can bound the loss; LogIdentity::continues reads the chain (newest 8 steps, older ones clamped), and a reader resumes at min(cursor, common) instead of forgetting. On real files with the boot stood in by Boot::Known: a same-boot reopen moves nothing and a reboot moves the generation by one with lineage at the head; a clean close (BrokerLog::close_clean, run at the last listener's shutdown and an unserved Broker's drop) keeps the identity across a reboot, and the same log dropped without it moves; Strict never moves for stopping uncleanly, Lossy moves whatever the tier; an Ordered run whose embedder acks only at its full flushes (OpenOptions::acks = Acks::FullFlush, which a broker refuses) is recorded as a Strict one and keeps its identity across a reboot after an unclean stop, while the same run acking at its publishes moves it (on the crash simulation's device, so on every platform); a replica mints a fresh id rather than move its leader's generation; an embedder verdict and astream's move it once; a torn three-record Strict batch is cut at its start with lineage there; a kill -9 that left a multi-page batch half-written (one record whole, the next cut at the page) is cut at the batch's start and moves nothing when reopened in the same boot, at either tier, while the same bytes after a reboot are announced with lineage. A move caused by a cut is recorded in the sidecar before the cut (phase 1, state Cutting, which carries the open's unclean verdict, so a verdict from a Lossy state or a one-shot prepare survives an open that dies before its cut), and the floor after the open's own fsync (phase 2). FORMAT 2 encodes and decodes, damaged sidecars read as none, FORMAT 1 reads as Closed with no floor, and the unclean verdict follows the tier, the boot and the state. Hibernation and a process crash keep the boot and move nothing where the host names its boot and the log is on the boot volume (elsewhere every unclean weak stop moves it, and a Strict cut of a half-written batch is announced); a log copied elsewhere, or restored with its sidecar, escapes. An embedder's tests stand in an unclean stop and a lineage through public doors instead of forging astream's files. Broker::arm_unclean_stop is an inert seam (always compiled, armed by nothing in astream) that stops a broker without the clean close: under it a served broker's stop and an unserved one's drop leave <log>.id (identity_path) byte for byte as the open wrote it and release the log; a reboot straight after the served run's stop moves the generation once, with lineage at the head the reboot recovered (3, where that run's open recorded 0), when the run served Relaxed, or Ordered where it is served, while one served Strict moves nothing (off Apple an Ordered request is served Strict, and its run asserts that instead); on a log of its own, the same-boot reopen after the seam, with the boot named and the log on the boot volume, moves nothing, and that reopen's unserved drop under the seam leaves its own open's sidecar for the reboot after it; the negative control, the same run without the seam, rewrites the sidecar and keeps the identity across the reboot at every tier. The stop still latches the log shut: a BrokerLog whose lock was released (the broker's stop) refuses a publish, a commit and the clean close while a successor appends, and (retention feature) a Broker kept after a stop under the seam refuses retain_before and retain_before_carrying_state, naming the release, while a successor serves the log, which then holds every record the successor acked; before that latch the same retain_before answered Ok(3) and renamed its compacted copy over the successor's log. Lineage::from_steps builds exactly the lineage a Hello answer carrying the same steps decodes to, and refuses what that decoder refuses (more than Lineage::MAX steps, generations that do not rise), over an exhaustive sweep of short step lists and rising chains past the cap; inside astream-wire, where the type lives, constructors without the cap or without the rising rule disagree with the decoder on the same sweep. The broker's own step (only an open records one) clamps older steps to the newest common, keeps the newest 8, and records no lineage for a step that does not rise. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --lib -- unclean_tests logid:: && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --features retention --test suite -- relaxed_tier:: unclean_stop:: && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-wire --lib logid` |
| `broker.crash-sim.prefix-law` | The power-loss law, checked on the REAL writer (stage + commit_batch, with its batch marks; on the weaker tiers the full flush an escalated record owes, taken through the same flush handle the broker's writer takes outside the lock) and the REAL open (recovery, the classifier, the identity rules, both sidecar phases, the tier served, the clean close) run unchanged against a simulated block device behind a test-only storage seam that covers the identity sidecar too. The device keeps durable, ISSUED and volatile blocks (16, 32 or 64 bytes): an ordering barrier (the Ordered tier's per-batch F_BARRIERFSYNC) issues the volatile blocks, with their contents then, and the file's size as a new epoch; a full flush makes everything durable; a sidecar replacement drains the issued epochs but not the volatile blocks. A power-loss image keeps every durable byte, every block of a PREFIX of the epochs, and any subset of the next epoch's blocks (the volatile ones counting as one more), with or without its size change, unpersisted space reading zeros or 0xEE; a process crash continues the device as it was, and a process killed during an append leaves a short write, the bytes before it plus a block-aligned prefix of it. Every workload (every sequence of 1-2 batches of 1-3 records, publishes and group commits, each batch escalated through a Subject rule, a PrincipalPrefix rule bound by a /a/bind staged earlier in the same batch, or a Group commit, or not, and followed by the Ordered horizon's full flush, a sync_full or nothing — every pair of those choices meeting at every pair of adjacent batches in every tier and shape, the same kind twice and none then some included (whole triples are left to the random sample) — with and without a clean close, on an empty log and on an unsealed v2 prefix with a FORMAT 1 sidecar; and 60 seeded random ones of 4-8 batches), for Strict, Ordered and Relaxed, is crashed after every device operation, the open's own included, recovered as a power loss (another boot), as a process crash and as every short write (the same boot), and a sample crashed a second time after two more batches. Acks are marked where the product sends them, by its own predicate (Committed::acks_now, the one the broker's writer answers each op by): what it acks at once at the commit, the rest once the full flush it owes is done; that the writer itself sends nothing more before the flush is broker.ordered-tier's race-free check. At each crash point the sweep takes, for each prefix of the epochs, EVERY subset of the next epoch's blocks while they fit a budget of 64 images (16 in a second crash: at most 6 blocks), and beyond it a sample: none, all, every prefix, each block alone, all but each one, and seeded random subsets; it prints how many points it enumerated and how many it sampled (in the default sweep about a third are sampled), so a loss that needs two particular blocks among many is tried only if the sample lands on it. No image violates P1 (no refusal with a known floor), P2 (a prefix of what was written, at a batch boundary for Strict and Ordered; inside an open, of either run's writes), P3 (every record a full flush covered survives, and every record below the durable head the product claimed), P3e (every escalated record acked before the loss survives, on Ordered and Relaxed; which records are escalated is the harness's own oracle, not the product's table), P4 (a lost acked record moves the identity), P5 (nothing lost or moved after a clean close), P6 (a same-boot crash, a short write included, loses and moves nothing) or P7 (a reader resuming at min(cursor, continues) skips nothing and finds what it saw); the default sweep (2.3M recoveries) loses acked records many times, two thirds of those losses Ordered's, every one with a move, mostly with lineage, and its escalated commits held the visible head for the full flush they owed 1,134 times, so it is not vacuous. Every byte of a clean multi-batch log flipped once is refused below the floor or behind a later batch and refused or cut with a move in the newest, a whole last record under a rotted length field included, keeping only records written (P8, P4, P2). An older build's unmarked records appended above the floor after a revert keep every record a flush covered and refuse no image the rules before the floor recover; workloads whose record bodies carry whole record frames hold every property, P1 above all; an open that cuts on what the next open cannot re-derive (a Lossy state, an embedder's one-shot prepare, a repair, the tier the last run served where this open asks another) and dies at any point after it first touches the device is followed by the same cut under the same identity and lineage (a plain open after a repair that died before its cut may refuse the damage, as before the repair). Negative controls each break their property: a device that ignores barriers, under Ordered (P1: a later batch completed behind a hole — while P2 holds, so the failure is a refusal, never a silent loss), the writer before the marks (P1), the escalation table emptied (P3e), power losses recovered as the same boot (P4), the floor written before the open's flush (P1), a cut at any damage (P8), the horizon's full flush issued as a barrier (P3: records below the claimed durable head lost), a Relaxed cut at the first damage without the unclean verdict (P8: rot in an older batch cut), lineage for a restored shorter copy (P7), a reader that re-bases at the head (P7), no empty-frame rule (P1); and the rules that predate the floor refuse thousands of the same images. The default run is the 2-batch exhaustive set and the random sample; this command adds the ignored full sweeps (the 3-batch set at every block size and phase, one recovery in 16 crashed twice: 11.1M recoveries; 3000 seeded random workloads: 10.5M; no violation, eight to ten minutes for both in debug on the dev Mac, by its load). Not a power-cut test: it rests on block writes being atomic and on fcntl(2)'s barrier contract, which a device that broke it would turn into refusals (the first control), not into lost records. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --lib -- crash_sim:: --include-ignored` |
| `broker.ordered-tier` | Durability::Ordered { horizon } acks a batch once it is ORDERED on the device (F_BARRIERFSYNC through astream-sys), served only on Apple with the log on the boot volume and a barrier probe that succeeds at open; anywhere else it is served Strict with the reason (not Apple, not the boot volume, the barrier refused at open), never weaker, and the tier SERVED is what <log>.id records and the Hello block reports (requested, served, reason, horizon_ms, the escalation table's size). The escalation table (Escalate::Subject, PrincipalPrefix and Group, in OpenOptions) is parsed at open, a rule that does not parse refusing the open by name before the file exists; it can be read from text (parse_escalation_file: a subject, principal or group line per rule in its order and at most one horizon=<n>ms line, each rule refused by its line number where the open would refuse it, and a word, an argument count or a horizon the grammar does not take refused too), the form the fixture tests/fixtures/aterm-broker.escalate keeps aterm's table and horizon in; and it is matched by the broker as each record is staged, so no client version decides it: a subject the Subject rule names, a producer bound by a /a/bind staged earlier in the same batch, a pure commit of a group the Group rule names; hidden subjects escape the subject and principal rules. At the log, on the crash simulation's device (so on every platform): an Ordered batch is one barrier and no full flush; a batch holding an escalated record holds the visible head at it and owes a full flush, taken through a flush handle as the broker's writer takes it outside the lock, after which the visible and durable heads reach the log's head; a resend of an escalated op whose original no full flush covers owes one with nothing staged, and its ack waits for it; Strict owes nothing (a full flush per batch) and Relaxed owes the escalated record its flush; a barrier refused while serving latches the log to Strict and flushes that batch in full; a FAILED full flush records the run Lossy, latches Strict for Hello and POISONS the open, because a later flush that succeeds says nothing about what the failed one was to cover (on a real device it has nothing dirty left to rewrite for those records and returns 0 whether or not they are on media): the escalated record stays unseen with no durable head past it, the resend that would dedup to it, every later batch, sync_full and the clean close are refused, a success reported afterwards moves nothing, and the reopen moves the generation even in the same boot; a flush failing inside a commit rolls its batch back, and poisons too only when earlier acks rode barriers (a Strict log just rolls back); a group commit a held batch carries does not move group_start until the flush releases it; sync_full is skipped when covered; a direct escalated publish is durable when it returns, and so is a direct resend of an escalated op whose original no full flush covered. On real files: the tier served on this platform, recorded so that an unclean stop of a run that served Ordered moves the generation in the next boot and one served Strict does not (that half on every platform: an Ordered request served Strict off the boot volume, stopped uncleanly, keeps its identity in another boot); Volume::Other is served Strict; the boot volume is judged by the opened file's own device (fstat), not its directory's (a file reached through /dev/fd, whose directory is devfs, is on the boot volume, and /dev/null is not). That predicate is public (on_boot_volume), for an embedder that trusts its own unflushed writes within one boot, such as aterm's incarnation lease. A directory opened as a File answers for the log written inside it, on Apple a log in /tmp is served Ordered exactly when the predicate says boot volume, and devfs's /dev is not on the boot volume. In the broker, race-free: with the writer stopped between the acks it sends at once and the full flush (Broker::pause_next_flush; ops submitted straight to the writer, each with its own ack channel), the op before the escalated record is answered and the escalated op and the one after it are not, the visible head standing at the escalated record and the durable head below it, then the flush answers them and releases the head (on Relaxed, and on Apple on Ordered); a will registration in a held batch is answered by its own commit, so when the flush then fails the producer is told the will is registered, which the log holds, while the escalated publish beside it is refused. Over a real socket: an escalated publish is durable at its ack (Broker::durable_head above its offset) and an ordinary one is not; no subscriber is delivered an escalated record the durable head does not yet cover (both read after the ack or delivery arrives, so they corroborate the race-free check above rather than replace it); an escalation costs exactly one full flush (Broker::flush_counts), and so does a resent escalation of an uncovered original; a covered flush_durable is skipped and counted; a zero horizon makes the writer flush in full on its own (a one-minute hang detector, never a budget); at a 250 ms horizon one ordinary publish followed by nothing at all is flushed in full when its horizon falls due, counted as one full flush whether the writer notices the due horizon after the batch or on its timed wake, and a subscriber that asked for idle marks (an hour away) is sent a durable mark past the record, the signal aterm's notifier pages on (the_horizon_flushes_an_idle_broker_on_its_timer, behind a one-minute hang detector); at a one-hour horizon two publishes, the second's ack read, leave two barriers, no full flush and no timed wake, the writer being one thread, and flush_durable then adds exactly one (a_long_horizon_flushes_nothing_early, counted, not timed); Hello reports the tier served for every tier, and its horizon tells none (None) from zero (Some(0)), a sub-millisecond one reading 1 ms and a tier other than Ordered none; retention above the held head is refused; a failed full flush (the fault seam failing every full flush while the barrier succeeds) answers the waiting escalated op ERR_REFUSED, outcome unknown, keeps its record unseen, reports Strict in Hello, refuses the producer's resend instead of acking it on a later flush, and the next open moves the generation (on Ordered where it is served, and on Relaxed everywhere), after which that resend lands exactly once, deduped to the held record the same-boot reopen read back, another body under the same key deduped too and a fresh seq, never sent before, appended (the cut branch, a power loss having dropped the record, is broker.poisoned-refusal's crash simulation, and the boundary that claim names, a real device's failed flush that left the record's pages clean but unwritten, is no test's); a group commit the Group rule escalates steers no second reader of the group while it is held, which resumes below it, and does once its flush succeeds. asb serve --durability strict\|ordered\|relaxed [--horizon-ms N] serves the tier asked, as Hello reports it (--horizon-ms 0 as Some(0)), and refuses an unknown tier, a horizon for another tier or a non-numeric one (exit 2) before opening anything. Off Apple each Ordered socket test asserts the Strict serving instead. Not tested here: whether a device honours the barrier (Apple's fcntl(2) contract, against which broker.crash-sim.prefix-law checks the power-loss law), how late past its horizon a timed flush comes on a loaded host (the tests count full flushes and wait for delivery under one-minute hang detectors, never assert a latency budget), Ordered's cost, which bench claims gate on macOS rather than this test (broker.bench.ordered-floor and broker.bench.ordered-pipeline-floor as rates at aterm's 100 ms horizon, one producer and aterm's 64-deep pipeline, the HORIZON_MS and WINDOW aterm-link's astream_fixture_tests holds those commands to, and broker.bench.ordered-full-flush-ceiling and broker.bench.ordered-pipeline-full-flush-ceiling as full flushes per second of the run, which the horizon bounds however busy the volume is), and a descriptor shortage at a flush — none is taken for one any more (the flush outside the lock shares the log's own descriptor), which is what a shortage used to turn into a failed flush. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --lib -- ordered_tests escalation:: broker::tests::held_head proto::tests::the_hello_horizon && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --features retention --test suite -- ordered_tier:: cli_serve_durability::` |
| `broker.durable-mark` | Every Mark a broker sends carries the DURABLE head — every record below it is on stable media by a full flush — as an optional trailer after resume (Response::Mark.durable, Event::Mark.durable, Client::seen_durable, Subscription::seen_durable): a frozen copy of the decoder from the release before reads a Mark carrying it as the same (next, head, resume), this build reads an older broker's Mark as durable None, a trailer cut one to seven bytes short is malformed and bytes past its eight are skipped; no request tag, protocol version or error code is spent. Over a real socket a Fetch head query reports Broker::durable_head; on a broker that serves Ordered a subscriber that asked for idle marks gets a DURABLE mark covering a record it was delivered once flush_durable makes it durable, and on a Strict broker (idle marks an hour away) no mark comes between one delivery and the next. A log that latches from Ordered to Strict while serving (the test seam stands in a device that refuses the barrier) keeps sending them for the rest of its open: a tail that attached while it served Ordered is delivered the batch that latched, flushed in full, then a durable mark covering it, and the same for a batch after the latch, with idle marks an hour away. Apple only: elsewhere Ordered is served Strict from the open. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --lib -- proto::tests::a_marks_durable_trailer_is_additive_both_ways proto::tests::a_mark_truncated_after_head_is_malformed broker::tests::a_latched_log_keeps_marking_what_it_made_durable && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --test suite -- ordered_tier::a_durable_mark ordered_tier::an_escalated_publish_is_durable` |
| `os.cordon` | astream-sys is the durable log's OS cordon: zero dependencies, its crate root deny(unsafe_code), and only two modules re-permit unsafe. Its cfg(unix) `owner` module holds the owner-only file rule's calls: geteuid behind euid, on Apple fstatx_np, filesec_init, filesec_query_property, filesec_get_property, filesec_free, acl_get_entry, acl_get_tag_type and acl_free behind owner_private and owner_private_dir (the list decided by return codes and the filesec's validity bits, never by errno, which libmalloc's free inside filesec_free can leave EINVAL after acl_get_fd_np's ENOENT), on macOS arm64 lstatx_np, filesec_init, filesec_get_property, filesec_query_property and filesec_free behind owner_private_dir_by_path (no struct stat field read: the owner and mode come from the filesec, and the list bit is tested != 0, the bitmask fs_valid & FS_VALID_ACL being 32, Libc-1752.120.2 gen/filesec.c:325-328), and off Apple fsync behind plain_fsync (retried on EINTR), beside the per-OS O_NONBLOCK and O_NOFOLLOW numbers open_nonblocking and open_nonblocking_rw hand to safe std (Darwin and the BSDs; Linux and Android on x86, x86-64, RISC-V, s390x, LoongArch, ARM and PowerPC; any other target is Unsupported and opens nothing). Its cfg(target_vendor = "apple") module holds six hand-declared FFI calls with a SAFETY note each: fcntl(F_BARRIERFSYNC) behind barrier_sync (retried on EINTR; elsewhere a full sync_data, never weaker); fsync behind plain_fsync in every build (retried on EINTR; elsewhere on Unix plain_fsync is the owner module's fsync, the call sync_all makes there), on any descriptor (impl AsFd: a &File, an OwnedFd, a BorrowedFd), for a caller that asks for exactly a plain flush, and behind full_sync and full_sync_data only in a debug build's process whose environment carries TEST_PLAIN_FSYNC_ENV (ASTREAM_TEST_PLAIN_FSYNC=1), the mark a test binary sets for its own process tree, so a release build and every application's full_sync and full_sync_data flush in full (sync_all and sync_data); sysctlbyname("kern.bootsessionuuid") behind boot_session (Linux: /proc/sys/kernel/random/boot_id through safe std; elsewhere None); a zero-wait poll behind socket_hung_up, which astream-broker's client asks only after setting a socket's read or write timeout was refused with EINVAL (Subscription::set_read_timeout, Closer::set_read_timeout, Closer::set_write_timeout), to tell a socket whose peer already hung up (nothing left to bound, so no error) from a refusal it keeps; clock_gettime_nsec_np(CLOCK_UPTIME_RAW) behind clock_ns, the machine's monotonic clock (the one std's Instant reads on Apple) that flush_times' totals and slow-flush ring are on (elsewhere an Instant since the process first read it, comparable within the process only; claim os.flush-times); and signal(SIGHUP) behind install_sighup_handler, a handler that only counts signals (sighup_generation), installed only by the broker's revocation file (Broker::load_revocation_file, asb serve --revocation-file) so that a SIGHUP reloads it. astream-broker depends on it and stays forbid(unsafe_code). aterm depends on it directly: it reads the boot through boot_session and parse_boot_id in aterm-link, aterm-agent, aterm-acaffeine, aterm-gui, aterm-uds, aterm-release (its publisher fence's fence-boot, compared as 16 bytes) aterm-cli (its setup ledger) and aterm-update (which reads Linux's boot_id file itself and judges it with parse_boot_id), and writes the boot text it records with boot_id_text (aterm-agent's ledger and tapes, aterm-acaffeine's state file, aterm-release's fence, aterm-link's messages); outside tests only aterm-machines reads kern.bootsessionuuid otherwise, by a sysctl -n subprocess, to read what its enrollment root script reads; every flush aterm-durable makes is this crate's, so flush_tally counts each one: its durable writes end with full_sync or full_sync_data, its ordered writes, files and directories alike (aterm-durable's ordered and ordered_dir), with barrier_sync on Apple targets (a barrier barrier_refused reads as refused answered by full_sync) and full_sync elsewhere, and the plain flush its ordered_or_fsync falls back to where a volume refuses both is plain_fsync, as is every plain flush aterm names (aterm-durable's primitive::fsync: aterm-gui's crash journal folders and pinned folders, atpkg's stores and its build cache's handle-relative folders), and aterm-link's fabric report quotes the tally a broker's <log>.health carries; it reaches fcntl, fsync, sysctlbyname and poll (through astream-broker's Closer, whose read and write timeouts it sets on every dial), and never installs the SIGHUP handler; file_flush_refused reaches it through aterm-durable's refusal_accepted, by which aterm-gui's session log journal, aterm-update's manifest, aterm-update-core's sentinel and atpkg (its store, vendor_direct and local harness) finish a file's flush, each writer's own policy; on Unix aterm-uds requests close_on_fork on its sockets and drops the answer, its spawn's close-on-exec policy being the rule (close_on_fork_reports_the_kernel_inheritance_behavior, on Apple: the answer is whether the kernel kept the flag, and only a kept flag keeps a socket out of a child), and aterm-agent and atpkg ask file_matches_path whether a held file is still the one its path names (file_identity_survives_rename_and_distinguishes_replacement). Tested: the boot-id parser takes the UUID form in either case with or without its terminator, drops a hyphen wherever it falls, and refuses anything else, the reserved all-zero id included; boot_id_text writes an id in the kernel's 8-4-4-4-12 form in the case asked, and the parser reads it back as the same 16 bytes (on Linux, this boot's text in the kernel's case is the kernel's own); the live boot session is stable within a process and named on Apple and Linux; a barrier on a regular file succeeds, and so does one on a directory opened read-only (File::open), which is not read as refused (its negative control, /dev/null, is refused on Apple and read as refused); flush_tally counts plain_fsync as its own kind: two plain flushes of a regular file succeed and raise plain by exactly two, and two of a directory held as a bare descriptor (an OwnedFd, then a BorrowedFd of it) by exactly two more (no other test of the binary calls it, so a plain flush counted as full or barrier falls short), and tests/flush_tally.rs, a binary of one test in which nothing else flushes, reads every kind exactly: each full_sync, full_sync_data, barrier_sync and plain_fsync of a regular file, and a plain_fsync of a directory's OwnedFd, moves its own kind by exactly one and no other (a plain_fsync also counted full fails there, which the library binary's bound cannot catch), a barrier /dev/null refuses on Apple moves barrier by exactly one, and the negative control, the same file's File::sync_all and File::sync_data, moves nothing; the retry every cordon flush takes (barrier_sync's on Apple, plain_fsync's on every Unix) calls again on each EINTR (two, then success on the third call) and returns the first other answer as it comes (EIO after one EINTR, on the second call, never retried); the EINTR it is fed is the kernel's 4 on Unix, read as Interrupted, so a retry that matched another kind returns it (the negative control); barrier_refused reads Darwin's ENOTSUP, ENODEV, EINVAL and ENOTTY as the device's refusal to order (the Ordered tier then flushes in full and serves Strict) and nothing else — not EIO, not ENOSPC, and nothing off Apple, where the barrier is itself a full flush. dir_flush_unavailable is the one classification of an error from opening a directory or from a full flush of its handle: Unsupported, InvalidInput, PermissionDenied and NotFound by kind everywhere, and on Apple the same refusal errnos barrier_refused reads (no std names ENODEV or ENOTTY by a kind the rule reads, and a stock std reads Darwin's ENOTSUP, 45, as uncategorized and only its EOPNOTSUPP, 102, as Unsupported, where the Trust std reads both as Unsupported), mean the directory's entries cannot be flushed here, and EIO, ENOSPC or a generic Other error never do; on Apple a full flush of /dev/null is refused with an errno whose kind alone is none of the four, and reads as unavailable (that kind check is the negative control). file_flush_refused is its refusal half, for a flush of a file's own handle: Unsupported and InvalidInput by kind everywhere and on Apple the same refusal errnos, and never PermissionDenied or NotFound (dir_flush_unavailable adds those two, what only a directory's open answers), EIO, ENOSPC or a generic Other error; the same /dev/null refusal reads as refused. Numeric POSIX errno controls are Unix-scoped and Darwin-only numbers Apple-scoped; the ErrorKind controls have no OS gate and also reject StorageFull. The owner rule, over real files (owner_tests): a file of ours at 0600, 0400 or 0700 passes with the descriptor's metadata, and 0640, 0604, 0644 and 0660 are refused as Mode naming the bits; a directory is not a file to owner_private and a file is not a directory to owner_private_dir, a 0700 directory passes and a 0755 one is Mode; `/` is refused as root's (Owner(0)) unless the reader is root, then for its mode; a FIFO opens at once under open_nonblocking and under open_nonblocking_rw and is refused as one, behind a one-minute hang detector; open_nonblocking_rw, which writes nothing, refuses a file of ours at 0400 that open_nonblocking opens (PermissionDenied; root writes past a mode) and opens it at 0600 with its bytes unchanged; Links::Refuse refuses a symlink that Links::Follow opens; and on macOS an allow entry on a 0600 file or a 0700 directory is Acl, `chmod -N` clears it, and a deny entry passes; with errno left EINVAL as each filesec_free returns (a test seam standing for libmalloc's free), a file and a directory with no list and a file with a deny entry still pass and an allow entry is still Acl (the earlier acl_get_fd_np-then-errno read fails it with os error 22, aterm's ATERM-KI-1111-30), and every list reading maps to one verdict without errno (each_list_reading_has_one_verdict: statx1's 0 with no mode set is unread, never no list). The ACL is judged before the mode, so a Mode answer means every other clause held (aterm-uds narrows such a directory's mode): an allow entry on a 0644 file or a 0755 directory, open by their mode bits as well, is Acl, never Mode, and the same file and directory are Mode once `chmod -N` clears it (moving the mode clause ahead of the ACL clause fails this test). The clauses are written once, in the private owner_rule module (kind_and_owner, the only maker of an Owned, whose field is private to the module, then Owned::list_and_mode), and every entry judges through them: the descriptor's owner_private and owner_private_dir (one fstat, the access list read only once kind and owner held), owner_private_dir_by_path (macOS arm64: one lstatx_np of the path, never an open; elsewhere Undecided(Unsupported) with no call), and kind_and_owner_of, the kind and owner clauses over a caller's own metadata. The path entry decides only from the lookup's ENOENT (Missing) and from a complete stat of an object with no access list: a link (Symlink), a kind or owner refusal, and this user's directory with its owner read and search bits set (Ok or Mode; the search bit too because devfs's /dev/fd/N node of a held directory, whose open dups the held descriptor, shows every search bit clear); everything else is Undecided (Lookup, Unread, Listed, NoOwnerRead) and goes to the open. Laws (owner_tests): A1 the_owner_clauses_are_one_order_for_both_entries, a pure table of st_mode, uid, dir and allows rows (the owner judged before the kind answers Owner(0) for a root FIFO; the mode before the list answers Mode(0o755) for a listed 0755 directory; & 0o007 passes a 0750 one); A2 a_kind_word_agrees_with_std, the kind words of a file, a directory, a FIFO, a socket, /dev/null and a symlink equal to std's FileType predicates (S_IFSOCK given the symlink's number names the socket `not a regular file` and the symlink `a socket`); A3 the_path_judgement_is_the_opened_one, 27 fixtures (missing paths, a file and a path through it, live and dangling links, a FIFO, a socket, /dev/null, own directories at 0700, 0500, 0755, 0750, 0711, 0300 and 0000, 0700 and 0755 directories with allow, deny and a 20-entry deny list of distinct principals that passes statx1's first 16-entry buffer (a CONTROL counts at least 17 entries), a directory under a 0000 one, `/`, two trailing-slash paths, and the /dev/fd/N nodes of a held 0733 directory and of a held 0700 one carrying `everyone allow list`, NoOwnerRead where their opens, dups of the held descriptors, say Mode(0o733) and Acl) each held to its absolute answer and to its opened verdict (open_nonblocking with Links::Refuse, then owner_private_dir), every decided answer equal to it, with root's answers branched (dropping the owner-read test, dropping the owner-search test, reading the list bit as == 1, answering Missing for any failed lookup, reading FILESEC_GROUP for the owner, dropping the link test and judging the owner before the kind each fail it); A4 the_libc_outcomes_map_as_libc_does, a pure table of every lstatx_np outcome (Libc sys/statx_np.c:141-220) through the decision (rc == -1 in place of rc != 0 decides statx1's malloc failure; errno tested before rc reads a stale ENOENT as Missing); A5 lstatx_np_writes_within_its_buffer, bytes 144-255 of the 256-byte buffer unwritten by a stat, word 17 (the zeroed st_qspare[1]) written as the CONTROL (the buffer handed over 14 words in fails it), and a compile-time assertion refuses a buffer of 144 bytes or fewer (an 18-word one does not compile); A6 kind_and_owner_of_holds_metadata_to_the_descriptors_clauses, its answer over real objects equal to the Kind and Owner refusals of the opened descriptor's verdict (reading the gid for the owner fails it). The test-marked fsync is not a test of this command (a test that set the mark would change every flush of its own process). The other two Apple calls are tested where they are used, not by this command: socket_hung_up in astream-broker's client (client::tests::a_close_mid_frame_is_classified_like_read_frame, on Apple: a live and a half-closed socket keep the EINVAL, a socket hung up both ways does not, and nothing but that EINVAL polls), and the SIGHUP reload under cap.expiry-enforced (cap_hardening::sighup_reloads_revocation_file_and_revokes_open_principal_subscriptions_and_wills), since a raised SIGHUP reaches the whole test process. What each caller does with dir_flush_unavailable is likewise tested where it is used, not by this command: astream-broker's sync_parent_dir goes on without the entry's flush exactly where it says so and returns EIO (store::tests::the_directory_flush_tolerates_enotsup_and_returns_eio), and asb's --seq-file publishes on past it and refuses on EIO (asb's tests::the_seq_files_directory_flush_tolerates_enotsup_and_refuses_on_eio), each fed Darwin's ENOTSUP and ENODEV, the second of which on Apple fails both tests under the old list of kinds on every std (each test's control checks that ENODEV's kind alone is not unavailable); and in aterm, aterm-durable's Flush::dir_best_effort (a_best_effort_dir_flush_reports_eio_and_reads_enotsup_as_unavailable) and aterm-link's tolerated state-dir flushes (a_tolerated_flush_goes_on_past_enotsup_and_returns_eio) go on exactly where it says so and return any other failure. astream-engine's segment open takes no tolerance, by its code (File::open then sync_all, each error returned), which no test feeds a refusal. Whether a device honours the barrier is Apple's fcntl(2) contract and is not tested here. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-sys` |
| `os.flush-times` | astream-sys times every flush it makes, so a caller can line a stall up with the flushes that ran under it: full_sync, full_sync_data, barrier_sync and plain_fsync each go through one door that counts the call in its kind as it begins (flush_tally, exact at entry), reads clock_ns, flushes, reads clock_ns again, adds the span to its kind's total and, for a span slower than SLOW_FLUSH_NS (1 ms), keeps its index, kind, start and end in a lock-free ring of the last SLOW_FLUSHES_KEPT (64), read with flush_times (FlushTimes: the clock, full_ns, barrier_ns, plain_ns, slow_count, the ring's flushes oldest first, and running). Each slow flush takes the next index (from 0) as it returns, so a reader can tell exactly which slow flushes a ring lost (aterm E09c): the window between two readings is the indices from the earlier one's slow_count to the later one's, and FlushTimes::missed (or slow_flushes_missed, for a reader's own copy of a ring) is how many of them the later ring does not hold, 0 exactly when it holds the window whole. clock_ns is the machine's CLOCK_UPTIME_RAW on Apple (the cordon's clock_gettime_nsec_np, the clock std's Instant reads there), so two processes' readings on one boot compare (FlushClock::Machine); elsewhere an Instant since the process first read it, comparable within the process only (FlushClock::Process); FLUSH_CLOCK names this build's. The ring's writers take an index by fetch_add and claim its slot by a compare-and-swap of the slot's tag to the index's odd value, publish the even value once the fields are written, and leave a slot that is being written or holds a later index; a reader takes a slot whose tag reads even and the same before and after its fields, the index its tag names with it. A writer takes its index after it reads its flush's end (release) and a reading loads the count after the slots (acquire), so every kept index is below its reading's count, and every flush below a reading's count ended no later than a clock_ns read after that reading returns; a flush still running at a reading, or between its end and its index, is a later reading's, and the reading counts it in running instead: the calls through the door begun and not yet returned (a call returns, with release ordering, once its span is noted, and a reading loads the returned calls, acquire, before the begun ones and both before the count), so a reading whose running is 0 accounts for every slow flush that began before it, and a window of it held whole holds each one that ran there. These orders are argued from the memory model: no test here tells them from relaxed ones, which a deterministic test cannot provoke. Tested: tests/flush_tally.rs, a binary of one test in which nothing else flushes, calls each of the four flushes on a regular file (and on Apple a barrier /dev/null refuses) and reads around each: its own kind's total moves by more than zero and by no more than the span the test read around the call, every other kind's stays where it was, and the ring gains exactly that flush, with its kind, the next index (the reading before's slow_count, the window from it whole), inside the test's span and its length the total's move, exactly when the total moved past 1 ms, and nothing otherwise, and the readings around each call count no call running; the negative control, the same file's File::sync_all and File::sync_data, moves no total and no ring. A flush that skips the door's timing fails that test (run by hand when this landed: plain_fsync counted but untimed fails `plain_fsync: a flush that skipped its timing`). On a ledger of its own: exactly 1 ms joins its total and not the ring, 1 ms and 1 ns joins both, a failed flush is timed and answers its own error; 100 slow flushes leave the last 64, the 37th to the 100th (indices 36 to 99), oldest first, with all 100 counted, a window from index 36 whole and one from 35 missing exactly one; a slot whose tag is odd is read as no flush (the control: the even tag back, it reads), a writer leaves a slot holding a later index and one being written; over 300 seeded interleavings of up to five writers' three steps (take an index, claim its slot, publish it), one of them lagging while the others wrap the ring, and readings between any two steps, every held flush is the one its index names, below its reading's count, and missed from every earlier reading's count is exactly the window's indices the later ring does not hold, so a window called whole holds every slow flush of it (negative control: the same readings with their indices dropped, the window taken as the ring's newest flushes of its count, claim windows whole that are not, and the interleavings lose writes; by hand, a full ring with a 65th write raced reads 64 flushes for a window of one, its newest from before the window, where missed names the one); eight threads writing 500 slow flushes each while a reader reads leave every flush counted, no read a mix of two (each flush's length is encoded in its start) and every held flush the one its index names, below its reading's count; four threads that read their ends off the clock never leave a held flush ending after the clock read that follows its reading (which the slot's sequence lock gives, whatever orders the count has); a reading taken inside a door call counts it running (one inside a call inside it, two) with none of its time, and none once it has returned, failed or not, and a note alone never runs; four threads held inside the door at a barrier read four running and none once they return (the control: none before any began). The clock never goes back within a process. Whether a slow flush's start and end compare with another process's is CLOCK_UPTIME_RAW's contract on Apple, not tested here; astream-broker's <log>.health carries the totals and the ring (broker.health-sidecar). | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-sys --test flush_tally && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-sys --lib -- flush_times::` |
| `evidence.selftest.hash-pinned` | A deterministic command's output hash is pinned, demonstrating output-drift detection. | `targo --unverified run --quiet --locked -p astream-evidence -- selftest` |
| `wire.bench.frame-roundtrip-floor` | Frame encode/decode holds a throughput floor (a regression gate, not a cross-system comparison; those land with the broker on disclosed hardware). | `targo --unverified run --quiet --release --locked -p astream-wire --example microbench` |
| `wire.bench.match-floor` | The wildcard matcher holds a throughput floor on a deep pattern, guarding against an accidental complexity regression. | `targo --unverified run --quiet --release --locked -p astream-wire --example microbench` |
| `wire.bench.crc32-floor` | crc32_ieee, run on every frame encode and decode, must stay several times faster than the bitwise CRC it replaces, gated as a ratio: the example times both forms over the same 64 KiB buffer, asserts they agree, and prints crc32_speedup_vs_bitwise (the bitwise reference lives only in the example). A regression to the bitwise form drives the ratio to ~1.0 and fails. The two forms are timed in separate consecutive windows, not interleaved, so contention in one window can move the ratio either way; nothing asserts its stability under load. crc32_mib_per_sec is printed but not gated. | `targo --unverified run --quiet --release --locked -p astream-wire --example microbench` |
| `engine.bench.append-floor` | The single-writer append hot path (Record -> versioned envelope -> CRC frame -> in-memory disk through the effect seam) holds a throughput floor. A regression gate on the engine's write path, not a cross-system comparison; cross-system numbers land with the broker on disclosed hardware. | `targo --unverified run --quiet --release --locked -p astream-engine --example engine_microbench` |
| `engine.bench.replay-floor` | The read-by-offset replay hot path (Frame::decode + Envelope::from_payload over stored bytes) holds a throughput floor, guarding the path every replay/fork/resume depends on against a catastrophic slowdown. | `targo --unverified run --quiet --release --locked -p astream-engine --example engine_microbench` |
| `term.bench.fold-floor` | The pure VT screen fold holds a throughput floor over a representative 80x24 cursor/SGR/text stream. The rate is whole-machine and contention can only depress it, so the floor is set roughly 12x below the quiet-machine rate: it catches only a catastrophic, order-of-magnitude fold slowdown, not a 2x regression. No same-run ratio is gated, and nothing here measures load or asserts stability. | `targo --unverified run --quiet --release --locked -p astream-term --example term_microbench` |
| `term.predict.watermark-squash` | SpeculativeTerminal holds a >= 0.80 prediction hit-rate floor (`METRIC predict_hit_rate`) while preserving exact final-screen convergence (`reconciled.eq_full(authoritative)`) across every step of a deterministic 5-phase interactive terminal corpus (cooked shell commands with Backspace/Ctrl-W/Left-Right line editing, repeated deterministic status/TUI inspection commands learned in 0 RTT via ProgramPredictor, truecolour status output, password/passphrase silent-prompt suppression, and divergent dynamic output forcing rollback + rule invalidation). | `targo --unverified run --quiet --release --locked -p astream-term --example watermark_squash_bench` |
| `broker.ack-key-is-one-wire-contract` | `client::ack` and `asb ack` derive an ack's producer sequence as `ACK_SEQ_BASE \| offset` from one exported constant, so broker dedup recognises a retry that crosses faces: an ack sent through the library, then the CLI's derivation of the same ack sent as the `process_and_produce` frame `asb` sends, is deduped to the original offset and appends nothing. A second test arranges the collision the reservation prevents -- producer 9 publishes sequences 1..=6, then acks input offset 5 -- and asserts the ack is not deduped. The CLI frame is built in-process: the asb binary is not spawned, the will-fence separation is asserted by broker.will-fires-exactly-once, and a caller picking sequences by hand must still keep ordinary publishes below 2^63. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --test suite -- cross_face_ack::` |
| `evidence.benchmark-first-attempt` | A benchmark claim executes once and keeps that attempt's exit status, metric, stdout hash and failure tails. A real counter-file fixture would produce an in-band metric on a second execution: a first floor miss, ceiling miss or nonzero exit remains failed, and the counter stays one. A genuine first result inside both bounds passes. The native fixture uses a Unix shell; this claim is qualified on macOS. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-evidence --lib benchmark_claim_keeps_its_first_attempt_and_failure_evidence` (re-run on macos only; skipped elsewhere) |
| `broker.monotone-broker-time` | Every appended record is stamped at commit with a monotone per-broker Unix-millisecond timestamp (`bt=`, REQUIREMENTS R3) via Clean-origin `broker_bt_advance` (`max(prev_bt, wall_ms)`), stored inside BREC v2's additive `0xB6` trailer without changing `BREC_VERSION 2` or touching the sender's `t=` in the payload body. Negotiated additively on the wire via `Request::BrokerTimes` (tag 0x12, announced in NonceExt tag 0x03 bit 3 `REQUEST_BROKER_TIMES`): connections that opt in (`Client::fetch_stamped`, `fetch_told_stamped`, `last_stamped`, `last_page_stamped`, `last_all_stamped`, `subscribe_stamped`, `subscribe_group_stamped`, `asb fetch\|last\|sub --bt`) read each record's `bt=` across `Fetch`, `Last`, `Subscribe`, and `Will`-fired goodbyes, while un-negotiated clients receive byte-identical legacy `Delivery` frames. A backward wall-clock step clamps monotonically; `Broker::open_replica`, live `Replicated` follower shipping, `<log>.held`, and `<log>.state` compaction preserve the leader's exact stored `bt=` values across replication and restart without recomputing. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --all-features --test suite -- broker_time_and_stats::r3_` |
| `broker.wire-stats` | Operators query live operational broker metrics over the wire via `Request::Stats { filter }` (tag 0x13, reply `Response::Stats(WireStats)` tag 0x88, announced in NonceExt tag 0x03 bit 4 `REQUEST_STATS`; REQUIREMENTS R5; `Client::stats` and `asb stats <endpoint> <filter> <transport> <cap>`). Answers `started_at_ms`, active `listeners`, scoped live attached `producers` (principal, transport, `producer_id`, `attached_at_ms`, and `last_bt_ms`), visible `faces` (`records` and `bytes` per `/f/<F>/<face>` prefix, never bodies), `oldest`/`head`, `pending_wills`, `dedup_entries`, `disk_segments`, and `disk_bytes`. On a guarded broker `Stats` requires a live read capability covering `/f/<F>/>` (or root `/>`): unattached connections, narrow sub-fleet grants (`ro:/f/alpha/in/n1/>`), and cross-fleet requests are refused `ERR_UNAUTHORIZED`, and sibling-fleet `Stats` never leaks another fleet's attached producers or faces. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --all-features --test suite -- broker_time_and_stats::r5_` |
| `broker.rate-limit` | Monotone per-principal / per-producer token-bucket rate limiting (`RateLimit`, `OpenOptions::with_rate_limit`, `Broker::set_rate_limit`, `Broker::rate_limit`, `BrokerStats::rate_limited`, `asb serve --rate-limit SPEC`; Clean-origin `TlaRateBudget`: `rate_tokens_clamp`, `rate_clock_advance`, `rate_tokens_sufficient_isl`) bounds ingest record rate and/or byte rate per `producer_id` across at most `MAX_RATE_BUCKETS = 4096` tracked buckets (evicting the stalest bucket on overflow). On a guarded broker (`cap`) each principal's SHA-256-derived `producer_id` gets an isolated bucket, so a flooding principal cannot starve peers; refusals answer `ERR_RATE_LIMITED` (code 12, `rate limited: retry-after-ms=<N>`, `BrokerRefusal::is_rate_limited`, `BrokerRefusal::retry_after_ms`) without staging or poisoning the log; consumer-group commits (`Commit`), subject retirements (`Publish` on `/a/retire` and conditional `Retire`), and all read verbs (`Fetch`, `Last`, `Subscribe`) are never throttled; and disabling or updating the rate limit live takes effect immediately. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --all-features --test suite -- rate_limit::` |
| `broker.relay` | Origin-keyed relay: on a three-broker cycle and a ring with chords, over socketpairs the brokers adopted (`Broker::serve_stream`, `FederationWire::Stream`), each broker holds each relayed record exactly once, where the same cycle of mirroring bridges (the negative control) keeps growing. A forged signature is refused for good and a revoked or unknown origin is answered code 15, not judged (`Broker::set_origin_keys`); neither takes a key from the real record, and a bridge given `hold_while` holds the latter until its origin is taken. A `relay` grant carries only its owner's records. Under `Keep::Last` the highest origin sequence wins in either arrival order, live, after a compaction and after a reopen. A retention cut raises a floor carried in `<log>.state`, so a replay after the key was cut appends nothing, live or after a reopen, a sequence in the ack half is refused, and a cut of one subject refuses nothing late on another while the origin holds fewer than 4,096 floors. `Astream.Broker.TlaOriginRelay` (Clean-origin `relay_floor_raise`, `relay_floor_admits`) proves, on the `Relay` verb, at most one append of a key on its subject through arrivals, cuts and replays. This command runs the tests; the island is kernel-checked by every Trust compile of the crate. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --all-features --lib relay_floor:: && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --all-features --test suite -- federation_relay:: stream_link:: federation_push::` |
| `broker.federation` | Cross-broker subtree mirroring (`FederationBridge`, `FederationConfig`, `FederationEndpoint`, `FederationRule`, `parse_federation_rules`, `FederationRunner`, `SubjectRewrite`, `FederationLink`, `FederationReport`), dynamic SWIM-incarnation peer discovery and EWMA-hysteresis multi-region routing (`PeerAdvertisement`, `FederationDirectory`, `FederationMesh`, `FederationMeshConfig`; `federation_route_preferred`, `federation_incarnation_fresh`), the `asb federate` CLI (`--rules-file`, `--once`, `--interval-ms`, `--cut-file`, `--dst-cap-file`), and Chandy-Lamport consistent cuts across independent broker logs (`FederatedCut`; Clean-origin `TlaFederationBridge`: `federation_cursor_advance`, `federation_seq_clamp`, `federation_hop_allowed_isl`): `FederationBridge`, multi-rule `FederationRunner`, and multi-region `FederationMesh` drain source broker filters from durable consumer-group cursors (`Client::fetch_told`), discover regional gateways dynamically over retained seed registries, route each remote region through its lowest-RTT healthy gateway with hysteresis-bounded failover (`Draining` / `Suspect` / `Dead`) and automatic split-horizon echo exclusion, pipeline batches to the destination broker with deterministic `(producer_id, src_offset)` deduplication, verify `LogIdentity` continuity across sync ticks and reconnects, and work across Unix, TCP, Sealed, Handshake, and mutual Ed25519 Identity + capability-guarded endpoints; `asb federate` executes inline or declarative `--rules-file` topologies while persisting healed `FederatedCut` files atomically; `FederatedCut` verifies, heals, joins, meets, and canonically encodes/decodes `CausalCut<LogIdentity>` frontiers across cross-broker causal edges. A push bridge may follow one group over several lanes (`FederationConfig::carry`): it carries each record of any of them once, by poll and by push and across a restart, where two bridges on one group, one lane each (the negative control), strand a record the first commits past; and a destination told that one producer continues another's sequence (`Broker::set_producer_lineage`) answers a mirror whose source's key rotated, resending a burst its old producer landed and never committed, from the old producer's records, where with no lineage (the negative control) that burst lands twice. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --all-features --lib federation:: && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --all-features --test suite -- federation:: federation_push::` |
