| `broker.replicated-failover-matrix` | Multi-node replicated-tier failover and autonomous background lease-heartbeat supervision (`astream_broker::failover`, `FailoverCluster`, `FailoverDaemon`, `FailoverLeaseConfig`, `Astream.Broker.TlaLeaderFailover`) enforce strict majority-quorum candidate election (`elect_highest_candidate`, `failover_quorum_clamp`), monotonic leader-lease renewal + guard-band expiry gating (`failover_lease_expired`), crash-safe replica-to-leader promotion (`BrokerLog::promote_replica`, `promote_replica_log`, `failover_epoch_advance`) that records a `Lineage` step `(next_generation, head_offset)` in `<log>.id` before removing `<log>.replica` so client cursors `< head_offset` continue seamlessly via `LogIdentity::continues`, permanent follower fencing (`failover_fences_stale`, `replica fenced`) of deposed stale-generation zombie leaders, and automatic divergent-tail truncation + follower rejoin (`repair_follower_for_leader`, `Broker::rejoin_followers`) across cascading multi-epoch failovers with zero split-brain records. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --lib failover:: && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --test replicated -- three_node_failover_matrix && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --test replicated -- autonomous_background_failover_daemon` |
| `wire.wake-and-control-acks` | Zero-allocation fabric wake priority algebra, sender-demotion rules, and per-recipient sliding-window wake coalescing (`WakeClass`, `WakeBudget`, `SessionWakeLedger`, `Astream.Wire.TlaWakeBudget`: `wake_rank_max`, `wake_charge_clamp`, `wake_within_budget`) plus typed `v=1` control-face decoders (`V1NodeAck` / `NodeAckState` for `/f/<F>/pub/<node>/node/ack` §5.3 and `V1BarrierAck` / `BarrierAckState` for `/f/<F>/pub/<owner>/ack/<B>` §5.4): enforces the nine-kind priority lattice (`control > ask > task > answer > report > ack > expired > undeliverable > note`), demotes relayed (`via=`), stray-reply, and unlisted `task`/`control` deliveries to silent `note`, emits at most one coalesced wake per pending batch, enforces `<n>/<unit>` sliding-window budgets with `retry_after_ms` throttling while exempting accepted `control` directives, and rejects pre-published (`record_offset < B`) or mismatched-`re=` barrier answers. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-wire --lib wake:: && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-wire --lib receipt::tests::v1_node_ack` |
| `term.echo.overlap-attribution` | Fine-grained per-byte PTY write->echo causal attribution across overlapping in-flight inputs (`InFlightEchoQueue`, `ExpectedEcho`, `EchoAttribution`, `Astream.Term.TlaOverlapEcho`: `echo_queue_len_clamp`, `echo_matched_advance`, `echo_queue_within_cap`) models POSIX `termios` (`ECHO \| ECHOE \| ECHOCTL \| ONLCR`) byte expansions (`^X` caret control echo, `\r\n` newline translation, `\x08 \x08` rubout, escape suppression), attributes arbitrary split or coalesced PTY `Out` chunks back to the exact sequence of `(in_offset, client_seq)` writes they echo, advances `last_retired_in_offset` monotonically, and bounds in-flight memory by `MAX_IN_FLIGHT_ECHO_BYTES`. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-term --lib overlap_echo::` |
| `evidence.claim-timeout` | Per-claim wall-clock timeout enforcement in `astream-evidence` (`Claim::timeout_secs`, `effective_claim_timeout_ms`, `run_command_with_timeout`, `Astream.Evidence.TlaClaimTimeout`: `claim_elapsed_clamp`, `claim_timeout_floor`, `claim_timed_out`) bounds every claim execution by a strictly positive millisecond deadline (`DEFAULT_CLAIM_TIMEOUT_SECS = 900`, floored at `MIN_CLAIM_TIMEOUT_MS = 1`), captures `stdout`/`stderr` via unlinked-on-drop temporary files so neither multi-megabyte output nor leaked grandchild file descriptors deadlock the runner, and kills hung child processes fail-closed (`command timed out after <N> ms`). | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-evidence --lib timeout && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-evidence --lib clean_origin_claim_timeout` |
| `cap.ring-ceiling` | Least-privilege capability-ring ceiling verification in `astream-cap` (`GrantCeiling`, `CeilingViolation`, `CeilingViolationKind`, `Astream.Cap.TlaRingCeiling`: `ceiling_rule_index_clamp`, `ceiling_mode_subsumes_code`, `ceiling_rule_within_cap`) enforces monotonic subsumption (`can_attenuate`) across mode, principal binding, expiry, and subject filter for every capability in a ring (`DESIGN-aterm-fabric` §8.2 & §14 Open Question 5): `GrantCeiling::aterm_node_ring` (8 node grants, none under the cut `term/` drive face; the last two read every node's halt subject and write the node's own) and `GrantCeiling::aterm_person_ring` (6 human grants with halt authority vs 5 agent grants without halt authority) render their grants in mint order through `GrantCeiling::rules`, the ring aterm mints; a ring minted from those rules is accepted, as are offline-attenuated Macaroon rings, while the drive face's `term/` grants, unbound fleet-root or pub god caps (`UnboundGodCap`), cross-node principal caps, mode escalations, forged HMAC tags, and expired grants are rejected. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-cap --lib ceiling:: && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-cap --lib twins::` |
| `broker.shared-refill` | Multi-lane coalesced refill planning and B09 cost-bounded handoff metering in `astream-broker` (`SharedRefillPlan`, `SharedRefillMeter`, `RefillStep`, `Astream.Broker.TlaSharedRefill`: `refill_join_cursor_max`, `refill_debt_clamp`, `refill_cursor_at_least_oldest`) schedules per-lane `[from .. inbox_oldest)` catch-up reads before joining a shared subtree `Fetch` at `max(from, inbox_oldest)` (`>= inbox_oldest`, refuting `RefillJoinBelowTheOldest`), charges `carried * REFILL_ROW_COST` against `(reading - 1) * scanned` saved index visits, and hands remaining lanes back to narrow per-lane filters (`RefillStep::HandOn`) within `DEFAULT_REFILL_SPLIT_SLACK / REFILL_ROW_COST` plus one page with zero missed or duplicate records. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --lib refill:: && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --test sticky_and_refill -- shared_refill_executes_three_phase` |
| `broker.sticky-batch-refusal` | Opt-in sticky producer sequence gap fencing on refused batches in `astream-broker` (`OpenOptions::sticky_batch_refusal`, fixed for the broker's lifetime, `StickyFenceTable`, `parse_gap_fenced_seq`, `Astream.Broker.TlaStickyBatchFence`: `sticky_fence_min_seq`, `sticky_fence_seq_allowed`, `sticky_fence_admits_code`, `sticky_fence_table_within_cap`) latches a per-`(conn_id, producer_id)` fence at `min(fence_seq, failed_seq)` whenever a `Publish` or `ProcessAndProduce` fails pre-writer throttling, staging, batch fsync commit, quorum replication, or deferred full flush (`DESIGN-aterm-fabric` §14 Open Question 7 & §15), refuses pipelined successors `req_seq > fence_seq` in send order (`gap-fenced=<fence_seq>: ...`) so zero out-of-order writes commit, clears automatically when an idempotent retry `<= fence_seq` commits or the connection closes, says so in every `Hello` on a fenced broker (the request set's `REQUEST_GAP_FENCES`, bit 5: `NonceExt::supports_gap_fences`, `Client::supports_gap_fences`), fences only a connection that asks (`Request::FenceGaps`, `0x15`, `Client::fence_gaps`, refused by an unfenced broker), so one that never asks, a client that predates the ask included, is served unfenced and a refusal it does not retry next fences nothing after it, and lifts a connection's fences on `Request::ClearGapFences` (`0x14`, `Client::clear_gap_fences`), answered once every write sent before it is answered, so a producer that has read its answers goes on without a reconnect, and leaves default `sticky_batch_refusal == false` behavior untouched (no bit; a write pipelined behind a refused batch lands ahead of its retry). | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --lib sticky_fence:: && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-broker --test sticky_and_refill -- sticky_batch_refusal_` |
| `agent.foreign-tape-splice` | CRC-framed offset-addressed standalone `EffectsLog` wire persistence (`EffectEnvelope`, `encode_effect_tape`, `decode_effect_tape`, `EFFECT_TAPE_VERSION = 1`) and causal `ToolResult`-anchored foreign-process tape splicing (`ToolEffectSpan`, `splice_foreign_tapes`, `replay_session_with_foreign_tapes`, `Astream.Agent.TlaForeignTapeSplice`: `foreign_tape_cursor_advance`, `foreign_tape_clamp_records`, `foreign_tape_exhausted`) splice recorded `EffectRecord` runs (`astream-host::foreign` or cooperative `RecordingSeam`) onto the unified `Event` offset axis immediately before each matching `CogRecord::ToolResult`, preserve exact replay convergence, detect counterfactual fork divergence (`fork_session`), and fail closed on non-monotonic sequence offsets, out-of-order spans, missing tool results, or unconsumed records. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-agent --lib foreign_tape:: && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-agent --lib twins::` |
| `kafka.sasl-auth` | Kafka SASL authentication (`SaslHandshake` key 17 v0..=v1, `SaslAuthenticate` key 36 v0..=v2 including KIP-482 flexible v2, `SaslConfig`, `SaslSession`, `ScramSha256Client`, `Astream.Kafka.TlaSaslAuth`) and encrypted, CA-certified, and zero-gap UDP-roaming wire listeners (`KafkaEncryptedListener::{Sealed, Handshake, Identity, Certified}`, `KafkaServer::bind_encrypted*` / `bind_durable_encrypted*` / `bind_roaming*` / `bind_durable_roaming*`, `KafkaClient::connect_{sealed,handshake,identity,certified,roaming}`, `KafkaSessionSecurity` levels `0..=4`, `ListenerSecurityPolicy`, `Astream.Kafka.TlaSealedListener`): default `astream-kafka` enforces RFC 4616 `PLAIN` and mutual RFC 5802 / RFC 7677 `SCRAM-SHA-256` over `astream-digest` with zero third-party dependencies, while optional `aead` / `handshake` / `identity` features upgrade TCP and migrating `AROM` v1 UDP listeners over `astream-aead` (`SealedStream` PSK, forward-secret ephemeral X25519 + PSK, zero-gap Connection-ID UDP migration, mutual Ed25519 signed-DH, and CA-certified `ACRT` SIGMA with `HostCertRotator` + `SSH_AUTH_SOCK` signing), rejecting plaintext, wrong-key, unpinned-host, untrusted-CA, unauthorized-client, and pre-SASL data-plane frames fail-closed. | `targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-kafka --all-features --lib sasl:: && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-kafka --all-features --lib kafka_tls:: && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-kafka --all-features --lib live_tcp_kafka_server_enforces_sasl && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-kafka --all-features --lib kafka_encrypted_listeners_enforce_sealed_handshake_identity_and_sasl_e2e && targo --unverified test --locked -p astream-kafka --all-features --lib kafka_certified_ssh_agent_and_roaming_udp_listeners_e2e` |

## Not yet built

- Segmented out-of-core log storage (`BrokerLog` holds active uncompacted
  `Arc<BrokerRecord>` entries in RAM between compactions rather than paging cold
  segments from disk)
- Replication of the held store (`<log>.held`): a follower holds none
- Identity bound to an external authority (SSH agent, OIDC), with key
  distribution and rotation
- Automatic leader election and failover for the replicated tier (epoch fencing
  and watermark tracking are built; leader assignment is static)
- The Kafka Produce/Fetch data path

### Owned last-value views (direct library callers)

`BrokerLog::last_matching` now reads a `LastView`, not an arbitrary historical
`visible: u64`. The owner reports its actual published head with
`publish_last_visible`, separately from a query; the broker does so at its existing
held-head or quorum publication point. Drop the returned `LastViewGarbage` after
releasing an external log mutex. A standalone owner must report its own visibility
policy explicitly, not infer quorum confirmation from `log.head()`.

Capture with `last_view_at` under the same lock as the head, retain that view through
all internal rounds, and distinguish `LastStep::Pending` from a completed empty page.
Retirement-event visits and subject-index visits share the step work budget. The
broker retains its request-round limit and returns an error when it cannot complete;
it does not turn exhausted work into a complete answer. Wire `Last`/`Mark` and the
client's per-page/tail splice contract do not change.

This is a direct-library source migration in the private development tree. A new
capture below the available floor returns `TooOld`; a view from another open returns
`WrongOpen`. Existing views continue to observe retirement links filled after their
capture, including promoted records still held behind visibility, and survive the
store's publication and compaction within that omission-and-tail contract. Drop the
view outside an external log mutex. Pre-open retirement history is not reconstructed:
a recovered log starts its available floor at its recovered head, so a lower quorum
head is refused until it catches up rather than treated as a complete historical face.
No disk/wire migration, public release, or version action is implied.

The store releases its old history ownership as published visibility advances. A live
view, its overlay, returned record handles, an unpublished suffix, or a standalone
owner that never reports publication can still retain memory. Partial-prefix cleanup
is bounded per publication; a fully visible prefix detaches in constant work and its
iterative destruction runs outside the broker log lock. No global memory, latency,
allocator reclamation, power-loss, or performance theorem is claimed.
